  - **Two counts are gated, because the stronger claim has the weaker coverage.** `named` is a guard anchored on the page — a test the machine does not make, in C that compiles — and it sees **8 of 12** under the control, missing the named witness. `admitted` is the predecessor `flagPredecessor` chose, which is *necessary* for either route to a condition here (`branchFor`'s `IRBranch` and `extractCondition`'s machine-text fallback both take their stream from `flagScanStream(block, flagPredecessor(…))`), so it is address-exact, needs no anchoring, and sees **12 of 12**.
  - **`admitted` is a differential test, not an oracle outside the question** — the disagreement rule written twice, once in `unanimousCompare` and once here from raw operand text, in the manner the PE parser is checked against an independently written from-spec reader. `named` is the oracle over the output, and it is the half with the holes. Liveness for the first is `soleAdmitted === sole` (32/13/9/22), for the second `soleNamed` (6/4/2/3 of the same 76).
  - **The witness is `t64!sub_140002A2C` at `0x140002afd`**, a block holding only `je 0x140002c5b`, entered by fallthrough from `test rbx, rbx` and by `jmp` from `test rbp, rbp`. Under the control it emits `if (rbx_2 == 0)` at emitted line 67 against `if (!!__unrecovered_1 /* je */)` on the shipping tree — and it is one of the four sites the polarity anchor cannot reach, which is why `named` alone would not have been enough.
  - **Disagreement is text inequality after folding `test`'s two exact equivalences** (`test x, x` ≡ `cmp x, 0`; `test` is commutative), so any other coincidental equivalence is a false positive to hand-adjudicate; all 12 pairs here are two different registers, two different immediates, or two widths of the same register whose values come from *different calls*. It under-reports a rip-relative compare, is gated only where every owner is a `cmp`/`test`, and the presence of a guard is the whole question **only while refusing is the only sound answer** — a mechanism materialising the test in each predecessor would make a guard here correct and would have to sharpen both counts, exactly as `wrongOperand` was sharpened when captures landed.

  **`named` changed its question in `peek-a-bin-xskz` and the old one had become the wrong one.** It used to be "is a guard emitted at this jcc at all", which is right only while refusing is the only sound answer; the lifter now *materialises* a spoiled compare's operands and 104 of those guards are recovered and correct, so the old count would gate red on every one. It now asks whether the emitted condition **reads that capture** — derived in `staleGuards.ts` from the compare address the shape scan itself found, not imported from the lifter. The old number survives as **`emittedAtShape`**, report-only, **29/5/3/21** at `97249dc` + xskz: it is the *recovery*, and a fall in it is what a regression looks like. **The rule this looks like it should be — "names a register the spoiler wrote" — is refuted by its own negative control**: copy propagation rebinds the overwritten register out of the expression (`peek-a-bin-xe01`'s central finding), so the defect emits `edx == 5` for `cmp eax, 5 / mov eax, edx / je` and `*(int32_t*)(edx) == 5` for `cmp [ecx], 5 / mov ecx, edx / je`, and a clobbered-register scan reports **0** on both. Negative-controlled in three directions: bypassing the refusal without capturing exits 1 at **29/5/3/21 = 58**, i.e. *every* row in `emittedAtShape` with the same unrecovered-value counts as the fix, so only this gate separates them; base code with the refined audit reads **0 named, 0 emittedAtShape**; and the historical control for the two original fixes still reports 58 with the emitted C byte-identical to `e22ba6e` (md5, all four), which are precisely the 58 guards `compare.mjs` independently reports leaving the audited set.
- **A guard is now written by the same dataflow that writes everything else, and the ledger is 770 guards.** `peek-a-bin-c33`'s flip — `extractCondition` reading the extracted `IRBranch` instead of re-parsing `insn.opStr` — moved the emitted condition at **223/187/163/201 jcc addresses** (t32/t64/w64/w32) with `only-change 0`, i.e. no guard appeared that was not there before. Classified by hand over all 770, and this is the honest split rather than a headline:
  - **323 state a constant** the base named a register for (`eax < edi` → `eax < 0`, `arg_0 == (uint16_t)eax` → `arg_0 == 0xFFFF`) — the base was reading a register a `xor` had zeroed.
  - **192 replace a stale name** with the value the guard means, which is `mapReads`' new `branch` arm plus `splitStaleReads` (`while ((uint16_t)r8d != *(uint16_t*)(rcx))` → `!= *(uint16_t*)(rcx_4)` in a loop whose body advances RCX). These are wrong names in the base, not spellings.
  - **122 other**, sampled: an absolute address resolved (`*(uint16_t*)(eax)` → `*(uint16_t*)(0x410AFC)`), a struct base repaired.
  - **133 lose a frame-slot promotion** and are the one negative: `ebx < arg_0` became `ebx < *(int32_t*)(ebp_1 + 8)`. **Same address, nothing false** — `ebp_1` is EBP's own split-repair name and the base already emitted `ebp_1`-based slots at *other* sites in the same functions. The cause is that `promote.ts` recognises a frame slot only off the literal frame register and does not follow a split copy of it, which is pre-existing and now more visible. Not fixed here: teaching `promote.ts` to resolve `<framereg>_N` would re-promote base-visible sites too and needs its own measurement.

  **7 guards per x64 binary left the audited set** (`polarity guards audited` 547 → 544 and 477 → 474, `guards w/o single compare` +6 on each), which `compare.mjs` marks as a regression. All 7 shapes were read against `objdump` and the emitted C: every one is an *improvement* the auditor cannot anchor because the condition became a field access — `arg_0x30 != 0` → `((struct_44 *)rdx)->field_0x30 != 0`, where `[rdx+0x30]` on x64 was never an argument slot and the struct gained two real fields. Everything else held: 0 throws, 0 inverted, 0 statements dropped, 0 callees lost, gcc 1127/1127, offsetof ratio 1.00, arity unmoved (79/90/96/85 exact, over 0/3/3/0), wrong-operand guards 0 named, stale version-0 0 wrong. **Unrecovered values did not move at all** — 159/94/73/122 *as they stood at that commit*, token occurrences identical — so nothing was traded for an admission. (That pair is historical: `peek-a-bin-92yy` and `peek-a-bin-suql` have since taken it to 106/45/35/87.) Emitted text grew 238 lines corpus-wide (+0.4%), all of it split-read repair copies.
  **Loop shape was measured directly, because no gate models it** (`peek-a-bin-9q2`): `for` 3/5/5/3, `while` 114/107/100/101, `do/while` 78/77/76/77 — **identical before and after**. (Quoted here as 192/184/176/178 until the instrument split the two constructs; that figure was the raw `while (` match count, i.e. these two rows summed.) That is not luck; it is what `foldBlock`'s refusal to inline into a branch buys, and removing the refusal moves it.

- **The `eflags` proxy is gone, and removing it was a net recovery rather than a tidy-up** (`peek-a-bin-c33` stage 2b, measured base `f685b6d`). `liftBlock` no longer emits `eflags = …` for `cmp`/`test` or the SSE `comis*` forms, and `flagModel.ts`'s forward owner model — until now imported for `isFlagReadingJump` and nothing else — decides which instruction a Jcc reads, so an *arithmetic* flag setter produces a branch too. Emitted C moved in **18 of 1127 functions** and every diff falls in three classes, all read by hand:
  - **10 sites over 8 functions state a real value where the base named the unassigned `eflags`** (`rdx = eflags >> 3` → `rdx = rcx - 0x140013000 >> 3`, `ecx = eflags - 1` → `ecx--`). Corpus-wide, reads of an unassigned `eflags` go **20 across 14 functions → 0**, and a crude read-but-never-assigned scan over all emitted identifiers falls 246/602/588/250 → 242/599/585/246.
  - **2 guards recovered** (one per x64 binary): `var_30 = r15b & 0x48; if (__unrecovered_2)` → `al = r15b & 0x48; var_30 = al; if (al != 0)`. Unrecovered values 94 → 93 and 73 → 72; t32 and w32 unmoved at 159 and 122. The base failed these on `conditionFromFlagResult`'s "is this still the register's last write" check, which folding had already made false — the check SSA now answers by construction.
  - **9 dead statements removed**, each one whose only reader was the proxy `ssaOptimize` then deleted anyway. Seven are provably unread. **The two that are not are the one cost**: an `al = *(uint8_t*)(0x1400159A0)` disappears on each x64 binary while a later line still reads `al` — but the base's value for that read was *already wrong* (the `not al` / `and al, [rbp+0x68]` between them are missing in both), so this makes a pre-existing defect visible instead of plausible. It is not a new class.

  Loop shapes **identical**: `for` 3/5/5/3, `while` 114/107/100/101, `do/while` 78/77/76/77. Every gate held — gcc 1127/1127, offsetof ratio 1.00, polarity 0 inverted, 0 throws, 0 statements dropped, 0 callees lost, 0 loops short, arity over 0 with exact and under unmoved, wrong-operand guards 0 named with `shapes` unmoved at 53/9/6/38, stale version-0 0 wrong and 0 spoiled. `compare.mjs` flags **2 rows, both the same thing**: one guard per x64 binary (`sub_14000B094` 0x14000b236, `sub_140009D5C` 0x140009efe) whose **text is byte-identical** and whose polarity *anchor* moved A → A2 — A2 rises by exactly 1 on each, total audited unchanged, both `OK`. It moved because the dead statement that used to open that arm carried the block's start address.
- **Stage 4 deleted the scaffolding and changed nothing at all**, which is the point of doing it as its own step: `protectedFlagDefs`' two loops, the post-fixpoint `eflags` strip, `conditionFromFlagResult`, `RESULT_ANSWERABLE_JCC` and `flagResult.ts`'s backward walk, all removed, and the emitted C is **byte-identical on all 1127 functions of all four binaries** (md5 per binary, `compare.mjs` `CHANGED 0` / verdict "no regression"). Measured before it was attempted, too: instrumented over the whole corpus with the scaffolding still present, `conditionFromFlagResult` fired **0 times**. `peek-a-bin-wf7t` closes here.

- **A register read that names a value it does not hold is a gate**: `corpus/staleReads.ts`, **0 wrong and 0 spoiled repairs on all four binaries**, over 33/182/181/34 sites at `82ed61e`, from 28/78/28/78 wrong reads and 13/19/13/18 spoiled copies before the fix. Unlike the two audits above this one *is* gated at zero — every row it can report is a provably wrong name (a store through the wrong pointer, a call given the wrong argument), which is `polarity inverted`'s character rather than a count awaiting a threshold. It must run `foldBlock`: stopping at `destroySSA` reports 151 on t64 instead of 78, by counting dominating writes that fold into their single use (`peek-a-bin-dqpk`). Two things it must keep doing, both of which it once did not: attribute a phi's definition to each *predecessor* as well as to the phi block, since that is where the copy lands, and compare the *name* rather than the canonical register, since a correct live-range split emits two names for one register (`peek-a-bin-fppy`, `peek-a-bin-pzws`).
- **A register a `pop` wrote, read under its previous value, is a GATE at 0** — `corpus/popReads.ts`, **0 wrong and 0 `ret`-wrong on all four binaries** at `bd73798` + `peek-a-bin-6f3v`, over **151/5/5/146 of 1095/350/328/1043 pops paired by the lifter** (t32/t64/w64/w32). It was **7/0/0/6 wrong over 5/0/0/4** pops with 67/0/0/63 paired at `6952d53` and 4/0/0/4 over 2/0/0/2 with 73/0/0/68 paired at `6d5ae92` + `peek-a-bin-6ilz`. Every row it can print is a provably wrong name — a store through the wrong pointer, or a `return` of a loop counter where the machine returns a pointer — which always gave it `polarity inverted`'s character rather than a baseline's; the standing instruction was to gate at 0 the moment a fix got there, and three fixes did: `peek-a-bin-3axd` (block-local `push <imm>`), `peek-a-bin-6ilz` (the same idiom across a branch, a **phi of immediates**, so the definition lands in each predecessor) and `peek-a-bin-6f3v` (a matched `push <reg>` / `pop <reg>`, by `matchedStackSlots`' balanced-depth model over the whole CFG).
  - **A PAIRED pop leaves this audit's population, so read the ratio beside the gate.** The scan skips any pop the lifter defines, and it *must*: its test for "wrong" is that the register is read at all after the pop, a shape test that only means something while the pop is not a definition. So a rule that claimed every pop would drive the gate to 0 **by no longer looking**, and `popsLifted/pops` is what makes that visible — 151 of 1095 on t32 still leaves 944 examined. The liveness assertions are in the same `it` as the gate for that reason.
  - **Both benign counts read 0 now, and that is the population leaving rather than the fact changing.** The one benign row per x86 binary was t32!`sub_40A925`'s `push eax / pop eax / ret 4`, and 6f3v pairs it; its emitted C is **byte-identical** either way, which is the direct evidence the count was standing in for. A 0 here therefore no longer argues against a blanket refusal of every pop — `peek-a-bin-4ynk`'s measured table does, and it is on the bead.

  Three things the filed bead had wrong, and they are the reason to trust the number:
  - **The write test must be the machine's, not the IR's.** With an IR-level "first write after the pop" the count reads ~30 per x86 binary, because it attributes *other* passes' defects to the pop — `t32!sub_40D99A` accounted for 11 of them and is not a pop defect at all — it was `foldBlock` deleting a definition that escaped its block, since fixed and gated (`peek-a-bin-7eyn`).
  - **x64 is clean, and its apparent rows are false positives.** All 6 are the epilogue `mov rax, rbx / pop rbx / ret`, where `return rbx` names a value copied *before* the pop that the C never reassigns — correct output.
  - **Refusing every `pop` would cost almost nothing in benign restores, and is still the wrong trade.** Benign restores are **0**: an epilogue restore is followed by `ret`, not by a read. The only survivor was the implicit read a `ret` makes of the return register, 1 per x86 binary. So the bead's fear that refusal "trades every currently-correct save/restore reading" overstated it by two orders of magnitude — but refusal was implemented and measured anyway, and it takes unrecovered values 106 → 166 on t32 and 87 → 146 on w32 (+229 token occurrences, 66 functions changed) to buy 25 wrong readings, with most of the rise *masking* an unrelated defect behind an admission. Not taken; both value-recovering splits were (`peek-a-bin-6ilz`, `peek-a-bin-6f3v`).

  Validated by negative control in two directions: handing `liftBlock` no `stackSlots` takes the gate red at exactly the 4/0/0/4 wrong and 2/0/0/2 `ret`-wrong of `bd73798`, naming the sites; and disabling `peek-a-bin-3axd`'s pairing takes t32 **7 → 78** wrong over 44 pops and w32 **6 → 61**, so the instrument sees the class and 3axd fixed 71 of t32's 78. One instrument defect was caught that way and is worth remembering — **"is this pop already lifted" has to be asked of the lift, not of the lowered program**: `push 0x1a / pop eax / ret` *is* lifted, the constant then folds into the `return` and DCE deletes the assignment, which printed 4 false rows per x86 binary. **This audit replicates `pipeline.ts` stages 1–3 plus `foldBlock`, and the replica must include step 2b** — `liftCrossBlockPops` before `liftedPops` is collected, since the cross-block definition carries the *pop's* address precisely so that scan finds it. A replica that skips it fails **quietly**: the audit keeps reporting rows a landed fix already removed, so a green tree reads as a red one. `src/disasm/__tests__/stackIdiom.test.ts` fails if any file calling `liftBlock(` does not also call `liftCrossBlockPops(` — and, as a **separate** assertion, `matchedStackSlots(`, which reaches `liftBlock` as an argument rather than as a pass over the lifted blocks, so a half-finished edit can carry one and not the other. `staleReads.ts` and `lostDefs.ts` replicate the same loop.
- **A definition the fold deleted while a later block still read it is a gate**: `corpus/lostDefs.ts`, **0 reads on all four binaries**, from **168/110/110/156 (544 over 172 functions)** at `91085f3`. `foldBlock` inlines a definition read exactly *once* into that reader and drops the assignment, and it is handed ONE block — so "once" counted only that block's reads and a value read again in a successor was deleted out from under every later read. Every row is a register the emitted C reads and never assigns, which is `polarity inverted`'s character rather than a baseline's, and gcc is structurally blind to it because `preludeFor` declares each undeclared identifier as its own `long`. Two things about it are the point:
  - **The discriminator is a before-and-after, not a scan.** A read no definition reaches is *usually correct output* — it is the function's entry value, i.e. what a parameter in a register looks like — which is why CLAUDE.md's crude read-but-never-assigned scan is an upper bound and cannot gate. The audit brackets the fold and counts only reads that **had** a reaching definition before it and have none after; an entry value has none on either side. `entryReads` (1159/1513/1460/1498) reports that legitimate population beside the gate, so a zero for want of observation is visible.
  - **It brackets `foldBlock` and nothing else**, and its independence is weaker than the audits above it: `fold.ts` decides what to keep from *liveness*, this decides what went missing from *reaching definitions*. Dual analyses rather than one shared routine, but a regression gate on one pass rather than an oracle outside the question. Negative-controlled — force `escapes` false in `fold.ts` and `npm run corpus` exits 1 at exactly the 168/110/110/156 a separate throwaway instrument reported (`peek-a-bin-7eyn`).
- **A switch arm claiming the switch is over while its block goes on is a gate**: `corpus/armExits.ts`, **0 on all four binaries**, from **35/0/0/17** at `91085f3` (25/12 ending in a conditional jump, 10/5 in a `jmp`; 31 and 14 of them a false claim). `armBody` claims exactly one block per arm and used to close it with `break` however the block ends — and `break` is a *claim* about control flow, not an appendable terminator. Every row is therefore a provably false statement the output makes rather than an omission, which is `polarity inverted`'s character. Three things about it are the point:
  - **The denominator is 0 on both x64 binaries**, which recover no jump table at all, so `structureSwitch` never runs there and a green gate on those two says nothing whatever. The liveness check is consequently tied to the recovered-table count rather than asserted blind: where tables exist, arms and truthful closures must both be non-zero (72 arms over 5 functions on t32, 54 over 3 on w32 at `5c5dab9`, every one truthful).
  - **It hangs off `pipeline.ts`'s existing `StructuringTap` rather than a second mechanism.** Whether an arm's terminator is true of the machine is not a question the emitted C can be asked — a `break` looks identical whether the block ends or goes on — so `structureCFG` reports each closure to an observer wired on exactly the tap's condition, and `sweep.ts` reads it beside the statement snapshot. Emitted C is **byte-identical on all four binaries** with the audit running, which is the proof it observes without disturbing.
  - **It is scoped to `armExit`'s decision and to `buildCFG`'s successor list.** A `break` elsewhere in the tree is not judged; a block whose real successor the CFG never drew reads as a block with nowhere to go, the blind spot function sizing gave the loop-exit audit; and an arm that under-emits its *body* while spelling its exit correctly passes, which is `peek-a-bin-pqs5`'s residue — **that residue is now measured, at 0, by the row below, and the gate provably cannot see it**. Negative-controlled — replace `armExit`'s body with `[{ kind: "break" }]` and `npm run corpus` exits 1 naming exactly 35/0/0/17, the figures an independent throwaway probe reported (`peek-a-bin-pqs5`, `peek-a-bin-64gp`).
- **A case label whose whole body is `break;` is REPORTED and is not a gate**: `emptyCaseBodies` in `corpus/emitAudits.ts`, **0 of 72 case labels on t32 and 0 of 54 on w32** (0 of 0 on both x64 binaries) at `d8d2d02` — **the denominators are now 80 and 62 at `74879a1`**, because `peek-a-bin-padl` recovered two more jump tables per 32-bit binary, and the count is still 0, from **10 of 72 on t32** when `peek-a-bin-37az` was filed on 2026-08-12 — 8 of the 27 labels `peek-a-bin-mk42` had just added, plus 2 pre-existing in `sub_40887C`. `case N: break;` says the case does nothing, which a reader cannot tell from a case whose block was emitted elsewhere. **The bead's defect no longer exists**: `peek-a-bin-dp6` spells a reclaimed block's arm as a `goto` to the label it *was* emitted under, and `peek-a-bin-pqs5`'s `armExit` spells the arm block's own exit, so every one of t32's 72 and w32's 54 labels is now 29/29 a lone `goto`, 43/25 a body of its own, and 0/0 bare. Five things (`peek-a-bin-37az`):
  - **It is NOT gated, and the refusal matches `armExits.ts`'s own.** Where the short-circuit fold consumed the target block without emitting a label there is no name for a `goto` to use and `break` is all that is left — `armExits` reports that population as `unnameable` and refuses to gate it for exactly this reason. Gating here would additionally rest on an empty population: `unnameable` is 0 at `d8d2d02` too, and CLAUDE.md already records that mistake against `selfAssigns.openOperand`. A **rise** is judged in `compare.mjs`.
  - **Read it beside `a lone goto`.** A rule spelling *every* arm as a `goto` would drive `bare` to 0 by saying nothing about any of them, so the three buckets are reported together and sum to `labels`.
  - **THE NEAREST EXISTING GATE IS BLIND TO IT, and that is measured rather than argued.** Under the negative control — `armBody`'s reclaimed-label branch returning `break` instead of `goto`, i.e. `peek-a-bin-dp6` reverted — this row goes **0 → 29 on each PE32 binary** and `compare.mjs` flags it, while `armExits`' gate `falseBreaks` stays at **0** and its `unnameable` rises only **0 → 1**: 28 of the 29 reclaimed blocks end in `ret`, so `succs.length === 0` and the audit correctly calls them *truthful*. **`npm run corpus` exits 0 over that whole control** — every gate green with 29 cases per binary claiming to do nothing — which is why the class had to be hand-counted with a throwaway script when the bead was filed.
  - **The two stop rules in `caseBody` are the whole instrument** and each was wrong in a first draft. `emit.ts` puts a case label at the *same* indent as its `switch (`, and the switch's closing brace with it, so a stop at a strictly shallower brace runs the last arm's body on into whatever follows the switch and a genuinely bare final arm reads as one that does work — the audit reporting 0 for the one reason indistinguishable from a clean tree. And a `loc_` label is emitted at **column 0** whatever its nesting, so an indent-only rule ends the body at the first one; it is skipped by name instead. Both are pinned in `build/caseBodyAudit.test.ts`, with the grouped-label, nested-brace and conditional-exit shapes.
  - **It judges the body's emptiness, never its contents.** An arm that emits half its block passes, as does one that emits the wrong statements; the emitted C is self-consistent either way and the oracle is `objdump` read by hand. What *was* read by hand at `d8d2d02`, against `objdump -d -M intel`, is `t32!sub_407ABC`: case 1's block at 0x407c3d is seven stores and `jmp 0x408656`, and the arm emits exactly seven assignments and `goto loc_408656`; case 2's at 0x407c6d is `movsx eax,dl / sub eax,0x20 / je 0x407cbf` and case 3's at 0x407ccb is `cmp dl,0x2a / jne 0x407cfc`, and each arm is exactly that block and nothing less. Corpus-wide, **every one of t32's 98 and w32's 67 arm `goto`s names a label its own function defines** (0 undefined, beside the standing `dangling 0`), so the case bodies are reachable in the output rather than lost — the bead's hypothesis that they are "emitted somewhere other than under their case label" is right, and it is `peek-a-bin-64gp`'s measured-and-refused decision rather than a defect.
- **A register name the image has no encoding for is a gate**: `unencodableNames` in `corpus/emitAudits.ts`, **0 mentions over 573 PE32 functions**, from **124 mentions of 18 distinct names over 19 functions** at `d514274` (71/18/11 on t32, 53/13/8 on w32; 21 and 14 invented prelude declarations with them). `canonReg` maps every alias to the 64-bit parent because that is the register's *identity* and SSA keys on it, so any path letting a canonical name reach the page prints `rcx` in a function whose every other line says `ecx`. Three things about it are the point:
  - **It is asked of PE32 only, and the restriction is what makes it an oracle.** A 32-bit image has no RCX, so every occurrence is provably a name no instruction wrote and no reader can mean — `polarity inverted`'s character rather than a count awaiting a threshold. On x64 `rcx` is an ordinary correct spelling and separating a canonical name from a real 64-bit read needs the live range's own width, which is `registerSpeller`'s question and is not recorded in the emitted text; so the x64 pair contributes a structural 0, a green row there says nothing, and `funcs` beside it is the liveness half.
  - **The two gates nearest to it are both blind, one of them by design.** `gcc` cannot see it because `preludeFor` declares every undeclared identifier as its own `long` — `rcx` and `ecx` are two unrelated variables — and `corpus/staleReads.ts` compares the *name* a read uses rather than the canonical register, deliberately, since a correct live-range split emits two names for one register; so a canonical name reads to it as a legitimate second live range and passes. Until this existed, CLAUDE.md's crude read-but-never-assigned scan was the only thing that had ever seen the class, and only as an upper bound.
  - **Negative-controlled**: restore the phi operand's spelling evidence in `splitStaleReads` and `npm run corpus` exits 1 naming exactly 124, with the emitted C **byte-identical to `d514274`** on all four binaries (md5) — so the instrument and the fix are provably measuring the same thing (`peek-a-bin-0s6e`).
- **A declared parameter a callee-saved register overwrites at entry is a gate**: `paramClobberedAtEntry` in `corpus/emitAudits.ts`, **0 rows over 430/578/414/565 declared parameters** at `99203fb`. It exists because the `offsetNamedArgs` row beside it **cannot tell a right change from a wrong one, and that is measured rather than argued**. The Microsoft x64 home space is four slots the *caller* reserves and the *callee* may use for anything, so a home slot holding a saved register is not a parameter: `peek-a-bin-g186` drives `offsetNamedArgs` to 0 by declaring no parameter for such a slot, and the variant `peek-a-bin-sx57` measured and refused drives it to the same 0 by *naming* all 35 slots `arg_<i>` — moving nothing else in the entire report — while printing **11 declared parameters per x64 binary that a callee-saved register overwrites at entry** (10 register saves over `sub_1400027C8`/`sub_14000BEE8`/`sub_14000C24C`, plus one byte local, `arg_3 = r13b`). Asked over the *name* the two answers are identical; asked over the **declared parameter list** they differ. So `offsetNamedArgs` is a **target** and this is the **gate** (`peek-a-bin-15q7`). Three things about it: it is **first appearance, not any appearance** — once a callee has consumed an argument it may reuse the slot as scratch and MSVC does, so only a write preceding every read says the declaration was wrong; the **register set is what makes it a defect rather than a shape**, and the same corpus contains `arg_3 = rax` under that variant which is correctly *not* counted, RAX being volatile; and `params`/`funcs` are the liveness halves, because a text-scraping audit fails by silently matching nothing. Negative-controlled by making `inUnfilledHomeSpace` return false, which takes it 0 → 11 on each x64 binary while `offsetNamedArgs` stays at 0 on all four. Nothing else here sees it — gcc compiles an unread parameter, `offsetof` only checks layouts it was given, and polarity/`staleGuards`/`staleReads` are indifferent to a parameter's provenance.
- **What a call destroys is counted, and the count is not a target in either direction.** `clobbered_<reg>_<n>` in the emitted C is a read of a register version a *call* handed out — the emitter naming an indeterminate value rather than printing a register name that would read as the pre-call value. **11 → 21 reads on t64 and w64, 0 → 0 on t32 and w32**, when `disasm/callSummary.ts`'s per-callee summaries were wired in (measured at `f685b6d`; 17 distinct values across 7 functions per x64 binary). A rise is not a defect: a call that really does destroy a register should say so, and the narrow model reaches zero by saying nothing at all. What makes it judgeable is the emitted **`if`/`while`/`for` counts beside it**, which the same audit reports and which were **unchanged on all four binaries** — the ABI volatile set's measured harm was a guard deleted, and that is where a harm of this shape shows up. The cost is entry-value copies: 103 → 153 on t64 and 96 → 144 on w64, all of them `0 spoiled`, and 66/61 functions of emitted C moved on t64/w64 against byte-identical PE32. Six guards on t64 and five on w64 left the polarity audit's *anchored* set, and every one was read: an entry-value copy inserted at the top of the guarded block moves the anchor, the guard text is unchanged and no guard was lost.
- **Loop shape is reported at last, and the figure it replaces was two constructs added together.** `auditClobbered` counted `if (`, `while (` and `for (` by regex over each function's emitted text, and `while (` matches *both* a top-tested `while (c) {` and the `} while (c);` back edge of a do/while — so the audit's one `while` number was their sum, do/while was **not counted anywhere**, and every session that quoted a loop shape counted that third construct by hand. `clobbered.doWhiles` is the splitter, read per line with the same anchored regex pair `siteOf` uses; the audit and `compare.mjs` now print `if`, top-tested `while`, `do/while` and `for` side by side. Measured at `6d5ae92`: **`for` 3/5/5/3, `while` 114/107/100/101, `do/while` 78/77/76/77, `if` 1987/1934/1717/1759** — the split sums to the raw match count exactly on all four binaries (114+78=192, 107+77=184, 100+76=176, 101+77=178), which is the corroboration that the two readings were ever one quantity. Three things about it:
  - **`clobbered.whiles` keeps its raw meaning, deliberately.** Artifact directories from earlier commits carry it as the sum, so narrowing the field would make every old-vs-new `compare.mjs` run report a fall of exactly `doWhiles` as though output had been lost. `compare.mjs` prints the raw row unchanged, derives the top-tested figure, and falls back to a `NOT MEASURED` note when either side predates the split — verified against the `177ada8` artifacts, where the raw row correctly reads 192 → 192.
  - **It is REPORT-ONLY in both directions and is not a gate.** A `for` that becomes a `while` is a fidelity loss, not a false statement about the machine, and the absolutes move whenever function detection does. What it buys is that a shape change between two pinned runs is a *row* rather than something the next agent has to think to count — which is the whole of `peek-a-bin-9q2`, whose 4× fall in for-loop recognition happened with nothing recording it.
  - **Negative-controlled, and the control shows the blind spot precisely**: perturbing one summary's `doWhiles` from 78 to 60 — a do/while silently becoming a top-tested while — moves both new rows while `while emitted (raw)` stays at **192 → 192**, i.e. exactly the change the old instrument could not see.
- **A direct branch whose target the image does not contain is a gate**: `corpus/wildBranches.ts`, **0 on all four binaries**, from **1/0/0/1 over 4241/4073/3644/3792 direct branches** at `6d5ae92` (t32 0x40b824 `jmp 0x288402b`, w32 0x40964c `jmp 0x2301953`, both `source: "gap-fill"`). A literal branch displacement is resolved by the linker inside the image it is producing, so a filed branch aiming outside `[imageBase, imageBase + sizeOfImage)` is not an instruction the file contains — it is a byte string the disassembler walked into — and every row is provably fiction decided against the PE header alone, which is `polarity inverted`'s character. Three things about it:
  - **It is deliberately not the weaker question.** "This branch leaves its function" is ordinary (a tail call, a `__finally` funclet, an ICF-shared epilogue) and is what `peek-a-bin-y1di`'s instrument asked; "outside the *code section*" would be arguable, since a thunk table lives in a data section on some toolchains. The image is the one boundary nothing legitimate crosses.
  - **It is independent of what it caught.** It is phrased in terms of the emitted instruction stream and `optionalHeader.sizeOfImage` and mentions neither jump tables nor `unboundedTableExtent` — which matters, because `peek-a-bin-xqxy` works by marking table bytes as *data*, and an audit in those terms would be measuring its own input.
  - **It is a LOWER bound, and a loose one.** Bytes read as code register here only where they happen to decode as a direct branch whose displacement lands outside the image: of the four sites `peek-a-bin-xqxy` fixed it saw **one per 32-bit binary**. So a green reading is weak evidence that no data is being decoded; a red one is proof that some is. Every other standing instrument is structurally blind — gcc compiles fiction, polarity judges guards that exist, the statement-drop audit counts drops rather than inventions, and `peek-a-bin-7lb9`'s spurious-run census needs a run of four code-pointer words containing its own dispatch base. Negative-controlled: revert `overlappedTableExtent` and `npm run corpus` exits 1 naming exactly those two rows (`peek-a-bin-xqxy`).
- **A self-assignment in the emitted C is resolved back to its instruction, and only two of the three things that can go wrong are gateable**: `corpus/selfAssigns.ts`, measured at `97249dc` (t32/t64/w64/w32) — **0/0/0/0 `wrong`, 0/0/0/0 `unresolved`, 1/0/0/1 `openOperand` of which 0/0/0/0 uncorroborated, over 30/0/0/30 machine identities across 13/0/0/13 functions**, and every one of those re-measured unmoved at `99203fb`. `eax = eax;` is one line of noise where the instruction behind it is an identity (MSVC's `lea ecx,[ecx+0x0]` NOP, its `mov edi,edi` hot-patch pad, `or al,al`, `add eax,0x0`) and a wrong statement about the machine where it is `add edi, esi`. **This is the one visible trace a LOST OPERAND leaves, and it is why a self-assignment must never be suppressed**: `peek-a-bin-3axd` — 97 wrong reads over 28 t32 functions, including an inverted success/failure return — was found through exactly *two* such lines. Every other gate is structurally blind to the class: gcc compiles `eax = eax`; polarity judges the operator of a guard that exists; `staleReads` and `lostDefs` each check that a definition reaches a read, and a self-assignment **is** one, so the reads below it pass; and the statement-drop audit snapshots *after* `foldBlock`, so an operand removed at or before `foldBlock` is not a dropped statement but a statement one operand short. Five things:
  - **The bead's recommended gate could not be taken literally, and that is the finding.** `peek-a-bin-o7pj` asked for a gate at 0 over "the subset whose instruction is not an identity idiom". That subset is **not empty**: t32 0x403034 and w32 0x40320B are `sub ecx, ebx` whose EBX is zeroed by the only write of it before that address in the whole function, so the fold is right to propagate the zero and `ecx = ecx;` is correct output. A legitimate zero-propagation and a lost operand are the **same shape** from the emitted text, so `openOperand` is **reported and judged in `compare.mjs`**, with the status `unrecovered values` has.
  - **What IS gated is the instrument's integrity, and it is labelled as such.** `wrong` — the emitted name is not even an alias of the destination of the instruction whose address the line carries, i.e. broken attribution no dataflow answer rescues — and `unresolved` — a row that could not be judged at all, gated because a row silently leaving the population is how a gate reads 0 by not looking. Neither has been observed non-zero here, so both are negative-controlled in `build/selfAssignAudit.test.ts` rather than by the corpus.
  - **The whitelist is tested against the instruction's OPERANDS, never its mnemonic alone**, which is the whole reason it keeps its teeth: a lost operand is still spelled in `insn.opStr`, so `add edi, esi` matches neither `add <dst>,0` nor a same-register form however the emitted line reads. Two judgements inside it: the same-register set is a whitelist of mnemonics (`mov`/`or`/`and`/`xchg`) and **not** "the operands are equal", since `add r,r` doubles and `sub r,r`/`xor r,r` zero; and **`mov <r32>,<r32>` on x64 is a zero-extension rather than a no-op** (`peek-a-bin-tez6`), so it is refused at that one width while `mov al,al`, `mov ax,ax`, `mov rax,rax` and every PE32 width stay identities.
  - **Zero-corroboration is a hint and can never be a gate. The standing upgrade to gate it was TAKEN UP, the copy chain landed, and THE GATE WAS REFUSED** — do not re-attempt it. For an `openOperand` row the function's instructions *before that address* are asked whether every write of the open operand register zeroes it, through `callSummary.ts`'s `writtenRegsOfInsn`. It stops at the site deliberately — `sub_402FEF`'s epilogue `pop ebx` would otherwise read as a non-zeroing write and lose the corroboration for a genuine zero. `everyWriteZeroes` now follows a chain of register-to-register copies, so `xor eax,eax / mov ebx,eax` — ordinary MSVC output and a real zero — corroborates. Four things, all measured at `99203fb`:
    - **The strengthening changed nothing, and that is the finding.** Every self-assign count is unmoved on all four binaries (1/0/0/1 `openOperand`, 0 uncorroborated, 30/0/0/30 identities) and the emitted C is **byte-identical on all 1127 functions** — necessarily, since only an audit file changed, but measured rather than assumed (md5 of every `funcs_*.jsonl` and of the whole `cc/` tree, `compare.mjs` "byte-identical throughout", verdict no regression).
    - **The rule is REACHED and it refuses, which is not the same as being unexercised.** Reach census over every arithmetic instruction with a register source in every detected function — the population any future open operand is drawn from — is **284/258/232/261 sites** (t32/t64/w64/w32, 1035 corpus-wide). Of those, **141/119/105/130 (495, 48%) have a pre-site register-copy write of the source**, so the chain is consultable at nearly half of them; corroborated goes **2/0/0/2 → 2/0/0/2** and **chain-only is 0 on all four**. In every consultable case the chain either is not reached, because an earlier non-copy write already refuses, or the copy's own source is not all-zeroing.
    - **The gate is refused because an uncorroborated row is a statement about the SCAN, not about the machine** — and that is exactly where the `arity over` analogy breaks. `arity over`'s provability comes from an oracle *outside* the code under test: no entry in `apitypes.ts` is variadic, so an over-count is provably an argument the machine never passed. Here the judge is the audit's own peephole, which is address-ordered rather than dominance-ordered (`firstCalleeSavedWrites`' one-directional approximation) and enumerates a handful of routes to zero out of unboundedly many — a frame slot the fold proved, a `movzx` of a zero byte, a call returning zero, a phi of two zeroing paths laid out below the site. Each of those is a red gate on **correct** C. And the corpus cannot argue otherwise either way: the population is **one row per PE32 binary**, corroborated *directly* rather than through the chain, so a zero here is a property of a population of size 1 — where `arity over` reached 0 through three real fixes to the decompiler, driving a population of 8/3/3/10 down.
    - **The chain's own trap was implemented deliberately and it moves 112 sites.** Had the recursive call dropped the `writes > 0` requirement, a copy from a register the function never wrote — its arbitrary ENTRY value — would corroborate vacuously: measured, that wrong version corroborates **38/20/20/34 of the 1035 sites** (112, including 40 on the two x64 binaries where the correct rule corroborates nothing at all), i.e. it drives the count toward 0 *by no longer looking*. Two tests in `build/selfAssignAudit.test.ts` fail under it and pass under the correct rule, and only those two. Only `mov <r>,<r>` links; a memory load, a `lea`, a `pop` and an arithmetic result are each refused, which is what keeps the negative control below discriminating (`peek-a-bin-o7pj`).
  - **Validated by negative control, and the control is the historical defect itself.** Disabling `stackIdiom.ts`'s block-local `push <imm>`/`pop <reg>` pairing takes `openOperand` **1 → 4 on both PE32 binaries** with **uncorroborated 0 → 3**, x64 unmoved at 0 and `identity` unmoved at 30 — and the rows it names are `t32!sub_40CBBE` 0x40cd96 `sub eax, esi`, 0x40cda7 `or dword ptr [ebp-0x210], esi` and **0x40d23f `add edi, esi`**, the last being the exact site `peek-a-bin-3axd` records. ESI has six writes in that function and one zeroes it, so all three are correctly uncorroborated while the base row stays corroborated. **All three are still named with the copy chain on** (re-run at `99203fb`), because ESI's first pre-site write there is `mov esi, [ebp+8]` — a memory load, which no chain rule admits. **The denominator is not decoration**: `peek-a-bin-qbk3` emptied the whole x64 population three commits earlier (six `lock or byte ptr [rsp], 0` fences folding to `var_0 = var_0`), so 0 on t64/w64 is as vacuous as `armExits`' zero on the two binaries with no jump table. And it is a **faint** trace — 95 of `3axd`'s 97 wrong reads left none — so a green reading is weak evidence and a red one is proof (`peek-a-bin-o7pj`).
- **A call whose callee the emitted C defines NOWHERE is counted, and the split is the whole instrument**: `corpus/undefinedCallees.ts`, **25/0/0/23 internal and 7/3/3/7 external** (t32/t64/w64/w32) over 1274/1161/1092/1203 `sub_<hex>(` call sites, measured at `d8d2d02`. Every emitted `sub_<hex>(` is resolved against the addresses this binary's output actually *defines* a function at, and a miss is classified by whether the target lies inside the **calling** function's own extent. **Internal** is a body that is in the output, in that same function, further down under a `loc_` label — call and body simply not connected; all 48 rows are MSVC x86 `__finally` funclets the detector folded into their parents (`peek-a-bin-qe8z`, `peek-a-bin-d827`), read against `objdump -d -M intel`, each with **exactly one** direct caller and that caller inside the function it is now part of. **External** is a tail `jmp` to a function detection never produced (4 per 32-bit binary) or an indirect `call dword ptr ds:0x…` through a data-section pointer with no IAT entry, where the minted name is the **pointer's** address (3 per binary, all four) — detection's or the IAT's business, not the emitter's. Four things:
  - **REPORT-ONLY in both directions, and not gateable at 0.** `sub_4038F7();` is an *incompleteness*, not a falsehood — the machine really does call that address and the name is derived from it — so the row has `offsetNamedArgs`' character rather than `unencodableNames`'. A gate is unavailable on the count alone anyway; if a change ever connects the internal half and drives it to 0, gating that half is worth re-arguing.
  - **`internalLabelled` is the row that decided `peek-a-bin-pf5g`** — 8 of 25 and 6 of 23 — and it is why the repair was refused rather than merely unattempted. See the Gotchas entry. **THAT DECISION HAS SINCE BEEN RE-OPENED, and the figures above are `d8d2d02`'s.** `peek-a-bin-d827`'s fifth admission added one internal row per folded funclet, so at `74879a1` the count is **33/0/0/31 internal over 64 rows**, and `internalLabelled` is **15 of 33 and 13 of 31** — the nameable-label fraction went from 32% to 45%, because the scope-table rule folds funclets whose body IS the block leader. `peek-a-bin-pf5g` is open again at P2 for exactly that: the refusal was sound at 32% and has to be re-argued at 45%, not inherited. `compare.mjs` flags the rise and exits 1, which it did on the `d827` integration, correctly.
  - **Nothing else here sees the class and `gcc` is blind STRUCTURALLY.** `ccSyntaxCheck` compiles one function per file with no prototypes, so *every* callee in the corpus is an implicit declaration and `-std=gnu89` accepts one **silently — gcc 15.2.0 emits no diagnostic for it at all, with or without `-w`**, so there is not even a warning for `preludeFor` to be consulted about and this leaves **no invented prelude declaration either** and `peek-a-bin-k8i`'s instrument is blind too. (The bead had recorded the mechanism as `preludeFor` inventing `long sub_4038F7();`; measured, it does not — the file compiles first try.) `distinct callees lost` asks only whether the name is on the page, which is CLAUDE.md's own `__SEH_epilog4` camouflage; `wildBranches` judges targets outside the *image* and these are inside it.
  - **Negative-controlled, and the control is the change that created the population.** Reverting `peek-a-bin-d827`'s fourth admission (the `precedingOperands` disjunct in `interiorBranchedOverStarts`) takes `internal` **25 → 13** on t32 and **23 → 11** on w32 while `functions` rises 268 → 280 and 266 → 278 — exactly the twelve funclet starts that admission withdraws per binary — with `external` **unmoved at 7** and `internalLabelled` unmoved at 8 and 6, so all 12 land in the unlabelled class. Run the other way, `compare.mjs` flags the rise on both 32-bit binaries and exits 1: the row would have been red at `21ea66e`, which is why the bead exists. The classifier's own rules are pinned in `build/undefinedCalleeAudit.test.ts`, since the corpus populates each half with one shape apiece and cannot separate them.
- **Which of two OVERLAPPING readings of a struct base became a field is counted, and no column of it is a gate**: `corpus/structOverlaps.ts`, **12 overlaps over 1231 bases at `f3b89ec`** (3 per binary; 6 contained, 6 partial; 8 reaching a declaration; `notMaximal` 0, `ambiguous` 10, `narrowedOut` 10), measured over the same run that reads `offsetof` **946/946 fields across 162 definitions**. It is the only instrument here that can see the class at all, and it is report-only in both directions because neither answer is provably wrong — see the `candidateFields` paragraph under **Struct synthesis** for the adjudication and for the two defects it uncovered. **BOTH OF THOSE ARE NOW FIXED AND THE POPULATION IS 0 ON ALL FOUR BINARIES at `74879a1`** — `peek-a-bin-z8q7` re-keyed the base and `peek-a-bin-iz8j` dissolved as a consequence — so a run today reports 0 overlaps and that is the expected reading, not a broken scan (`groups`/`candidates`/`extents` are the liveness halves and rose exactly where the rows fell). **Its zero is a LOWER BOUND on fabrication and not a clean bill of health**: `z8q7` found a fabricated base (`t64!sub_14000EE7C`, combining `*_errno()` with another object's flag word) that produced no overlap row at all, which is why `peek-a-bin-slkh` was adjudicated against `objdump` rather than against this instrument — and it was decisive there, the "delete the reset" ceiling fabricating an object at `t32!sub_4041D0` with **0 rows here in both runs**. `groups`/`candidates` are the liveness halves and `corpus.audit.ts` asserts them, since a structure-scraping census fails by silently matching nothing (`peek-a-bin-k6hh`).
- **An ARM64 inline comment naming an address the instruction does not reference is counted, and it is the only audit here that reads `Instruction.comment` at all**: `corpus/comments.ts`, **0 coincidences on both ARM64 binaries**, from 248 of 248 and 253 of 253 at `91085f3`. Every row it can report is a comment about an address the instruction provably does not reference, so it has a gate's character; it is *reported* rather than gated only because it is not wired into `npm run corpus` (that run's header names the four x86 binaries, and this needs the two ARM64 ones `preflight.ts` does not model — run `npm run corpus:comments`). Its x86 half is not a judgement but a **digest** of every commented instruction, which is the byte-identity instrument for any change to the shared comment path — and **the digest now has a recorded baseline, without which it could only be used by someone who ran both sides themselves**. Re-measured at `f3b89ec`: ARM64 **275 of 275 and 290 of 290 comments justified, 0 coincidences**, 146 and 157 distinct texts, over 27428 and 24393 instructions — i.e. unmoved from the `peek-a-bin-vg3` fix. x86 digest at the same commit: t32 284 commented / md5 `ebeee5898bcc`, t64 325 / `dc41107568eb`, w64 332 / `92a1c5b0f848`, w32 297 / `3f4e9eb11bdc`. These four md5s **must not move** unless the change is meant to touch the x86 comment path; if it is, restamp them here and say why. Neither half is covered by `npm run corpus`: a comment reaches neither the emitted C nor the IR, so gcc, polarity, `offsetof`, arity and the stale-read gates are all structurally blind to it (`peek-a-bin-vg3`).
- **A guard-shaped line the polarity audit does not understand is a gate, and it is the only gate here that judges the AUDIT rather than the output**: `guardShape` / `guardShapeCensus` in `corpus/guardShape.ts`, reported per binary as `guard-shaped lines` and **0 unparsed on all four binaries**, over **2104/2050/1824/1863 top-tested headers and 78/77/76/77 `do/while` tails** at `baa7f61` — all braced then, and **1532/1500/1312/1348 braced beside 572/550/512/515 inline** since `peek-a-bin-0qib` one-lined the guards whose body is a single terminator, with the top-tested sum unmoved on every binary. Every other instrument here asks whether the emitted C says something true; this asks whether the audit can still see the emitted C at all, and it exists because the polarity denominator *is* whatever the line walk recognised. `braced` and `doTail` are the liveness halves — a text-scraping audit fails by silently matching nothing — and `inline` is 0 today, so it is a bound on the grammar pinned by `build/guardShape.test.ts` rather than anything measured. See the gotcha below for the population it protects and the three controls it was proved with (`peek-a-bin-vwr5`). `corpus/selfAssigns.ts` reads the same grammar since `peek-a-bin-hfsq`, and reports **`for headers read` 6 per binary** beside a second gate at 0, **`for headers unsplittable`** — a `for` header the grammar recognises and whose clauses the splitter refused, i.e. a site that left a scan gating at 0.
- **The practical file-size ceiling is disassembly, not parsing** — performance envelope in `docs/architecture.md`.

**"Clean" is not "recovered."** 287 of those functions (of 847, when that count was taken) contain an *admitted* gap — a
`__unrecovered_N` or a `/* unlifted: … */` — and compile precisely because the emitter names
what it failed to recover instead of printing something plausible. That is the intended
behaviour, not a defect count, but do not read "all of them compile" as "all of them are right".
(287 was counted at `peek-a-bin-oro`; the funclet change landed after it and the count was not
re-taken.)

**Not verified. Say so rather than implying otherwise:**

- **Nothing has rendered a component.** No jsdom, no `@testing-library/react`, no renderer at all. Hooks cannot be mounted, so hook logic is tested only by extracting the decision into an exported pure function (`parseAnnotationMessage` in `useMcpSync.ts`, `modalScaffold.ts`, `listboxIds.ts`, `asyncMetricState.ts`, `decompileTabsState.ts`) or, for the two dependency arrays that matter most, by checking them against the function body over the AST. `DisassemblyView` and everything split out of it, all six modals, the focus trap, the body scroll lock, the command-palette listbox and the whole a11y pass are verified by typecheck, lint, pure-function tests, build and reading — nothing else. Adding a renderer means new deps plus React plugins in `vitest.config.ts`, and that decision has never been taken.
- **The browser now builds the callee-clobber summary, and the wiring is tested end to end — but no human has seen a `clobbered_` name on screen.** `peek-a-bin-s2ws` is wired and guarded (see the gotcha below for the mechanism): the client→worker hop is asserted through the real `disasmClient` answered by the real `dispatch`, both halves negative-controlled. What that does *not* cover is the render step, same as everything else here — the decompile panel has never been opened. Two residuals worth naming. **The build cost is measured only on 108 KB binaries**: ~18 ms over 16.8k instructions, linear at ~1.07 µs/instruction, so a large image is extrapolation, the same blind spot `REQUEST_TIMEOUT_MS` has. And **the emitted-C effect is measured on the MCP path, not this one** — 21 clobbered reads per x64 binary come from `corpus/`, which drives `FileSession`; the browser path is now the same code with the same inputs, which is an argument rather than a measurement, because no harness here loads a file the way the app does.
- **No human has looked at this branch in a browser.** `peek-a-bin-v2u` is the checklist; ~15 minutes with the app open closes more risk than any further static work.
- **The metrics worker's Blob hand-off is verified for *equivalence* and not at all for *speed*.** That a `Blob` and an `ArrayBuffer` source produce identical results — same checksum, same section entropies, byte for byte, including a `File`, a multi-part Blob and an odd length — is measured, in `workers/__tests__/metricsDispatch.test.ts`, and negative-controlled. That posting one is O(1) is a **spec property plus `peek-a-bin-7hg`'s earlier measurement of the copy it replaces (~100 ms for 253 MiB)**, not something measured here: the corpus binaries top out at ~180 KB, there is no 253 MiB image on this machine, and no test spawns a real worker or renders a component. Nothing has watched a real `File` cross a real `postMessage`, and the drop path is the only one that would (peek-a-bin-ex2).
- **The architecture refusal has never been *rendered*, but everything up to the render step is now covered.** Traced and pinned at `e22ba6e` (`peek-a-bin-8ru3`): the mechanism was already present and already reached — no defect was found, and the fix was coverage, not code. Three facts worth carrying. **The displayed text is not the worker's copy of the message**: `analysisNotice` re-derives it on the *main thread* from `pe.coffHeader.machine` via `unsupportedArchMessage("Code analysis")`, so the unsupported-arch notice would survive total loss of the worker's message, and the RPC error path is load-bearing only for the `"analysis-failed"` kind. **The parser-derived views do stay populated**, and that is now measured rather than assumed: the same fixture built twice with the COFF machine word as the only difference yields identical *and* non-empty headers, sections, imports, exports, imphash, strings, anomalies and buffer, with `dispatch("extractStrings")` pinned to answer under a configured ARMNT state — it is the one parser-derived tab fed by the *refusing* RPC, so an arch gate added there by symmetry is the live hazard. **The branch order at each render site is a guard now**, not a reasoned claim: the notice must precede `disassembling`/`disasmError` in `DisassemblyView` and `isAnalyzing`/`phase === "ready"` in `StatusBar`. What remains is only the render step, which nothing here can perform — no test has seen the banner, the panel or the status bar. Closing it needs a browser and belongs to `peek-a-bin-v2u`; no real ARM32 binary is required, since flipping any PE's machine word to 0x01C4 reproduces it.
- **There is no ARM32, ARM64EC or ARM64X binary on this machine**, so every one of those paths is verified against synthetic fixtures and nothing else. The A64 decode-rate floor is calibrated against real ARM64 and real x64 only — and that calibration is now a *gate* in both directions (`npm run corpus:arm64`), which is a stronger claim about the two bands and **not** a claim about ARM64X: half such an image is genuine A64 and may sit above the floor.
- **ARM64 is measured now, and it is measured only as far as instructions, boundaries, references and tables.** `npm run corpus:arm64` gates the sweep, the floor, `.pdata` conformance, wild branches, the `adrp`/`adr` grammar, switch dispatch and the sweep cache; what it does **not** cover is worth stating in full, because ARM64 has been this project's largest blind spot and a green run there is easy to over-read:
  - **Nothing about ARM64 *semantics* is checked at all.** There is no ARM64 decompilation — the refusal is at `mcp/tools.ts` and precedes address resolution — so no ARM64 IR, no emitted C, no guard, no type, no struct and no call arity has ever been judged. The dozen decompiler gates would each be a vacuous zero, which is exactly why they are not run.
  - **Stack frames and signatures have no ARM64 path** (`peek-a-bin-56q` item 1), so there is nothing to audit and nothing is claimed. An A64 frame is `stp x29, x30, [sp, #-N]!` plus `[x29, #off]` accesses and is learnable from the `.pdata` unwind codes; none of that exists.
  - **There is no ARM64 data-marking pass** (`peek-a-bin-56q` item 2). What exists is a *census* of the population one would have to cover — the two report rows above and the 169 / 111 undecoded in-extent words — which is the instrument a fix would be judged with, not a fix.
  - **Nothing has rendered an ARM64 view.** No human and no test has seen an A64 disassembly on screen: `source: "recursive"` / `"gap-fill"` dimming, the `db`/`dd` rendering, jump arrows, the CFG and the minimap on an A64 function are verified by typecheck and reading. This is the general no-renderer gap, but it bites harder here because the two report rows above are *view* defects.
  - **`.pdata` unwind CODES are not read by anything that audits them.** Begin, end, unwind-info address and handler agree with an independently written reader (419/419 and 381/381); the unwind opcodes themselves are unaudited.
  - **The `bl` call graph is counted, not verified.** 373 / 331 nodes and 1259 / 1086 edges are reported; no oracle checks an edge.
  - **Performance is measured only in Capstone call counts.** `4083 → 1361` and `3393 → 1131` are re-derived and gated; the `~130 ms` and `407 → 166 ms` wall-clock figures in `Arm64SweepCache`'s docstring are not, and no ARM64 timing has been taken on this tree.
- **The 5-minute worker RPC timeout now has one measurement behind it, and a bigger binary than the corpus can be built here.** `REQUEST_TIMEOUT_MS` in `workers/disasmClient.ts` is one budget for all methods — correctly, since the worker is serial and a cheap call queues behind a legitimate multi-minute `hybridDisassemble` — and the *number* was chosen with no large binary available. **`go` is installed on this machine (go1.26.0) and cross-compiles a real Windows/amd64 PE** (`GOOS=windows GOARCH=amd64 go build`), which is how the first figure was taken: on a 2407 KiB image with a 657 KiB `.text`, `parsePE` is **4 ms** and everything decode-bound is **2767 ms, i.e. ~4.3 s per MiB of code**, over 152689 instructions and 1952 functions. The per-RPC split — which is the one that matters, since the budget is per call — is `detectFunctions` **850 ms**, `hybridDisassemble` **1024 ms**, `buildXrefs` **924 ms**, i.e. 1326/1597/1442 ms per MiB of code, all three within 1.2x of each other. **So the largest single RPC reaches 300 s at about 180 MiB of code**, and a 253 MiB image at this file's 27% code fraction (~68 MiB) would spend ~110 s in it — comfortably inside the budget. The number is defensible, with a stated envelope rather than a guess: a 253 MiB image that is more than ~72% executable trips it, and that is what a packed or statically linked body looks like. Still extrapolation in three respects: Go's code density is not MSVC's, this was Node rather than the browser's WASM, and one machine — and the figures were taken under background load, with a repeat spread of 2764–3005 ms on the same tree, so treat them as an order of magnitude rather than a benchmark. The conclusion has enough headroom at 253 MiB that a 20% error does not change it. A timeout that fires on a legitimate run still surfaces as `analysisPhase: "failed"`, indistinguishable from a real parse failure (`peek-a-bin-8c6v`).
- **A real x64 PE with jump tables can be built here too, the tool recovers none of them, and this is SHAPE rather than defect — but not the shape this file used to claim.** `go` (1.26.0) cross-compiles a Windows/amd64 PE containing **20** dense dispatches of the `jmp [reg + reg*8]` form, every one behind a rip-relative `lea` table base, **17 bounded by `cmp <index>, imm` and 3 by an `and <index>, 0x3f` mask**; `detectFunctions` recovers **0**. Re-measured independently at `baa7f61` on a fresh `go build`, agreeing with `peek-a-bin-ayhj`'s own corrections figure for figure. **The figures previously recorded here — 21 dispatches, 19 `cmp`, 2 masks — were wrong**, and so was the sentence claiming this "closes `peek-a-bin-6rge`'s premise": it does not, and the reason is the whole point. Five things:
  - **The dense two-table reader is NOT the path this exercises, and it has still never run on a real image.** Instrumented call counts at `baa7f61`, over the Go image and all four corpus binaries: `readDenseRvaTable` **0** calls and `recoverDenseByteTable` **0** calls on every one of the five. The Go dispatch is memory-indirect, so it routes to `readAbsoluteTable` (21 calls — the 20 scaled sites plus one unscaled `jmp [rax]`) and `readRvaTable` is not even taken; the 32 `recoverX64RvaChain` calls are the image's bare `jmp reg` sites and **0** resolve a chain. On t64/w64 it is 15 `readAbsoluteTable` calls each, all rip-relative IAT thunks, and 0 of everything else. So `peek-a-bin-6rge` — the dense MSVC two-table form — remains verified by synthetic fixtures **only**, and no binary obtainable on this machine can change that.
  - **What defeats the reader is the dispatch's ADDRESSING FORM, not the table's entry format**, and conflating the two is what produced the wrong sentence. The unreadable shape is "table base held in a register, materialised by a separate rip-relative `lea`, dispatched in one instruction" — Go's own spelling, because its backend always materialises an address into a register first. An 8-byte absolute table is *not* itself a problem: `readAbsoluteTable`'s `scaleMatch` already reads `jmp QWORD PTR [rdi*8+0x0]`.
  - **The "clang/gcc-on-Windows emit this too" generality is REFUTED, and now twice over.** Measured with the host gcc 15.2.0 on switches built to provoke a two-table form — 33 dense cases over 7 distinct bodies, and 200 dense cases over 6 — **both codegen modes are already covered**: `-fno-pic` gives `jmp QWORD PTR [rdi*8+0x0]`, an 8-byte absolute table with an *absolute displacement in the operand*, which `readAbsoluteTable` reads; `-fPIC` gives `lea rdx,[rip+0]` / `movsxd rax,[rdx+rdi*4]` / `add rax,rdx` / `jmp rax`, which `recoverX64RvaChain` reads and whose docstring names that shape verbatim. GCC emits **one entry per case at every density tried** (`.rodata` exactly 200×8 and 200×4) and never the dense two-table form at all. Stated limit: mingw-w64 and clang are absent, so PE-targeting gcc/clang is inferred from the same compiler's shape choice on ELF — but it is a two-sided inference covering both spellings, so it would have to be wrong twice.
  - **The harm is measured at exactly ZERO, which is why nothing was landed.** All **20 of 20** table bases are in `.rdata` (`0x1400df300`–`0x1400e09c0`; `.text` ends at `0x1400a8051`), so the gap fill never reaches them and **no byte of any table is decoded as code** — `unboundedTableExtent` is confined to the code section deliberately, and this is exactly the situation its docstring describes. Of the **439** case targets, **439 are already decoded as instruction starts** and **439 are already inside the dispatching function's own detected range**. The residue is CFG edges: 20 indirect-jmp blocks with no successors, hence no `switch` structured and no jump arrows. Contrast the three landed jump-table fixes, every one of which was fiction *in the output* — `peek-a-bin-y1di` 9 lost CFG edges, `peek-a-bin-7lb9` phantom instructions feeding a real store, `peek-a-bin-xqxy` an invented call to an address outside the image. This is absence of decoration, not a wrong answer.
  - **`peek-a-bin-ayhj` adjudicated the register-base reader out of scope at `bd73798` and it stays out, but TWO of its stated obstacles have since been removed by `peek-a-bin-padl` and a future agent needs to know which.** ayhj objected that `readAbsoluteTable`'s bound loop "takes the NEAREST cmp … WITHOUT CHECKING THE COMPARED REGISTER AT ALL", so its 17-of-20 spike only worked by inheriting that looseness; and that admitting the mask "would widen the bounded path's vocabulary at every dispatch", which *can* move the four corpus binaries. `padl` did both — `boundedCaseCount` is now the one declaration, it checks the index register and follows copies, and `and <index>, imm` → `imm + 1` is one of its three recognised bound forms. So those two reasons are spent. What is **not** spent is the load-bearing pair: the generality premise is refuted (bullet 3) and the harm is zero (bullet 4), leaving an unverifiable reader, ungateable in principle, for one compiler's spelling, in a tool aimed at MSVC PEs, where nothing is currently wrong. Reopening should need new evidence — a real MSVC or clang-cl x64 PE using the register-base spelling — and not a re-reading of these facts.

  **The instrument is landed this time**, which ayhj deliberately did not do because its probe hard-coded 20 addresses off one build: `corpus/jumpTableReach.ts`, `npm run corpus:jumptables -- <path>`. Everything it reports is *derived* from the image — the dispatch census from the decoded stream, the section bounds from the PE header, the shape→reader mapping from the operand form — so there is no constant to go stale and it runs against any PE. It is a census and never a gate: it cannot over-recover, because it never decides a table exists. It reproduces every figure above from the tool's own instruction stream, independently of the `objdump` reading that produced them. `corpus/README.md` carries the `go build` invocation and the source so the image can be regenerated rather than stored. **Do not put such a binary in the corpus directory**: the audits iterate over whatever they find there, so an extra binary silently changes the population of every gate and every figure in this section, and Go's ABI and prologues are not MSVC's.
- **The nginx headers and the CSP have never been exercised in a browser** — both are researched from code and build output.
- **The a11y work has never met a screen reader.**
- **MCP → browser WebSocket annotation sync has never been exercised end to end**, in particular since the 127.0.0.1 bind change.
- `@vitest/coverage-v8` is not installed, so `npm run test:coverage` fails.

When a UI or deployment change lands, the honest report says which of these it did *not* move.

## Decompiler Architecture (`src/disasm/decompile/`)

**Pipeline** (`pipeline.ts`): `buildCFG → liftBlock → liftCrossBlockPops → buildSSA → ssaOptimize → destroySSA → foldBlock → structureCFG → cleanupStructured → wrapExceptionRegions → inferTypes → promoteVars → synthesizeStructs → emitFunction`

(`wrapExceptionRegions` is local to `pipeline.ts` and only runs when `.pdata` exception info is
present. The docstring at the top of `pipeline.ts` lists a shorter, outdated order — trust the
code, not that comment.)

**IR** (`ir.ts`): `IRExpr` union (12 kinds: const, reg, var, binary, unary, deref, call, cast, ternary, field_access, array_access, unknown) + `IRStmt` union (18 kinds including if/while/do_while/for/switch/break/continue/phi/try/**branch**).

**`branch` is confined to `liftedBlocks` and never appears in a structured tree — but its *condition* does, and `structureCFG` now takes it as a sixth argument.** `liftBlock` turns a block's trailing conditional jump into an `IRBranch` so its condition is a real IR reader — an SSA version, a reaching definition, a place in every use count — and `pipeline.ts` step 4b lifts every one of them out of `liftedBlocks` into a `Map<blockId, IRBranch>` *before* `structureCFG`, which `extractCondition` prefers over re-parsing `insn.opStr`. Two orderings are load-bearing and neither is obvious:

- The extraction runs **before the tap snapshot**, or the statement-drop audit reports every branch as a dropped statement in each block ending in a conditional jump.
- The branches must not survive into the tree: `detectForLoop` skips any body block whose last statement is not an `assign`, so one left in place takes **for-loop recognition to zero corpus-wide**, silently and with no failing test.

`structureCFG` has a **seventh** parameter after that, and it is a different kind of thing: an optional observer told how `structureSwitch` closed each switch arm, which `pipeline.ts` wires only when it has a tap of its own. It is an instrument reading the structurer rather than evidence the structurer reads, it must never be able to change what the other six decide, and `corpus/armExits.ts` gates its reports at 0 (see the Verification section).

`emit.ts` therefore *throws* on a branch rather than ignoring it — `decompileFunction`'s catch turns that into a counted `throws`, which `compare.mjs` gates on, where a silent arm would make a structural failure invisible. Anything that appends to the end of another block's statement list must go through **`pushBeforeTerminator`** (`ir.ts`): `destroySSA` lowering a phi into a predecessor and `loopInvariantCodeMotion` hoisting into a preheader both did a plain `push`, which was correct only while no terminator existed in the IR.

**Three things about the flip are judgements, not details, and reversing any of them silently breaks something no gate reports** (`peek-a-bin-c33`):

- **`foldBlock` counts a guard's reads but never inlines into a branch**, and — separately — never inlines a definition that escapes its block at all (see the `blockLiveOut` gotcha). Counting is what stops a definition two statements read from being folded into one and leaving the other naming nothing. Inlining *into* the guard is the opposite: it deletes the assignment and rewrites the condition, and both halves do damage — the register is frequently live out of the block (a loop counter always is), and `structureCFG` matches loop shapes on the statement a body block ends with, so `inc eax / cmp eax, 5 / jl` became `while (eax + 1 < 5)` with the increment gone. Measured before the refusal existed: 8 structuring tests changed shape. With it, corpus loop shapes are **identical** — for 3/5/5/3, while 114/107/100/101, do/while 78/77/76/77 on t32/t64/w64/w32, as those stood when the refusal was measured. (`for` is 6/6/6/6 and `while` 111/106/99/98 at HEAD; what this bullet claims is that the refusal moved none of them, not that these are current.)
- **`extractCondition`'s two refusals are asked of the condition read off the *instructions*, never of the IR one.** Both are questions about the machine. Asking `conditionSpoiled` of the IR condition defeats it outright: `cmp eax, 5 / mov eax, edx / je` has its guard rebound to EDX by copy propagation, so the overwritten register is no longer in the expression and the scan finds nothing to object to, while the emitted test is still one the machine does not make (`peek-a-bin-xe01`).
- **Which instruction a Jcc's flags belong to is `flagModel.ts`'s answer, and `lifter.ts`'s `branchFor` is the only place that asks.** It calls `blockFlagOwner` at the trailing jump and refuses four ways, each of which is a case where an answer would be a guess: a jump that reads no flags at all (`jmp`, `jecxz`/`jrcxz`/`jcxz`); an indirect or unresolved target; a **result** owner (or, since `peek-a-bin-frt8`, a **bittest** owner) in a block that also contains a `cmp`/`test`; and a result whose **destination** no longer holds it (`canSpellCondition`). The third is the one that is a policy rather than a fact — `cmp eax, 5 / sub ecx, edx / jne` really does branch on `ecx != 0`, but it is also the shape `corpus/staleGuards.ts` counts as a *superseded* reading, and that gate reads **any** condition emitted at such a jcc as the stale one. Recovering it is a decision to be taken together with the audit, not a side effect of the wiring. A **compare** owner is deliberately *not* filtered on `spoiled`: the guard's reads are what hold the compared values alive through DCE, and the same veto is applied against the machine text in `structure.ts`, which is the only place it can be asked (see the second bullet). A **bittest** owner — `bt <reg>, <imm>` — takes the third refusal for exactly the third's reason and needs no form test of its own, since `parseBitTest` admits only the forms that can be named.

`liftBlock` also clears `RegState`'s flag state on any instruction that is neither `isFlagTransparent` nor a modelled setter, which keeps `setcc`/`cmovcc` reading their own instruction's state. That is a *different* question from ownership and the two are deliberately not merged: the SSE `comis*` forms set flags `setcc` reads, and `flagModel` classes them as a clobber, so a `comis` leaves `setcc` working and produces no branch.

**A compare emits no statement at all, and the `eflags = …` proxy it used to emit was actively harmful** (`peek-a-bin-c33` stage 2b). The proxy existed because a guard was not an IR reader: nothing else named the compared registers, so DCE deleted the instructions producing them, `ssaopt.ts` had to hold the proxy live by hand, and it then had to be stripped again after the fixpoint so it did not reach the page. But `eflags = ecx - edx` is an ordinary IR expression, so **GVN gave it and a real `sub eax, edx` the same value number**, copy propagation rewrote the readers of EAX to name `eflags`, and the strip deleted the only assignment — leaving C that returns a register nothing assigns, with the subtraction the machine performs absent entirely. **20 such reads across 14 functions of the four corpus binaries at `f685b6d`, now 0**; minimal reproduction pinned in `pipeline.test.ts` ("does not rewrite a real value into the flag proxy and then delete it"), negative-controlled against that commit.

**`ssaopt.ts` no longer holds any flag definition live by hand, and `flagResult.ts` is gone** (`peek-a-bin-c33` stage 4, closing `peek-a-bin-wf7t`). Two loops of `protectedFlagDefs` — one for the `eflags` def of a Jcc block (`peek-a-bin-ua8`), one re-deriving an arithmetic setter from the instruction stream via `flagResultSetter` (`peek-a-bin-pu06`) — plus the post-fixpoint `eflags` strip, `conditionFromFlagResult`, `RESULT_ANSWERABLE_JCC` and the backward walk all deleted together. Every one of them existed to stand in for a use DCE could not see; the `branch` arm of `countStmtUses` *is* that use. **Deleting them is output-neutral: byte-identical emitted C on all 1127 functions of all four binaries, `compare.mjs` `CHANGED 0`, verdict "no regression".** That byte-identity is the proof the hand-holding was redundant, and it was taken as its own measured step for exactly that reason. Measured first: with the scaffolding still in place, `conditionFromFlagResult` was instrumented over the whole corpus and fired **0 times**. `flagResult.ts`'s two survivors — `isFlagTransparent` and `clobberedAfter` — now live at the bottom of `flagModel.ts`, so there is **one** copy of the x86 flag grammar; `flagModel.test.ts`'s drift guard was inverted to fail on a *second* declaration appearing anywhere under `src/disasm/decompile` or `corpus`.

**Two of the defects `peek-a-bin-c33`'s forward flag model was expected to fix as a side effect were fixed without it** (`peek-a-bin-jitf`, `peek-a-bin-xe01`), by two local refusals in `extractCondition` sharing the flag tables (then in `flagResult.ts`, now in `flagModel.ts`). That removes them from c33's justification and from what its Stage 3a has to demonstrate, and it means the **58 wrong-operand guards are already gone from the baseline** any future c33 measurement is taken against — so a c33 run that shows no movement there is agreeing with the current tree, not failing. It did not touch c33's own case, and c33 has since landed on the structural argument alone — see the `branch` paragraphs above and the flip's measurement below. The two refusals survive the flip unchanged and are still asked of the instruction stream; the wrong-operand gate is still 0 named. **`peek-a-bin-xskz` then made the refusals conditional** — they stand unless the lifter materialised the compared values at the compare — so the 58 are no longer an admitted gap but a recovered guard; see the `spoiledCompareCapture` gotcha.

**`ssadestroy.ts`'s `mapReads` has a `branch` arm, and it landed in the same change as the `extractCondition` flip — deliberately, not incidentally.** A guard's registers are reads, so `splitStaleReads` both sees and repairs one; before the arm existed there were ~300 stale, unrepairable reads per binary. Adding it *earlier* was measured and reverted: while step 4b discarded the conditions it cost **+116 emitted lines across 96 functions** in dead repair copies for no recovered value, and none of those reads is version 0, so the stale-read gate stayed green through every one (`peek-a-bin-c33`). Landed together, the repairs are 192 of the 770 guards whose text moved and the whole corpus grows by 238 lines (+0.4%).

### Adding new IRExpr / IRStmt kinds

Adding a kind means updating every switch that dispatches on `expr.kind` / `stmt.kind` — there
are **64** of them (44 switches and 20 if-chains), and a missed one silently drops data rather
than failing. The tables below are accurate about every site they name but do **not** name them
all — roughly 30 are missing, so grep as well as reading. They split into two
groups:

**That 64 counts `IRExpr` and `IRStmt` sites together, and it is a count by reading. For
`IRStmt` the number has now been taken over the AST instead, and the rule matters more than the
figure**, because a hand count has been corrected twice and the two rules do not agree.

The rule: resolve, with the TypeScript checker, every read of a `.kind` property whose object's
type is `IRStmt` or a narrowing of it — matching on the *declared interface names*
(`IRAssign`…`IRBranch`), never on kind strings, or `DisplayRow` (whose kinds include `"label"`)
and every other tagged union in the tree join the count. Then classify: a `switch`, or an
`if`/`else if` chain with **two or more** arms testing the kind, is a **dispatch**; a lone `if`,
ternary or boolean test is a **narrowing predicate**. Over `src/` and `corpus/` excluding
`__tests__`, at `f685b6d` + `peek-a-bin-c33` stages 2b and 4:
