  - **THE WRONG VERSION IS INVISIBLE TO EVERY GATE AND THE FUNCTION COUNT REWARDS IT.** Drop the pre-existing "no caller outside the previous function" test and the rule also swallows `__SEH_epilog4` (`t32!sub_4041B5` / `w32!sub_404415`), which opens `mov ecx, dword ptr [ebp - 0x10]` — reading its caller's frame **by design** — and is called from **31 and 29 sites**; its body ends up as `loc_4041B5:` inside `__SEH_prolog4` while all 31 call sites still print `sub_4041B5()` for a function that no longer exists in the output. `npm run corpus` reports that as clean, with `functions` 280 → 279 and 278 → 277 — *toward* the target — and the whole tree at `99203fb` + this change moving 288/279/275/285 → **280/279/275/278**, and `distinct callees lost` **0**, because that audit counts callees the emitted C loses and the name is still on the page. Implemented and measured deliberately; pinned by a test.
  - **The first instruction is decoded from the boundary, not read off the linear sweep.** The sweep's grid is whatever the bytes in front of it produced, so its instruction at a given address can be a misalignment; the address here is one a `call` names, so it provably *is* a boundary. `firstInstructionOperands` uses `decodeOne`.
  - **`push 0xa; call; pop ecx; ret` funclets are not touched by THIS admission** — the *family* is 23 per 32-bit binary, not the 9 recorded here, and the count is corrected in the two bullets below — because an immediate says nothing about whose frame is in scope, and the only evidence left would be the sole caller, which a helper laid out right after its one caller also has. `.pdata` still outranks all of this (`pdataEndMap.has(prev)` is checked first), so it is a PE32 rule in practice.
- **…and an unconditional `jmp` over a start NOTHING REACHES withdraws the start.** `interiorBranchedOverStarts` refused an unconditional jump as evidence, correctly, because a tail call and a shared epilogue are both spelled that way — but that ambiguity is entirely about the far side of the jump, and both of those alternatives are *reached*. Where the image holds **no direct transfer to the boundary at all** — no `call`, no `jmp`, no `jcc`, no jump-table case — the readings are gone. One candidate corpus-wide, and its harm was an **invented call** rather than a lost `jcc`: `w32!sub_401981` runs to 0x401b4b, the `6a 0c 68` at 0x4019e0 (`push 0xc; push 0x40ece4`, mid-body) cut it to 95 bytes, and its `jmp 0x401aaa` then aimed outside its own range, so the lifter read it as a tail call and emitted `eax = sub_401AAA(); return eax;` for an instruction that never leaves the function — while the 363 bytes past the cut became `sub_4019E0`, reading `[ebp + 0xC]` and `ebx` off a frame it never establishes and calling `sub_4024AE()` with **no arguments at all**. Three things (`peek-a-bin-qe8z`):
  - **`reached` must be a union over every transfer kind**, not the call sites alone. `t32!sub_403A88` and `w32!sub_403CDC` are `mainCRTStartup`'s body, reached by the entry point's own `jmp` and by nothing else and called from nowhere; a call-site-only test swallows both.
  - **The reachability walk still applies.** `.text` carries data and a linear decode turns it into plausible jumps, which is why `reachableCrossings` (renamed from `reachableCondJumpCrosses`, now returning *which kinds* crossed) decodes forward from the previous function rather than reading the sweep's answer.
  - **No standing instrument saw the defect.** `wildBranches` judges branches outside the *image* and 0x401aaa is inside it; the statement-drop audit counts drops rather than inventions; `gcc -std=gnu89` accepts a call to an implicit declaration at any arity; the guard ledger was `CHANGED 0` because the guards were correct on both sides. It was found by censusing the admitting evidence for every detected start, which is the instrument this bead produced — and the literal 0x4019e0 occurs nowhere in the image, so nothing takes its address either.
- **…and the funclet has TWO entries, which is why the first three admissions kept missing most of the family.** MSVC x86 emits the register reloads only the *unwinder* needs and then the funclet body; the parent's own `call` names the **body**, past the reloads, because the parent already has the register loaded. So the body is what gets detected and the reload falls to the previous function, where it is a dead assignment whose only consumer is now in another function — `t32!sub_40388B` ended `loc_4038F4: esi = arg_0;` and nothing else, while `sub_4038F7` called `sub_4023CD()` with **no argument at all**. Adjudicated against `objdump -d -M intel`; the bead had recorded this as "two of them are SPLIT", which is a 10× undercount. The **fourth admission** is the second and third composed and asked one instruction earlier: the instruction ending *at* the boundary reads memory through the frame register, so nothing there established a frame, and it falls into a body every caller of which is inside the same function. Withdrawn, `loc_4038F4: esi = arg_0;` becomes `eax = sub_4023CD(arg_0); return eax;`, and t32 0x405f1a's trailing `loc_405F23: esi = *(int32_t*)(ebp + 8)` — a frame read in a function that never assigns `ebp`, the exact defect the third admission exists to fix — disappears. Four things (`peek-a-bin-qe8z`):
  - **THE FRAME TEST IS THE WHOLE RESTRICTION, AND ONLY THE EMITTED C CAN SEE IT.** Relaxed to "the predecessor is not a terminator", the rule additionally takes `t32!0x4037b2` / `w32!0x403a06`, whose predecessor is `call _invalid_parameter_noinfo` — a call that does not return — and the merge appends `eax = sub_406CC0(7); return eax;` to the arm containing it, **stating that control flows out of a `noreturn` call**; it takes five more per binary besides, for which there is no evidence at all. `npm run corpus` reports the relaxed version as **clean, exit 0**.
  - **Reachability is deliberately NOT tested, and that is measured.** All 12 predecessors per binary are unreached — only the unwinder enters them — so the conjunct would contribute nothing, and it is not kept as a bound because the case it would exclude is *stronger*: a reachable instruction falling into the boundary means the previous function's own execution crosses it.
  - **It is the one place here that reads an instruction it cannot decode *from*.** Nothing names the predecessor's address, so `precedingOperands` walks the linear grid from `prev` — itself a detected start — and takes whatever ends exactly on `boundary`. Landing precisely on an address a `call` *does* name is the self-consistency check: a misaligned grid would have to survive the whole previous function and then come down on a known boundary.
  - **Measured at `cc45263`, both sides pinned: 12 starts per 32-bit binary and all 24 are funclet bodies — no false positive over 558 detected starts.** `functions` 280 → **268** and 278 → **266** (t64/w64 **byte-identical**), instructions unmoved at 18025/16601, 11 surviving functions changed per binary. Every gate figure in the report is identical except the denominators that move with `functions`: gcc 268/268 and 266/266 clean, `offsetof` 346/346 and 354/354 fields over 55/55 and 59/59 definitions at ratio 1.00, polarity anchor-A 517/517 and 441/441 with 0 inverted and 0 mismatch, `guards audited` exactly flat at 919/824/821/729 with `CHANGED 0 / only-base 0 / only-change 0`, **arity completely unmoved** (79/105 and 85/111 exact, under 26, over 0), unrecovered values 36/12/11/33, and 0 on staleReads, staleGuards, popReads, lostDefs, armExits, unencodableNames, wildBranches, callees lost, loops short, throws. `distinct callees lost` stays 0 with its denominator 970 → 957 and 935 → 922 (the merge dedups callee sets), and statements lifted falls by exactly 6 per binary — the six reloads that fold into the call they feed.
- **…and the REST of the funclet family is refused on evidence, with each refusal pinned by a test.** The census at `cc45263` corrects two figures that have been quoted at people: the family is **23 per 32-bit binary**, not the bead's "~40" nor CLAUDE.md's former "9", and it is **two shapes, not one** — 17 `push <imm>; call; pop ecx; ret` and 6 `push <callee-saved reg>; call; pop ecx; ret`, the second of which the record never described. After the fourth admission, **10 family members per binary are still detected — re-measured at `f3b89ec`, and 11 was already wrong at `cc45263` — and only 7 of those are even withdrawal candidates** — the other four have a caller outside the previous function, three of them because MSVC shares one funclet body between two parents' funclets (t32 0x40618d, 0x406196, 0x406c16) and the fourth because `t32!0x40660a` / `w32!0x4054a1` is a real four-caller helper. Anyone quoting 16, 23 or ~40 as the residue is quoting a figure taken before the fourth admission. **All 7 candidates per binary have since been withdrawn on the scope table (`peek-a-bin-d827`, below), so what follows is the record of what was refused BEFORE that evidence was available, and it is still the rule wherever the table is silent or unread.** The family size itself is instrument-dependent and should not be quoted as a fact: a shape scan over `objdump`'s whole linear grid at `f3b89ec` finds **26 per binary (20 `push <imm>` + 6 `push <callee-saved reg>`) plus one 12-byte `pop ecx; mov esi, [ebp - 0x1c]; ret` variant**, against this census's 23/17/6 — the scans differ over genuine one-line helpers of the same shape. The DETECTED subset is the number that means something. Refused, in order of how tempting they are:
  - **`push <imm>` with a `ret` predecessor: no candidate-internal evidence, and none external either.** `push 0xd; call _unlock; pop ecx; ret` is byte-for-byte what a legitimate one-line helper is, and the callee really is one — t32's `_unlock` at 0x406cc0 has 21 call sites of its own.
  - **`push <callee-saved reg>`: the tree already reads that instruction the opposite way.** No x86 convention passes EBX, so an entry point cannot mean anything by reading one — but `peek-a-bin-6lmh` established, against this same corpus, that a `push` of a callee-saved register the function has not yet written is a register **SAVE**, which is what stops `collectArgs32` walking into a prologue. Withdrawing on the value reading would put two contradictory readings of one instruction in the tree; separating them needs the whole body (there is no `pop ebx`, so the `pop ecx` balances the stack and the push was an argument), which is not a kind of reasoning this pass does. Note the fourth admission reaches all 6 of them anyway, by the predecessor — so this refusal costs nothing today and is the guard against reaching for the register instead.
  - **"the boundary is reached by fallthrough from the previous function's reachable code": 1 site per binary, measured**, and that site's predecessor is the `noreturn` `call` above. Refused on measurement.
  - **"the predecessor is unreached", with no test of what it does: 8 per binary, and ONE IS A REAL FUNCTION** — `t32!sub_40E1D8`, which opens with a full `mov edi, edi; push ebp; mov ebp, esp; push ebx` hot-patch prologue, has two callers, and is named by no scope table in the image; w32 has the same shape at 0x40c7f8. Withdrawing it deletes real code with nothing said. **The second counterexample this bullet used to name is REFUTED, by the linker's own record**: the "six-byte thunk `t32!sub_40BEBA` (`call 0x406196; ret`)" and its w32 counterpart 0x4050f4 are the `__finally` handlers of `sub_40BE84` / `sub_4050BE`, named as such by scope table 0x4113f0 / 0x40f2f8, each called exactly once from inside that parent (0x40beac / 0x4050e6), and each a funclet whose body calls the SHARED funclet body. The fifth admission withdraws both. Do not quote "two are real functions" again (`peek-a-bin-d827`).
  - **Moving a start BACKWARDS onto the unwinder entry is confirmed unjustifiable.** The true entries (t32 0x405f17, 0x4038f4, 0x40316f, …) are admitted by nothing and reached by nothing — they are not in the candidate set at all.
  - **The only sound rule left was the SEH scope table read as a funclet-of-parent RELATION, and it HAS NOW LANDED** — `src/disasm/seh32.ts` plus the fifth admission; see the bullet below. Two things this note predicted turned out not to hold. The relation did **not** need "every start inside `[handler, its ret]` is interior to P": at `f3b89ec` no detected start lies strictly inside any funclet's `[handler, ret]` on either binary — the fourth admission had already taken every start that sat past an unwinder entry — so the address-exact form and the range form are indistinguishable here and the range walk was not written. And the capability was in `src/disasm/`, not `src/pe/`: nothing in the PE directories points at the table, only the `push` in the parent's own prologue does. **Never put its handlers in `strong`** still stands, and is now measured — see the sysf bullet.
  - **AND THE COUNT-CHASING RULE IS INVISIBLE TO EVERY GATE, worse than qe8z's own trap.** Withdrawing the whole family on body shape (`push X; call <imm>; pop ecx; ret`, every caller inside prev) takes t32 280 → **262** and w32 278 → **260** — much further "toward" the target — with `npm run corpus` **exit 0**, gcc 262/262 clean, `offsetof` ratio 1.00, polarity 0 inverted, statements dropped 0, unrecovered values unmoved, `distinct callees lost` still **0** (denominator 970 → 949), and `compare.mjs` flagging **exactly the same 2 rows the correct change flags** — `functions scanned`. So no instrument in this repo distinguishes a right withdrawal from a wrong one in this population; the oracle is reading the parent's emitted C at the join, and it is what caught the `noreturn` claim above.
- **…and the FIFTH admission is not an inference at all: the previous function's own SEH scope table names the boundary.** An MSVC x86 function using `__try` opens `push <framesize>; push <scopetable>; call __SEH_prolog4`, and the `_EH4_SCOPETABLE` in `.rdata` names the filter and the handler of every scope — addresses MSVC emitted **inside** that function, running on its frame with no prologue of their own. `src/disasm/seh32.ts` reads it, `seh32FuncletRelation` turns it into `funclet -> the starts whose prologue names it`, and the admission is `parents.has(prev)`. **Verified layout** (t32.exe, w32.exe, read byte for byte and cross-checked against the 31 and 29 `call __SEH_prolog4` sites CLAUDE.md already records): a 16-byte header `{GSCookieOffset, GSCookieXOROffset, EHCookieOffset, EHCookieXOROffset}` then 12-byte `{EnclosingLevel, FilterFunc, HandlerFunc}` records with **no count field**, `EnclosingLevel` −2 meaning TRYLEVEL_NONE and a NULL filter meaning `__finally`. Measured at `f3b89ec`, both sides pinned: **8 starts withdrawn per 32-bit binary** — t32 0x403334, 0x4037b2, 0x405c2a, 0x405d64, 0x406a83, 0x406c0d, 0x406d90, 0x40beba and w32 0x403588, 0x403a06, 0x404883, 0x404c91, 0x4050f4, 0x405f8b, 0x406440, 0x4065ca — `functions` **268 → 260** and **266 → 258**, instructions unmoved at 18025/16601, **t64/w64 byte-identical**, exactly the 8 parents' emitted C changed per binary, and `polarity guards audited` **exactly flat** at 517/574/497/441 with `CHANGED 0 / only-base 0 / only-change 0` on all four. Every gate flat: gcc 260/260 and 258/258 clean, `offsetof` **unmoved** at 342/342 and 355/355 fields, 0 inverted, 0 mismatch, 0 throws, 0 statements dropped, 0 callees lost, 0 loops short, arity exact/under/over unmoved, unrecovered values unmoved at 36/12/11/33, staleReads/staleGuards/popReads/lostDefs/armExits/wildBranches/unencodableNames all 0, and loop shape identical (`for` 6/6/6/6, `while` 111/106/99/98, `do/while` 78/77/76/77, `if` 1987/1938/1719/1759). Seven things, and the first is what the function count cannot tell you (`peek-a-bin-d827`):
  - **THE ORACLE IS THE PARENT'S EMITTED C AT THE JOIN, and all 16 were read against `objdump -d -M intel`.** Fourteen are the textbook outcome: the funclet body appears at the end of its parent as `loc_<addr>:` holding exactly its own two lines, and the parent's `call` at the join spells the same address — `sub_4069F0` now ends `sub_406A83(); eax = sub_4041B5(); return eax;` then `loc_406A83: eax = sub_406CC0(1); return eax;`. One per binary is a real improvement the bead predicted: t32 0x405c2a / w32 0x405f8b is the 12-byte variant `push 0xc; call _unlock; pop ecx; mov esi, [ebp - 0x1c]; ret`, and the base emitted it as a standalone function with a **phantom `int32_t var_1C;` nothing read**; withdrawn, the declaration is gone and the body reads `esi = var_1C` off the parent's own slot, which is what the machine does.
  - **The sixteenth is the `noreturn` case CLAUDE.md warned about, and the warning does not survive contact with the two texts.** t32 0x4037b2 / w32 0x403a06 sits after `call 0x402c35` (`_invoke_watson`), so its body merges into that block rather than becoming its own region, and the arm gains `eax = sub_406CC0(7); return eax;` — exactly what the fourth admission's relaxed-frame-test control produced. But **the base's claim about the same path was also false and less literal**: it closed the arm at `sub_402C35(...)` and let control join `loc_403793`, which the machine's block does not branch to. The change is the byte-for-byte fall-through (0x4037ad `call`, 0x4037b2 `push 7`, 0x4037b4 `call`, 0x4037b9 `pop ecx`, 0x4037ba `ret`), and 0x402c35 **has a `ret`** at 0x402c59 — nothing in this tree models `noreturn`, so neither reading is checkable and the change's is the one the instruction stream states. It is also why `internalLabelled` rises by 7 where `internal` rises by 8: this is the one funclet with no label to name.
  - **`corpus/undefinedCallees.ts` is the instrument, and it moved by exactly one row per withdrawn start**: `internal` **25 → 33** on t32 and **23 → 31** on w32, `internalDistinct` likewise, `internalLabelled` 8 → 15 and 6 → 13, external unmoved at 7. That is the documented, accepted consequence of folding a funclet (`peek-a-bin-pf5g`) and not a new class.
  - **`parents.has(prev)` is what makes it a RELATION, and protecting the same addresses instead is now measured rather than argued.** Feeding the tables' named addresses into `strong` re-introduces **9 withdrawn starts on t32 and 7 on w32** — 0x401e67, 0x401f61, 0x402928, 0x402a51, 0x402f64, 0x40363f, 0x4058a6, 0x4063b8, 0x40d990 and the w32 equivalents — each of which cuts its parent in half again. 0x4058A6 and 0x4063B8 are the two the sysf note names, so the control lands on exactly the addresses the warning was about. (What the control could **not** exercise is the other half of that warning, *promoting* a handler into the start set: 0x403270 is not in `addrSet` at all — nothing detects it — so there is no measurement behind "0x403276 six bytes inside" and it remains a reading of the bytes.)
  - **The pre-existing "no caller outside the previous function" refusal still runs first and the table does not relax it.** The three shared funclet bodies per binary stay detected (t32 0x40618d, 0x406196, 0x406c16, called from 0x40be90, 0x40beba and 0x40233a; w32 0x402020, 0x402029, 0x4065d3), and **no scope table in either binary names any of the six** — the table names the funclet that *calls* the shared body — so the two rules agree here and the refusal costs nothing measured. `t32!sub_40660A` / `w32!sub_4054A1`, the four-caller helper, is likewise named by nothing.
  - **All three bounds inside `seh32.ts` fire 0 times on this corpus, and that is stated rather than hidden.** Dropping the `call` after the pushes, dropping the `EnclosingLevel` chain test, or letting the table address be a code address each leaves the named set at **37 and 35** and the withdrawn set at **8** — identical. They are bounds that make the claim sound by construction, not measured savings, and each is pinned by a unit test in `src/disasm/__tests__/seh32.test.ts` instead. `parents.has(prev)` is in the same position: all 16 named-and-detected addresses have their parent as the immediately preceding start, so the relation half is unfalsifiable here and is pinned by a test too.
  - **The range form of the relation was NOT written, and the reason is a measurement.** `peek-a-bin-sysf` prescribed "every start inside `[handler, its ret]` is interior to P", because the handler can be the *unwinder* entry 0–6 bytes before the detected body. At `f3b89ec` **no detected start lies strictly inside any funclet's `[handler, ret]`** on either binary — the fourth admission had already taken every start that sat past an unwinder entry — so the address-exact form and the range form are indistinguishable on this corpus and the extra decode would be untestable. If the fourth admission ever loosens, this is the first thing to add back.

- **…and a folded funclet leaves its parent calling an identifier the output never defines — MEASURED, ADJUDICATED AND DELIBERATELY NOT REPAIRED.** The four admissions fold the funclet body into its parent; the parent still contains the `call <funclet>`, so `t32!sub_40388B` emits `sub_4038F7();` at line 16 — declared nowhere — while the funclet's body sits at line 27 under `loc_4038F4:` in the same function. Both halves are faithful in isolation and they are not connected. **25 sites on t32 and 23 on w32 at `d8d2d02`, 0 on both x64 binaries** (**33 and 31 at `f3b89ec` + `peek-a-bin-d827`**, the fifth admission adding one row per withdrawn start) (`corpus/undefinedCallees.ts`; 12 per binary are the fourth admission's, the rest the earlier ones'). All 48 read against `objdump -d -M intel`: every one is a funclet with exactly one direct caller, inside its own parent. **No code change was made**, and the reasons are measurements rather than caution (`peek-a-bin-pf5g`):
  - **`goto` is wrong and re-emitting the funclet is worse.** The machine CALLs and the funclet RETURNs, so control comes back; and a funclet cannot be both a member of its parent and its own function — that is the contradiction the withdrawal exists to resolve, and re-emitting it restores `peek-a-bin-qe8z`'s defect (a body reading `[ebp+8]` off a frame nothing in it establishes).
  - **A COMMENT NAMING THE LABEL IS AVAILABLE AT ONLY 8 OF 25 AND 6 OF 23 SITES, and at the rest it would state something false.** MSVC emits the register reloads only the *unwinder* needs and the parent's `call` names the body **past** them, so at 16 sites per binary the block leader is the unwinder's own entry three to six bytes earlier: at t32 0x405f1a the label is `loc_405F17` and the line under it is `esi = arg_0`, a reload the call does **not** execute. At one site per binary (t32 0x4063b8, w32 0x40224b) the funclet body is a **fallthrough continuation** of the parent's own code and carries no label at all — verified against `objdump`: 0x4063b3 `call 0x406175` falls straight into it. A comment saying only "the body is inside this function" is what the audit already reports, does not tell the reader *where*, and would put editorial into 48 functions of output for no recovered fact — the objection `peek-a-bin-qbk3` records against naming a fence in prose.
  - **Forcing a label at the target is a CFG change, not a spelling one.** A `call` is not a CFG edge here, so the funclet's entry is mid-block by construction; making it a block leader splits a block at a merge point, which is where loop shape is decided and which no gate models. `emit.ts` also has no line to hang a label on at 17 of 25 sites, because at those the reload folded into the body's argument and **no emitted statement carries the target's address** — which is the same fact that denies the label.
  - **The honest outcome was the instrument.** Report-only, split internal from external, `internalLabelled` reported beside them so the refusal keeps its evidence. Emitted C is **byte-identical on all four binaries** with the audit landed (md5 of every `funcs_*.jsonl`, `diff -r` over the `cc/` and `offsetof/` trees, `compare.mjs` `CHANGED 0 / only-base 0 / only-change 0`, verdict "no regression"). **NOT verified:** nothing here read the funclet bodies of the x64 pair, because there are none; and no judgement was formed about whether a GNU nested function is the right long-term spelling, which would need the inline region suppressed and is therefore a lost-code risk of its own.
- **A jump-table case target is not a function start.** `detectFunctions` used to add every case target to the function-entry set, and function sizes are the gap to the next entry in that same set — so the function holding the `jmp [table]` ended at its first case and each case body became a bogus function. Downstream, `buildCFG`'s range guard rejected every target as a block leader, the indirect-jmp block got **zero** successors, and `structureSwitch` was dead code on real input: **no `switch` had ever been emitted for a real binary in the project's history**. Case targets now go to a separate set, outrank a byte-pattern guess at the same address (case bodies routinely follow alignment padding), and are fed to `hybridDisassemble` as seeds via `seeds.ts` — without them, phase-2 gap fill starts *on the table*, decodes its entries as instructions and eats the head of case 0.
- **A recovered jump table's bytes are data, and only `DetectResult.jumpTableSpans` says so.** Seeding the case bodies is not enough: nothing walks *into* a table, so the gap fill still reaches it as an uncovered range and decodes the case addresses as instructions — 32 bytes at t32.exe's 0x4086a4 came out as six conditional jumps aiming past the end of the function they were filed under, and w32.exe produced three more, which were the corpus's last 9 lost CFG edges (peek-a-bin-y1di, the residue of peek-a-bin-g7yp). `detectFunctions` reports the extent it actually read, `hybridDisassemble` takes it as a last optional argument and marks it covered before computing gaps, and the client and `mcp/session.ts` carry it beside the seeds. Omitting the argument keeps the old behaviour, deliberately: "nobody said where the tables are" is not "there are none". A bound the dispatch compares against a *register* — MSVC's `push 7 / pop ecx / cmp eax, ecx`, two bytes cheaper than `cmp eax, 7` — is followed back to the constant, because a table that cannot be sized is also a table whose bytes nothing knows to be data (peek-a-bin-mk42; +3 tables on t32, +1 on w32, and three more `switch`es emitted).
- **…and a table with NO bounds check has bytes but no cases, so those are answered separately.** A bound is the only statement of a switch's *length*, and mk42's sentence — "a table that cannot be sized is also a table whose bytes nothing knows to be data" — left a residue: where nothing bounds the index there is no size, so nothing marked the bytes and the gap fill decoded them. `unboundedTableExtent` (`functionDetect.ts`) answers the second question alone and reports `TableRead.dataOnly` — an extent with **no targets**, because reporting entries whose case order is unknown is what peek-a-bin-div refused. So `jumpTableSpans` is no longer a subset of `jumpTables`' extents; a span with no table is normal and the reverse is not. Four things are load-bearing:
  - **The extent is the maximal run of pointer-width words on the base's own grid that each hold an address inside the code section, scanned in BOTH directions.** Backwards is not symmetry for its own sake: MSVC's reverse `memmove` does `neg ecx` and then `jmp dword ptr [ecx*4 + <base>]`, so the base names the table's **last** slot (t32 0x40b968, w32 0x409602) and a forward-only read finds one entry.
  - **The fallback is on "no cases recovered", not on "no bound found".** The descending shape *does* have a `cmp ecx, 8` in front of it, and reading eight entries forward from the last slot yields exactly one — which is not a switch, and which the caller has always discarded. Keying on the absence of a bound leaves that table decoded as code.
  - **It errs SHORT, deliberately.** Over-reporting marks real code as data, which deletes instructions with nothing said; under-reporting leaves them as they are today. The run must contain the named base — a base that is not an address at all is not answered here but **deferred** to `overlappedTableExtent`, see the next gotcha; it stops at one non-resolving word rather than tolerating a gap; `MIN_UNBOUNDED_TABLE_ENTRIES` is **4**, not the bounded path's 2, because with no `cmp` the run *is* the whole evidence; and it is confined to the code section, since a table in `.rdata` is not gap-filled and there is nothing there to fix. It is also restricted to the *indexed* operand form — `jmp dword ptr [0x40f0a8]` is an import thunk, not a one-entry switch.
  - **The corroboration comes from outside the rule**: at all four sites it fires on, the run length is **8**, exactly the bound the machine imposes (`cmp ecx, 8` / `jb`) — a bound `readAbsoluteTable` cannot see because the compare sits in a control-flow predecessor that is *later* in address order, so the linear `recent` window never holds it.

  **The harm was bigger than fiction in the view, and the two instruments that found y1di were both blind to it.** Measured at `d514274`: 2 tables per 32-bit binary, 64 bytes each, decoded as **25 phantom instructions on t32 and 33 on w32** — and the misaligned walk ran off each table's end and ate the head of the case body after it (3 real bytes on t32, 10 on w32), so real instructions were replaced by fiction *outside* the table too. In the emitted C that fiction was **feeding a real store**: `t32!sub_40B780` printed `*(int32_t*)(edi + (ecx << 2) - 0x1C) = eax` where `eax` came from nine invented instructions including `/* unlifted: out 0xb8, eax */`, and it now reads `= *(int32_t*)(esi + (ecx << 2) - 0x1C)`, the CRT's actual unrolled copy. The y1di instrument — conditional jumps aiming outside their filed function — reads **0 before and after**, because none of this fiction happened to be a `jcc`; and no gate moved either, since gcc compiles fiction, polarity judges guards that exist and the statement-drop audit counts drops rather than inventions. The instrument that does see it is the **spurious-run census**: maximal runs of ≥4 code-pointer words anywhere in the code section, split by whether they intersect anything the disassembler files as code — 6/5/0/0 runs (t32/w32/t64/w64) with **2/2/0/0 intersecting, and 0/0/0/0 after** (peek-a-bin-7lb9).
- **…and a base that is not an address at all can still be a table base: MSVC overlaps an unbounded table's entry 0 with the instruction in front of it.** `unboundedTableExtent` requires the run to *contain* the named base, and at four sites it does not, because the base's four bytes are opcode. t32.exe, and the other three are the same shape (t32 0x40b985 base 0x40b990, w32 0x409491 base 0x4094a4, w32 0x409625 base 0x409630):

  ```text
    40b7ec  and eax, 3                          ; index is 1..3, never 0
    40b7ef  add ecx, eax
    40b7f1  jmp dword ptr [eax*4 + 0x40b804]    ; base 0x40b804
    40b7f8  jmp dword ptr [ecx*4 + 0x40b900]    ; Shape 2 — negative index, see below
    40b7ff  nop
    40b800  jmp dword ptr [ecx*4 + 0x40b884]    ; 7 bytes: 40b800..40b806
    40b807  nop                                 ; so [0x40b804] = b8 40 00 90
    40b808  dd 0x40b814, 0x40b840, 0x40b864     ; entries 1..3, real
    40b814  and edx, ecx                        ; case body 1
  ```

  Entry 0 is provably unreachable — the block is entered only when `test edi, 3` was non-zero, so `and eax, 3` cannot produce 0 — so the assembler spent its slot on an instruction rather than four bytes of padding. **A byte that belongs to a decoded instruction cannot be a table entry**, which is real evidence rather than statistical, so `overlappedTableExtent` (`functionDetect.ts`) starts the table at the first grid slot past that instruction and reports `[base + ptrSize, hi)` as the span — the overlapped slot is code and marking it data would delete the `jmp` in front of the dispatch. Five things (peek-a-bin-xqxy):
  - **The evidence arrives by deferral, not a second decode, and there was a measured choice here.** The overlapping instruction is at a *higher* address than the dispatch (0x40b800 against 0x40b7f1), so the linear `recent` window structurally cannot hold it. `TableRead.deferredBase` hands the refusal back to the sweep, which settles it when it walks onto an instruction containing the base and **drops it the moment `insn.address >= base`** — the sweep is address-monotone, so that is proof no later instruction can contain it. Cost is a handful of live entries and no extra work: no instruction-extents array (2.4 MB on a 657 KiB `.text`) and no forward Capstone probe, which would re-decode exactly what the sweep is about to decode and could then disagree with it. `MAX_PENDING_OVERLAP_BASES` is hostile-input hygiene, and dropping a pending base is the safe direction — it is today's behaviour.
  - **The instruction must end INSIDE the base slot** (`base < end <= base + ptrSize`). That is the idiom — one instruction's tail shared with entry 0 — and it is the whole claim; anything deeper has swallowed a slot whole and a "base" that far inside code is not a table base.
  - **The run is counted from the BASE, not from the span**, so `MIN_UNBOUNDED_TABLE_ENTRIES` still applies to the table's own length: the overlapped slot is a table slot (the dispatch names it) and is exactly one slot by the rule above, so four entries means three readable words. Lowering the threshold instead would weaken every *unanchored* run, which is the population that 4 was calibrated against.
  - **SHAPE 2 IS NOT A DEFECT AND MUST NOT BE "FIXED", and it is now refused three ways over.** t32 0x40b7f8 and w32 0x409498 dispatch through a **negative** index (`sub ecx, 4 / jb` leaves ecx at 0xFFFFFFFC..0xFFFFFFFF), so the words read are *below* the base and are a table already recovered and already spanned; the "base" 0x40b900 / 0x4095a0 is a case body. The caller refuses any `deferredBase` a recovered table already dispatches to — an address a `jmp [table]` reaches is code, so it is not any table's slot — which is why `overlappedTableExtent` is called exactly 4 times per 32-bit binary and never sees these. Measured with that check disabled, they are *also* refused: t32 by run-too-short (entries=1) and w32 by the overlap-extent test (its base sits inside a 6-byte `add` running to 0x4095a5). The case-target refusal is what makes it a property rather than two counts happening to disagree.
  - **The `and <index>, 3` at all four sites is corroboration from OUTSIDE the rule and is deliberately not used *here*.** It bounds the index to `[0, 3]` — four slots, exactly what the overlap plus the run finds — but it says nothing about which bytes are opcode, so on its own it would report a span covering four bytes of a real `jmp`. **The other two reasons this bullet gave — "a mask is not a `cmp`" and "using it would widen the bounded path's vocabulary at every dispatch" — have since been answered and the mask IS admitted as a bound, on `boundedCaseCount`'s side of the question only** (`peek-a-bin-padl`, see the gotcha below). What is refused here is unchanged: the span question is not the index question, and `overlappedTableExtent` still reads no mask.

  **The harm and the measurement.** 12 bytes per site were gap-filled, and the misaligned walk then ate real code past the table: t32 0x40b813 `add byte ptr [ebx], ah` swallowed the `23` of `and edx, ecx` and the next five decodes were fiction over 21 bytes of the CRT's real 3-byte unrolled copy. In the emitted C, `t32!sub_40B780` and `w32!sub_409420` each carried a region ending `eax = sub_288402B(); return eax;` — **a call to a function outside the image, followed by a return** — where the machine copies three bytes and loops. Measured at `6d5ae92` + the new audit, both sides pinned: instructions **18032 → 18025** (t32) and **16611 → 16601** (w32), x64 **byte-identical**; emitted C moved in exactly one function per 32-bit binary, read line by line against `objdump`, with three invented prelude declarations dropped per binary; `functions` 288/279/275/285 and `jumpTables` 13/0/0/11 unmoved, gcc all clean, `offsetof` 331/301/303/418 fields and 54/54/53/58 definitions **ratio 1.00 and unmoved**, polarity 0 inverted with `CHANGED 0` / `only-base 0` / `only-change 0` on all four, unrecovered values 91/21/17/72 unmoved, and **loop shapes identical** (`for` 3/5/5/3, `while` 114/107/100/101, `do/while` 78/77/76/77). One `nop` per site leaves the view and that is the pre-existing all-padding gap rule working: each is a single 0x90 that was only ever decoded because the gap ran on into the table.

  **7lb9's spurious-run census cannot see this residue** — Shape 1's run is 3 words and does not contain its base — so the gate is `corpus/wildBranches.ts`, **a filed direct branch whose target the image does not contain**, 1/0/0/1 → 0/0/0/0. That is deliberately phrased without mentioning jump tables: this fix works by marking table bytes as data, and an audit in those terms would measure its own input. It is a **lower bound**, and a loose one — it saw 1 of the 2 sites per binary, the other site's fiction happening not to decode as a branch out of the image.
- **…and what bounds a table is one rule about one register, which it took two disagreeing declarations to notice.** `readAbsoluteTable` walked back to the **first `cmp` it met**, read its immediate as the case count and stopped, never asking which register was compared; `boundedCaseCount`, forty lines away in the same file, tracked the index register but could not read a bound carried in a register. So the two paths answered the same question differently, and **each was wrong where the other was right** — the shape `sections.ts`, `ripRelative.ts`, `funcInsns.ts`, `apiLists.ts` and `stackIdiom.ts` each exist to end. There is one declaration now, `boundedCaseCount`, and it is at least as strong as either copy was. Measured at `51264fe`, both sides pinned (`peek-a-bin-padl`):
  - **The census, which corrects the bead in both directions.** Of 20/18 absolute-table dispatches on t32/w32, **9 per binary took a bound from a register the `jmp` does not index** — the bead's list, re-derived — every one an MSVC SSE memcpy/memset tail whose index is bounded by `and edx, 3` while `cmp ecx, 8` bounds the *dword count*. But **4 more per binary were bounded correctly by `readAbsoluteTable` in a way `boundedCaseCount` cannot reach** (t32 0x4046cd, 0x407c36, 0x40cd50; w32 0x40b0dc — MSVC's `push 7 / pop ecx / cmp eax, ecx`, `peek-a-bin-mk42`'s route), so *sharing `boundedCaseCount` as it stood* would have deleted four 8-case switches. Three pre-existing tests fail under a register check alone, which is how that half shows up.
  - **THE WRONG BOUND COST NOTHING OBSERVABLE, and the bead is right that this is luck.** At all 16 recovering sites the bound claimed 9 (or 5) cases and the read stopped at **4**, because the words after the table are the case bodies and `t32!0x40b900` holds `0x5E08458B`. Verified site by site: identical target lists, and the span is computed from `targets.length` so it was 16 bytes and correct too. What the bound was actually supplying is a **cap**, and the range check on each entry was doing the sizing. So this is not a measured repair of wrong output; it is the removal of an unsound reading, and the number that moves is the **exposure**: words the bound would have let the read accept past the end of the real case list, i.e. draws taken on the ~1-in-65536 chance that a case body's first word looks like a code address. **54 over 11 dispatches → 8 over 2, on each 32-bit binary.** The residual 2 are `peek-a-bin-xqxy`'s overlapped bases (t32 0x40b7f1, 0x40b985), where entry 0 is provably opcode so the read stops at `c = 0` and the slack is nominal.
  - **`and <index>, imm` is admitted as a bound of `imm + 1`, and that answers the refusal recorded above rather than ignoring it.** A mask states the index's range *exactly*, where a `cmp` states it only together with the sense of the branch below it — which the code does not read, hence `imm + 1` over-claiming by one after a `jb`. So the mask is the **stronger** of the two forms, not a weaker substitute. `overlappedTableExtent`'s refusal stands untouched: there the question is which *bytes* are opcode, which a mask cannot answer, and the scope here is narrower than either that bead or `peek-a-bin-ayhj` considered — the mask counts only for the register the dispatch actually subscripts with, and only if the register-tracking walk has not already been broken.
  - **A write of the index between the bound and the dispatch ends the search**, which `boundedCaseCount` did not do (it walked straight past `add ecx, eax`, `sub ecx, 4` and a `rep movsd` to reach a `cmp ecx, 8` five blocks back). A bound on a value the register no longer holds is not a bound — `conditionSpoiled`'s rule for a guard. `cmp`, `test` and `push` are the read-only forms; the x86 string primitives are refused explicitly because they write RCX/RSI/RDI while naming none of them, which `destReg` cannot see. Without this the change would additionally have recovered t32 0x40b800's real 8-entry table **by unsound reasoning** — right answer, walk across three writes — and that is declined.
  - **What keeps the output is that a table already read is a table: the base is the unit of identity.** The register check alone, mask included, is **refuted by measurement and reproduced here — `jumpTables` 13 → 6 (t32) and 11 → 4 (w32)** — because these tails are reached by `jmp`, so the eight addresses in front of one are not the eight instructions that ran, and the `and edx, 3` is out of linear reach at all but the first. No lookback length fixes that. But a base another dispatch has already resolved holds the same entries however it is reached, so `readAbsoluteTable` consults the sweep's `tablesByBase` before giving up — reusing the *entries*, **never a longer run than the first reading took**, which would be a length claim the second dispatch has no evidence for. First reading wins, and it is the one whose own evidence bounded the table.
  - **That reuse is also the only route by which two dispatches per 32-bit binary are read at all, and their absence was a FALSE control-flow claim.** t32 `0x40b8e7` and `0x40ba83` (w32 `0x409587`, `0x409723`) are the tail dispatch after the unrolled dword copy; with no bound in reach they got no successors, so the emitted C ran off the end of the unrolled block straight into `loc_40B925: ecx = ecx; goto loc_40B928;` — **always the 3-byte tail**, where the machine dispatches on `edx` into one of four. `armExits`' character: a statement the output makes that is not true. `jumpTables` **13/0/0/11 → 15/0/0/13**, every pre-existing target list byte-identical, x64 untouched (0 tables, and `readRvaTable` is the x64 path).
  - **Every moved row accounted for; t64 and w64 byte-identical throughout.** `compare.mjs` verdict no regression, `CHANGED 0 / only-base 0 / only-change 0` on all four, `guards audited` exactly flat at 517/574/441/497. Emitted C moved in **1 of 268 t32 functions and 5 of 266 w32**: the two memcpy/memset routines gain the four `switch (edx)` arms, and the other four w32 functions are `struct_N` renumbering (three are byte-identical after normalising the numbers) plus `w32!sub_40A420`'s 4-field partial view **completing into the 11-field shape** — the documented cross-function refinement, order-dependent through `fingerprintIndex`. `offsetof` t32 346 → **342** fields at 55/55 definitions and w32 354 → **355** at 59 → **56**: on t32 `struct_41`'s four accesses re-base onto `struct_13` (the case bodies are now reached from a merged `edi`/`esi`, so `splitStaleReads` repairs them, which is *more* correct — the base's `edi_2` was already wrong on the paths through the unrolled block), and on w32 three definitions merge for one net field gained. **Every ratio 1.00.** Also moved and all consequences of four more switch arms: `arms examined` and `truthful closures` 72 → 80 and 54 → 62 with **0 false breaks**, `case labels` 72 → 80 and 54 → 62 with **0 empty case bodies** and `lone goto` 29 → 37, `statements lifted` +12, `emitted lines scanned` +24. Flat: `functions`, `instructions`, gcc, polarity (0 inverted, 0 mismatch), `wildBranches` 0 over the same 4240/3791 denominators, loop exits, callees lost, statements dropped, unrecovered values, arity, staleReads, staleGuards, popReads, lostDefs, unencodableNames, and every loop-shape count.
  - **NOT verified.** The unified rule is also what `recoverX64RvaChain` and `recoverDenseByteTable` call, and **this corpus recovers 0 tables on t64 and w64**, so the three arms those two gained (the constant-register route, the mask, the write break) and the vocabulary they lost are **unmeasured on real x64 output** — synthetic fixtures only. `peek-a-bin-ayhj`'s Go image has **20** dense dispatches and recovers 0 of them — and it does not exercise `recoverDenseByteTable` either, which takes **0 calls** there as on all four corpus binaries (re-measured at `baa7f61`); it is deliberately not in the corpus directory and is driven separately by `npm run corpus:jumptables`. Nothing here rendered anything, and the exposure figure is a property of these four binaries, not a bound.
- **There is exactly one notion of "loop": dominance.** `cfg.ts`'s `detectLoops` delegates to `decompile/ssa.ts`'s `detectNaturalLoops` (an edge `u → v` is a back edge only when v dominates u) and repackages the result as `Loop[]`. It used to approximate back edges with BFS layers from the entry, which called the merge block of every `if`-without-`else` a loop header — ~86% of the loops reported on real binaries did not exist, and the mis-structuring **deleted guards**, turning conditional stores into unconditional ones. A diamond is immune to that mistake and a triangle is not, so hand-written fixtures never caught it; `__tests__/cfg.test.ts` and `decompile/__tests__/pipeline.test.ts` now pin both shapes. `detectLoops` is also what draws loop markers in the disassembly view (`useDisassemblyRows.ts`), so its semantics are shared with the UI.
- **`structureCFG` closes an `if` at the immediate post-dominator**, computed by `computePostDominators` (`structure.ts`) — `computeDominators` over the reversed CFG rooted at a virtual exit. The two "one arm ends in `ret`" shortcuts must not fire when that arm *is* the convergence point: in a triangle the branch target is the shared tail, so structuring it as the `then` body yields an empty body and drops the guard. A nearest-common-successor heuristic is not a substitute — for a switch it picks the default block, which is not on every path, and the code after the switch is then lost.
- **`extractCondition` returns the condition under which the jump is TAKEN.** Every entry in `regstate.ts`'s `condMap` is taken-polarity (`jg`→`>`, `jbe`→`u<=`, `js`→`< 0`), and `identifyBranches` returns the block the jcc jumps to. So the branch target is the `then` body under the *un-negated* condition, and `RegState.negate` is for the fallthrough — its own docstring says "for structuring: if-not-taken path". Getting this backwards inverted **every `if` and `while` the decompiler ever emitted** while leaving the bodies in place: valid C stating the opposite of the machine code, invisible to every stage-level test. `__tests__/pipeline.test.ts` guards it end to end now.
- **A guard is answered from the flags the Jcc *actually* reads, and only while the compare still describes them.** Two refusals in `extractCondition`, one defect class — the emitted guard naming the **right operator over the wrong operands**, which no gate could see because polarity checks the operator, gcc compiles it, and it is not `__unrecovered_N`. (a) The forward walk clears the flags on any instruction that is not `isFlagTransparent`, because it used to clear them on *nothing* — not a call, not arithmetic, not a shift — so `cmp eax, 5 / … / sub ecx, edx / jne` emitted `eax != 5` where the machine branches on `ecx - edx != 0` (`peek-a-bin-jitf`; 2 corpus occurrences, both MSVC's branchless `cmp / sbb eax,eax / inc eax / jne` size check, where the **`inc`** sets the ZF the `jne` reads). (b) A compare whose operand something overwrote before the Jcc is refused, because the block's statements are emitted **above** the `if`: `cmp eax, 5 / mov eax, edx / je` printed `eax = edx;` and then `if (eax != 5)`, reading the new EAX (`peek-a-bin-xe01`). **Both use `flagModel.ts`, not a copy of it** — `isFlagTransparent` is that module's own table exported, and the overwrite scan is `clobberedAfter`, asked by `extractCondition` of the `cmp`'s *operands*. It had a second caller, `flagResultSetter`'s condition 6, asking the identical question of an arithmetic *result* register; keeping them in one predicate is why they could not disagree, and they had disagreed since `peek-a-bin-b531` with condition 6's docstring stating the `cmp` path's bug verbatim. That caller is gone with the backward walk — a result-derived guard is an `IRBranch`, so SSA answers it by construction and `flagModel`'s `spoils` answers it forwards for the lifter. **Refusal is the whole repair**: the guard becomes `__unrecovered_N`, which is "clean is not recovered" working as intended, and it is why the cost shows up as unrecovered values rising (108/85/67/85 → 159/94/73/122 *at the time*; `peek-a-bin-92yy` has since taken the total down again, so do not read either pair as current) rather than as guard text changing — `compare.mjs` reports `CHANGED 0` and `only-change 0` on all four binaries. Gated by `corpus/staleGuards.ts`; do not weaken either refusal without reading it.
- **`push` and `pop` write no flags, they have been in `NO_FLAG_WRITE` since `b35a786`, and a guard behind an epilogue restore is refused for a DIFFERENT and CORRECT reason.** `peek-a-bin-thsj` was filed as "add `push` and `pop` to `NO_FLAG_WRITE`"; they were already there, and the census says the flag model displaces a compare owner on a `push`/`pop` **0 times on all four binaries** (the clearers are `sbb`, `bt` and `movnti`). Adjudicated and pinned at `41113c9` rather than fixed. What is actually going on is that **membership in `NO_FLAG_WRITE` and `spoils` are two questions about the same instruction, and a `pop` answers them oppositely**: it writes no flag, so the owner stands, *and* it writes its operand register, so the name the guard would be spelled with is gone. `test edi, edi / pop edi / pop esi / pop ebx / jne` (t32 `sub_40E1D8` 0x40e275, verified against `objdump -d -M intel`) keeps the `test` as owner and is then refused by `spoils`, because EDI at the Jcc holds the **restored callee-saved value** and `edi != 0` would be a test the machine does not make. Both scans already get this right — `spoils` forwards for the lifter, `clobberedAfter` for `structure.ts`'s `conditionSpoiled` — so the bead's hazard 2 was not a hazard, it was the mechanism. Five things worth carrying:
  - **The bead's symptom sentence is refuted, and by the majority of the population.** "Every Jcc whose flag owner is separated from it by a pop emits `__unrecovered_N`" is false: of the compare owners with a `push` or `pop` between the compare and the Jcc — **34/0/0/27** (t32/t64/w64/w32) — **26/0/0/20 are recovered today**, and they are recovered *because* `push`/`pop` are members. The x64 pair has 0 of the shape at all, so anything measured there is measured on nothing.
  - **The residue is 2/0/0/2 guards, and in all four the popped register IS a compare operand.** Of 54/9/6/38 spoiled compare owners the ones every one of whose spoilers is a `pop` are t32 `sub_40B780` 0x40b7bd (`cmp edi, esi / pop esi / pop edi / jne`) and `sub_40E1D8` 0x40e27a, and w32 `sub_409420` 0x40945d and `sub_40C7F8` 0x40c89a. So **0 of them are recoverable by any change to the flag tables** — the split the bead asked for has everything on the unrecoverable side.
  - **Recovering them needs the tested value materialised into a temporary before the clobber**, which `conditionSpoiled`'s docstring already named, and that mechanism is **general rather than `pop`-shaped: it was worth 104 guards corpus-wide, of which these 4 are 4%.** The spoiler histogram over all 107 spoiled compares is `mov=157 pop=15 lea=7 movsx=6 movzx=6 push=6` (re-derived at `97249dc`; an earlier count of `mov=133 movzx=6 pop=6 lea=2` was taken over a narrower stretch). Doing it `pop`-only would have spent the whole design cost on the smallest slice and left a second copy of a rule the general fix must own. **`peek-a-bin-xskz` LANDED that fix** — see the `spoiledCompareCapture` gotcha above — and all four of these sites are now recovered, `t32!sub_40E1D8` 0x40e27a as `flg_40e275_0 = edi;` above the three pops.
  - **`corpus/staleGuards.ts`'s gate FORBIDS the bead's own DONE criterion, and that is checkable.** All four target jccs are `clobbered` rows in `staleguards_<bin>.jsonl` with `emitted: null`; `named` counts *any* emitted guard at such a jcc, so emitting one takes the gate 0 → 4 red **even though the guard would be correct**. "The guard at 0x40e27a is emitted" and "`staleGuards` stays at 0 named" cannot both hold. The general fix therefore has to refine that audit — a decision to be taken *with* the audit, exactly as `branchFor`'s refusal 3 says of its own shape.
  - **DO NOT try to fix it by moving the `IRBranch` earlier in the block's statement list.** It is tempting, because `pipeline.ts` step 4b extracts branches into a `Map` before `structureCFG` so their position never reaches the page, and an earlier position *does* make `buildSSA` bind the condition to the pre-`pop` version. It still emits the wrong C: `structureCFG` emits a block's statements **above** the `if` it closes with, so the restore runs before the guard regardless of where the branch statement sat. The value has to exist as a statement, not merely be read early.
  - **Hazard 1 was already correct, and it is live rather than hypothetical.** `popf`/`popfd`/`popfq` load every flag and `pushf`/`pushfd`/`pushfq`, `pusha`/`popa` are different instructions again; none is a member, so all clobber — `baseMnemonic`'s exact match is what makes that true and a `startsWith("pop")` test would silently admit `popf`. **`pushfd` occurs twice in each 32-bit corpus binary** (t32 0x402b8b/0x403c4b, w32 0x402ddf/0x403eab — the CRT capturing an exception context). One latent asymmetry is documented and costs nothing today: `clobberedAfter` reports `push`/`pop` as writes of RSP and `spoils` does not, so the two disagree about a `cmp [esp + 4], 0 / pop edi / jne` — 4/39/34/4 compare owners name ESP/RSP at all and the scans disagree about **0** of them, with `conditionSpoiled` as the backstop for the block-local case either way.
- **…and refusing a spoiled compare was never the only sound answer: MATERIALISE the compared values at the compare, and the guard can be recovered.** `conditionSpoiled` refuses because the block's statements are emitted *above* the `if`, so `cmp eax, 5 / mov eax, edx / je` would print `eax = edx; if (eax != 5)` — the right operator over the wrong operands. Its own docstring named the better answer, and `spoiledCompareCapture` (`lifter.ts`) is it: one statement per non-constant operand at the compare's own program point, `flg_<compare address>_<operand index> = <parsed operand>`, and the `IRBranch`'s condition built over those. The clobber then runs between the capture and the guard and cannot reach either. **104 guards recovered corpus-wide** and unrecovered values **91/21/17/72 → 39/12/11/35** (`peek-a-bin-xskz`, measured at `97249dc`). Six things, and the first three are judgements that cost something:
  - **The destination is an `IRVar`, and a deletable pseudo-*register* was tried first, measured, and REJECTED.** `flg_x = eax` is a copy by `isCopyStmt`'s reckoning, so `copyPropagation` deletes it, rebinds the guard to the pre-clobber SSA version, and `splitStaleReads` repairs that as it repairs every other stale read — free where it recovers nothing, and a natural register name on the page where it does (`matchedStackSlots`' `stk_<addr>` argument, `peek-a-bin-6f3v`). It works. It was refused for two reasons that are one reason: **it leaves the class ungateable**, because after copy propagation a recovered `eax == 5` and a defective `edx == 5` are both bare register names and no text rule separates them; and **its correctness becomes a chain of passes** rather than a property — in the shape where DCE deletes the clobber outright the guard is a bare `eax` that is right *for that reason alone*. An `IRVar` is written once, at the compare, by a statement `copyPropagation`, `foldBlock`'s single-use inlining and `deadCodeElimination` all leave alone by construction (each requires a register destination). The price is 114 statements and 114 temporaries over the four binaries, including the 27 sites where a register would have folded away. That is what the gate costs, and it is worth it.
  - **The signal to `structure.ts` is the compare's ADDRESS, not a boolean, and the two walks must agree.** `IRBranch.capturedAt` carries it; `extractCondition` bypasses `conditionSpoiled` only when `capturedAt === flagSetterAddr`, i.e. when the instruction *its own* forward walk found to have set the flags is the one the lifter captured at. A disagreement leaves the refusal standing. And the bypass additionally requires a usable IR condition to fall through to, or it would hand back `cond` — the raw reading the refusal exists to suppress. Asking `conditionSpoiled` of the IR condition instead is what `peek-a-bin-xe01` records as defeating it outright, so the signal has to come from the lifter.
  - **`foldBlock`'s refusal to inline into a branch stops applying, and that is fine but not free.** A definition whose only reader was the guard was refused; its only reader is now a *capture*, an ordinary assign, so it gets inlined — `and edi, 0xf / and esi, 0xf / cmp edi, esi` becomes `flg_40b7b9_0 = edi & 0xF; flg_40b7b9_1 = esi & 0xF`, one statement fewer than the capture count on t32. The two reasons that refusal exists are both unaffected: `blockLiveOut` still stops a value that escapes the block, and `structureCFG` matches loop shapes on a block's *final* statement, which a capture never is. Empirically confirmed — **loop shapes identical on all four binaries**.
  - **The scope is a BLOCK-LOCAL compare owner — and `peek-a-bin-zylv` has since taken the cross-block population without extending it.** `blockFlagOwner` will answer from a sole predecessor, and a capture *placed* for that owner would have to go in the predecessor's statement list, which `liftBlock` does not have. It does not have to be placed: measured at `97249dc` and again at `16f1633`, all 2/0/0/1 cross-block spoiled owners (against 52/9/6/37 block-local, 3 of 107) are spoiled on the **predecessor's** side of the edge, and in every one the predecessor's own trailing jcc reads the *same* compare — so the predecessor is itself a block-local site and the capture is already on the page. `reusablePredecessorCapture` looks it up; see the gotcha. A `result` or `bittest` owner is still untouched — its value is the instruction's own destination, and capturing it would mean reading a destination the instruction has not written yet.
  - **The dominant shape is a compare over MEMORY refused for want of alias analysis** — 74 `mem/imm` plus 3 `mem/reg` of 104 at `97249dc`, against 25 `reg/reg` and 2 `reg/imm`. `test byte ptr [ebp - 0x408], 0x20 / mov dword ptr [ebp - 0x10], edi / je` is refused because `clobberedAfter` reports a memory write and the condition holds a deref, with no attempt to prove the two slots differ. Materialising the load needs no alias reasoning at all, which is why this is the right recovery rather than a weakening of `spoils`.
  - **Five sites were read by hand against `objdump`, and two of them settle a constant.** t64 `0x140005752` is `mov ecx,0x30 / cmp eax,ecx / mov ecx,0x58 / jne`: the fix emits `flg_140005752_1 = 0x30` and the negative control emits `eax_6 == 0x58`, the value the *spoiler* put there — a provably wrong test in compiling C, and the clearest evidence the capture is taken at the right point. t32 `0x40e27a` is `peek-a-bin-thsj`'s own site (`test edi, edi / pop edi / pop esi / pop ebx / jne`), recovered here as `flg_40e275_0 = edi;` **above** the pops; thsj's adjudication said this needed the general fix, and it did.
  - **…and the emitter must DECLARE the capture, which it did not until `peek-a-bin-x54q`.** A capture is an `IRVar` the emitter itself invents and writes, so the emitted C named an identifier nothing in it declares — **114 of them at `f169c00`** (53/14/9/38 on t32/t64/w64/w32), each in a function where a promoted frame slot beside it *was* declared. **This is not the documented decision to leave a *register* undeclared** (`structPointer`: nothing the reader supplies for a register may make C scale arithmetic on it, and an entry value really is conceptually a parameter): a capture is neither a register nor an incoming value, and the other locals the pipeline invents (`var_N` from `promoteVars`) are declared. Six things, and the first is the reason nobody had noticed:
    - **No gate could see it, and one of them was actively hiding it.** `corpus/emitAudits.ts`' `preludeFor` answers gcc's `'flg_…' undeclared` by manufacturing `long flg_…;`, so **gcc read 1127/1127 clean over C the harness had completed rather than over the C the emitter wrote** — the same structural blindness the file already records for a wrong register *name*. So the instrument is the **count of invented prelude declarations** (the `long <name>;` lines in `artifacts/<label>/cc/<bin>/*.c`), the one `peek-a-bin-k8i` used: **1579/2520/2337/1520 → 1526/2506/2328/1482**, down by exactly the 53/14/9/38 captures, with **0 `flg_` prelude declarations left** — which is also the proof that no capture is read without its definition being emitted.
    - **The `long` was the wrong WIDTH for every one of the 114, not merely absent.** 64 are 4 bytes, 42 are 1 and 8 are 2, and **not one is 8**, so the program gcc compiled was not the one the emitter wrote. Worse where the guard is unsigned: `cOp` spells `u<` as a bare `<`, so nothing in the expression says which comparison the machine makes and the declaration is the *only* thing that decides it — under `long` two `jb` guards per run compiled as signed 64-bit comparisons.
    - **The type is `IRVar.size` via `sizeToType`, preferring type inference only where it names the SAME width.** `IRVar.size` is what `capturedWidth` took from the operand `parseOperand` read out of the machine text, and it is already what `operandWidth` reads for that same variable when it decides a cast — so a declaration derived from anything else can disagree with the casts the emitter has already emitted around it. Inference is preferred where it speaks, exactly as `promoteVars` prefers it for a local, and that is what makes the 2 unsigned guards compile unsigned. The width test is a **bound, not a saving**: `typeInfer.ts` types a comparison operand `{ int, size: 4 }` with the width hard-coded, so for a 1- or 2-byte capture its spelling would claim a width the operand does not have; and it refuses every non-integer spelling, which is what keeps a declaration from turning `emitsAsPointer` true and rescaling arithmetic the body already emits. Measured: of the 114, inference has an opinion about **10** and all 10 agree in width, so it refuses nothing today.
    - **`isCapturedOperandName` is the scope, and "every undeclared `IRVar` destination" is NOT this set.** `ssadestroy.ts`'s `splitStaleReads` parks a pre-clobber value in an `IRVar` too — **2114 undeclared (function, name) pairs against these 114** — and those are spelled as registers deliberately, are compared as names by `corpus/staleReads.ts`, and are covered by the register decision. Declaring them is a much larger change with an emitted-C effect of its own (a pointer-typed one flips `emitsAsPointer`). So the predicate lives beside `capturedOperandName` in `lifter.ts`, one declaration read in both directions; `corpus/staleGuards.ts` still rebuilds the spelling itself, because an audit that imported it would be measuring its own input.
    - **Destinations only, deduped BY NAME — both bounds rather than savings.** A capture is built per *block* while a declaration is per *function*, and `structureCFG` can emit one block more than once (the switch-arm and leftover passes both emit), so two declarations of one name would not be valid C; measured, 0 of the 114 is emitted twice. And a capture *read* where its definition was not emitted is a lost definition, so declaring it would state that the function computes a value it never assigns — it stays undeclared and keeps showing up in the prelude, which is the honest signal; measured, 0 of 114. Both are pinned by tests in `emit.test.ts` for exactly that reason, and all five rules are negative-controlled.
    - **`collectCapturedOperands` is a NINTH compiler-caught `IRStmt` dispatch, `emit.ts`'s fourth**, measured with the throwaway-kind probe this file describes; the stamped table above says 8 and was taken at `f685b6d`, so it needs re-measuring rather than trusting. Measured at `f169c00`: emitted text **+57/+14/+9/+42** lines (114 declarations plus 8 new separator blank lines), 21/6/5/19 functions changed and **every diff exactly the added declarations**, `compare.mjs` verdict "no regression" with `CHANGED 0` / `only-base 0` / `only-change 0`, gcc 1127/1127, `offsetof` ratio 1.00 unmoved, polarity 0 inverted and 0 mismatch with the audited set unmoved, unrecovered values unmoved at 39/12/11/35, arity/staleReads/staleGuards/popReads/lostDefs/armExits/wildBranches/unencodableNames all flat, and loop shapes identical.

- **…and the cross-block half of that needed no new placement at all: the capture was already on the page.** A spoiled compare owned by a *predecessor* stayed refused because `liftBlock` returns one block's statements, and the standing plan was a `pipeline.ts` pass of its own appending into the predecessor through `pushBeforeTerminator`, `peek-a-bin-6ilz`-style. **The population refutes the premise.** Measured at `16f1633` (identical to `97249dc`): all **2/0/0/1** cross-block spoiled compare owners — against 52/9/6/37 block-local, which is exactly `staleGuards`' independently-counted `clobbered` shape count — are spoiled on the **predecessor's** side of the edge, have **exactly one** predecessor, and in every case the predecessor's *own* trailing jcc reads the same compare. So the predecessor is itself a block-local spoiled site, `spoiledCompareCapture` already ran there, and the emitted C read `flg_404957_0 = var_40C; … if (flg_404957_0 >= 0) { if (__unrecovered_3 /* jne */) {` — the admitted guard **one line below the value it needed**. `reusablePredecessorCapture` (`lifter.ts`) is a *lookup*, not a placement: no new pass, no `pushBeforeTerminator`, no statement emitted, and none of `6ilz`'s four refusals transfers, because nothing is defined early. Five things (`peek-a-bin-zylv`):
  - **Re-asking `spoiledCompareCapture` of the predecessor with no `flagPred` is provably its own answer, not an approximation.** A non-null answer means `flagOwnerBefore` over the predecessor's own instructions found a compare — and `flagPredecessor`'s first test is that a block's own scan reports `{none, no-owner}`, so it returns undefined for exactly that block. The predecessor's real lift therefore saw the same stream and emitted the same statements. A null answer agrees the same way: a predecessor with no local owner takes the `fromPredecessor` path and builds nothing.
  - **The names are the whole channel and no statement is emitted.** `IRVar` is a leaf for `renameVariables`, so a read binds across the edge with no versioning and no phi; a *second* assignment in the reading block would sit **below** the clobber and be the defect this recovers from. That the definition is *emitted* above the read is dominance plus `structureCFG`'s walk rather than something the lifter can assert — the instrument is `peek-a-bin-x54q`'s count of invented `long flg_…;` prelude declarations, **0 before and 0 after**, with the totals unmoved at 1526/2506/2328/1482.
  - **THE CORPUS CANNOT SEPARATE THIS FROM A WRONG FIX, AND THE TESTS CAN.** All three real sites are spoiled by stores to *other* frame slots — `spoils` refuses on any store, with no alias analysis — so simply bypassing the refusal and reading the raw operands recovers **the same 3 guards** with the same unrecovered counts, and every gate stays flat; measured, its only diff from the fix is `var_40C != 0` for `flg_404957_0 != 0`, which is also correct text here. The discriminator is `pipeline.test.ts`, whose fixture stores over the compared slot itself: the raw variant fails **three** tests, emitting `var_18 == 0` below `var_18 = edx;`, `edx == 0x53` for `cmp eax, 0x53 / mov eax, edx / je` (the register the spoiler *read*, `peek-a-bin-xe01`'s signature) and a guard where no capture exists. The test that pinned the old refusal states the new rule instead, as method requires.
  - **Two refusals, and one of them is provably unreachable today.** The capture must be at the compare this jump reads (both walks are forward and last-writer-wins over the same stream, so asking makes it a property). And the reading block must have **exactly one** predecessor: since `peek-a-bin-xdxt` a unanimous *several* may answer for the flags, and each holds its own compare at its own address, so a capture would exist on one edge only. That arm cannot currently fire — `unanimousCompare` refuses a spoiled compare outright (its refusal 3), so a multi-predecessor block never reaches `branchFor` with a spoiled owner — and it is kept as a bound on the rule rather than a saving, exactly as `pop esp` and the `rip`/`rsp` refusals are. The class it names, a lone jcc whose several unanimous predecessors are all *spoiled*, has 0 occurrences: `peek-a-bin-xdxt`'s census found a spellable, i.e. unspoiled, compare on both edges of all 14 such blocks.
  - **What stays refused is the honest half.** A cross-block owner spoiled on the *reading* side leaves the predecessor's compare intact, so the predecessor had no reason to take a capture and there is nothing to read; likewise a predecessor ending in `jmp` or `ret`, which reads no flags and answers no guard of its own. Both are pinned by tests and both have **0** corpus occurrences — that is the case the new placement would be for, and it has no population to justify it. Measured at base `16f1633`, both sides pinned: unrecovered values **38/12/11/34 → 36/12/11/33**, token occurrences 76/24/22/68 → 72/24/22/66, 2 of 288 and 1 of 285 functions changed, **t64/w64 byte-identical**, `compare.mjs` `CHANGED 0` / `only-base 0` / **`only-change 0`** on all four with the verdict "no regression". Every gate flat — gcc 1127/1127, `offsetof` ratio 1.00 with fields and definitions unmoved, polarity 0 inverted and 0 mismatch with the audited set **unmoved** at 517/574/497/441 and `guards w/o single compare` unmoved, 0 throws, 0 statements dropped, 0 callees lost, 0 loops short, arity exact/under/over unmoved, staleReads 0/0, staleGuards **0 named** with `shapes` unmoved at 53/9/6/38, popReads 0, lostDefs 0, armExits 0, wildBranches 0, unencodableNames 0 — and **loop shapes identical**: `for` 6/6/6/6, `while` 111/106/99/98, `do/while` 78/77/76/77, `if` 1987/1938/1719/1759. The three recovered guards do **not** enter the polarity audit, and that is expected rather than a shortfall: each guards a block whose first statement is itself a nested `if`, which carries no line-map entry, so there is no `bodyAddr` to anchor. The oracle is hand-reading — all three read against `objdump -d -M intel`, all three MSVC's `_output` `cmp [ebp-X], 0 / <stores> / jge L` with `L: jne M`, and in each the `!= 0` body is the taken target's `cmp [ebp-X], eax / jle` clamp.

- **`test` clears OF and CF; it does NOT clear SF. Which is why `getCondition`'s `test` arm answers strictly more Jcc forms than its `result` arm.** These two arms look parallel and are not, and conflating them costs recovery in one direction or correctness in the other. For a **result** owner (`dec ecx`, `sub eax, ecx`) only ZF and SF are functions of the result — CF and OF depend on the operands and on which arithmetic ran — so only `je`/`jne`/`js`/`jns` are answerable and everything else must stay unknown. For a **`test`** owner every flag a Jcc can read is either a function of `a & b` or a known constant, because `test` pins OF and CF to 0 (Intel SDM: "The OF and CF flags are set to 0. The SF, ZF, and PF flags are set according to the result"). So `jle` — `ZF=1 or SF≠OF` — collapses to `(a & b) <= 0`, and `jg`, `jl`, `jge`, `ja` and `jbe` follow the same way. The arm answered only the ZF/SF forms until `peek-a-bin-92yy`, which recovered **122 unrecovered values corpus-wide** (70 `jle`, 50 `jg`, 2 `jl`) with 0 pre-existing guards changed. **The suite had asserted the defect as the rule** — `it("returns unknown for a jcc with no meaning after test")` with the comment "jle depends on SF/OF, which `test` clears" — and its own neighbour refuted it, since `js` reads SF alone and was already answered. Two things not to re-try: `jb`/`jae` and `jo`/`jno` read *only* a flag `test` clears, so they are constants rather than conditions, and emitting `if (1)` is a control-flow claim that `structureCFG` may act on and no gate models (`peek-a-bin-x72e`, **6 real sites — 4 on t32 and 2 on w32, and 0 on BOTH x64 binaries** — re-measured at `075c031` two ways, adjacent and over a flag-transparent window, both giving the same 4/0/0/2; the bead and this file both said 12, which is wrong under either reading. MSVC's 64-bit negate idiom, which on a PE32 image is synthesised from 32-bit registers, hence the x64 zero. The flag owner there is correctly identified and the shape is **not** evidence of misattribution); and `jp`/`jnp` read PF, which is a real function of the result with no cheap spelling.
- **A Jcc alone in its block is answered from its single predecessor, and the terminator skip is the whole mechanism.** `blockFlagOwner` read only `block.insns`, so for a block holding nothing but the Jcc the walk ran zero iterations, reported `{kind: "none", reason: "no-owner"}`, and the guard emitted as `__unrecovered_N` — 88 blocks across the corpus, of which **69 are recoverable** (`peek-a-bin-suql`). `flagScanStream` (`flagModel.ts`) prepends the predecessor's instructions when, and only when, the block's own scan finds no flag writer at all; `flagPredecessor` supplies it (it was `solePredecessor` until `peek-a-bin-xdxt` generalised it — see the next gotcha), and both consumers — `lifter.ts`'s `branchFor` and `structure.ts`'s `extractCondition` — read the one stream, because two would drift about which edge and which terminator. Four things are load-bearing:
  - **The predecessor's terminator must be skipped explicitly.** `jmp`, `ret` and every Jcc are absent from `NO_FLAG_WRITE` — correctly, that set is narrow on purpose — so `flagEffect` classes them `{kind: "clobber", why: "unrecognised"}` and a walk that reads one clears the very owner it came for. Measured both ways: without the skip the change recovers **0** guards and the emitted C is byte-identical to base on all four binaries. It is not an optimisation, it is the difference between 0 and 69.
  - **Either edge counts.** A Jcc writes no flags, so a predecessor's exit flags reach its successor on the taken edge exactly as on the fallthrough; the predecessor's condition being true rather than false on that path is a fact about *values*. Restricting to the fallthrough leaves 17 of the 69 unclaimed, and on the x64 pair the taken edge is the *majority* (6 of 13 on t64, 4 of 9 on w64).
  - **The walk continues *through* the block rather than stopping at the boundary**, so `spoils` sets `spoiled` for a clobber on either side of the edge with no second grammar. Across the edge a **compare** owner is therefore filtered on `canSpellCondition`, where block-locally it deliberately is not (`structure.ts` re-asks that of the machine text); and refusal 3 (`blockHasCompare`) is asked of the *owner's* block, since keeping the compare and result paths disjoint is a property of the block the flags were set in.
  - **The condition cannot come from the reading block's `RegState`** — that state is what walking *this* block left behind and this block never executed the compare. It is re-read from the owning instruction through `setFlagsFromCompare`, the same call `extractCondition` makes, so the two readings cannot disagree.

  The 19 refusals are each an already-correct mechanism: 12 have more than one predecessor (`peek-a-bin-xdxt`, which has since taken *2* more of that class by unanimity and left those 12 refused — see the next gotcha), 4 have a predecessor whose owner is cleared by `sbb`'s carry-in, 3 have a spoiled compare. **Recovery reaches the page for only 51 of the 69**, and the gap is a separate defect rather than a shortfall here: 18 of the blocks are in regions `structureCFG` never emits at all (`peek-a-bin-3zji`).
- **…and "one predecessor" was the special case, not the rule: what makes a block's entry flags knowable is that every way in leaves them saying the same thing.** `solePredecessor` refused any block with two predecessors, on the correct reasoning that the flags — and so the condition — differ per incoming edge. Correct for most of that population and *false* for the part of it where both edges make the identical test: MSVC's `_stricmp` tail joins `cmp ah, al / jne` and `xor ecx, ecx / cmp ah, al / je` into one `mov ecx, -1 / jb` block, so the `jb` reads `ah u< al` however it was reached and one block-local `if` states the machine exactly. `flagPredecessor` (the renamed `solePredecessor`) now answers with any predecessor when **all** of them are unanimous, via `unanimousCompare`, and the single-predecessor arm is the degenerate case of the same sentence. Five things (`peek-a-bin-xdxt`):
  - **It is a UNANIMITY test and never a merge**, which is what keeps it out of the per-edge-condition business the surrounding machinery cannot do. Census re-derived at `f169c00`: of 35/18/13/24 lone-Jcc blocks (t32/t64/w64/w32) over 2285/2219/1966/2019 jcc blocks, 3/5/4/2 = **14** have more than one predecessor — every one of them exactly two, and every one with a spellable compare on both edges — of which **2 agree** (one per 32-bit binary, the `_stricmp` tail) and **12 do not**. The bead's figure of 12 was that differ count, measured before `peek-a-bin-xskz`; the 2 agreeing blocks were never in it.
  - **The 12 are refused on evidence and all three schemes for them were costed.** They are four shapes: `test rbx,rbx` against `test rbp,rbp` (2 sites, genuinely unrelated); `test dword ptr [rbx+0x18], 0x2000` against `test byte ptr [r13+rax+8], 4` (4 sites, unrelated); `cmp byte ptr [rbp-0x68], r11b` against `cmp …, sil` (3 sites, same place, different right-hand side); and `cmp eax, -1` against `cmp rax, -1` (2 sites, same test at different widths, so merging needs a width decision). Each needs a boolean or a value **materialised in each predecessor** and a phi over it — `liftBlock` returns one block's statements and cannot place one, so it would be a new `pipeline.ts` step, and the boolean-phi form states strictly *less* at the jcc than the machine text does (`if (cond_1)` is not a test the reader can check). Duplicating the block per predecessor instead was refused as changing the CFG the structurer sees at a *merge* point, which is where loop shape is decided and no gate models it. 12 guards is not worth either; refusing them is what the code now does explicitly.
