  - **The agreement test is compared over Capstone's own operand TEXT, with two refusals that make text equality exact rather than approximate.** No `rip` — `parseOperand` resolves a rip-relative operand against the instruction's own address, so identical text at two addresses is two different expressions — and no stack pointer, because `spoils` and `clobberedAfter` read `push`/`pop` differently about RSP and `structure.ts`'s `conditionSpoiled` scan is asked of the *returned* predecessor only. Both have 0 corpus occurrences and are there so the claim is sound by construction. A `result` or `bittest` owner is refused for the same reason: its condition is a function of the destination too, so operand text would not settle it (also 0 occurrences).
  - **Every edge gets the forward `spoils` scan, which is why one `conditionSpoiled` call still suffices.** The owner is read over `[predecessor's tail, ...the block's own instructions]` per edge — `flagScanStream`'s rule, from the same declaration — so a predecessor that overwrote what names its compare never reaches unanimity, and the returned predecessor is representative of the others in that respect as well as in the condition.
  - **THE DECISIVE MEASUREMENT IS THE WRONG VERSION, NOT THE ABSENT ONE.** Drop only the agreement test and answer from the first predecessor: **12 more guards are recovered — exactly the 12 differ sites — and `npm run corpus` exits 0 with VERDICT "no regression"**, polarity 0 inverted and 0 mismatch over 519/577/499/442 audited all correct, gcc 1127/1127, offsetof ratio 1.00, staleGuards named 0, every other gate flat. 8 of those 12 are *anchored and passed* by the polarity auditor, because the operator is right and only the operands are wrong on one path — and `staleGuards`' population is block-local, so a cross-edge error is not in its denominator (`peek-a-bin-0xe2`). Hand-read: `t64!sub_140002A2C` emits `if (rbx_2 == 0) goto loc_140002C5B;` at 0x140002afd while the edge from 0x140002c19 tests RBP. **So nothing in the corpus suite protects these 12 refusals; only the code and `pipeline.test.ts`/`flagModel.test.ts` do**, and three of those tests fail under that variant.

    Measured at base `f169c00`, both sides pinned: **2 guards recovered**, t32 0x40E696 and w32 0x40CDB6, both `jb` after `cmp ah, al` emitted as `ah >= al` on the fallthrough arm, both anchor **A** and `OK`. Unrecovered values **39/12/11/35 → 38/12/11/34**, token occurrences 78/24/22/70 → 76/24/22/68; polarity audited 516 → 517 and 440 → 441, correct 517/517 and 441/441, `CHANGED 0` / `only-base 0` / `only-change 1` on each 32-bit binary; **t64 and w64 byte-identical** (an x86-only idiom), and exactly one function of emitted C changed per 32-bit binary. Every gate flat — gcc 288/279/275/285 clean, offsetof ratio 1.00 with fields and definitions unmoved, 0 inverted, 0 mismatch, 0 throws, 0 statements dropped, 0 callees lost, 0 loops short, arity exact/under/over unmoved, staleReads 0/0, staleGuards 0 named with `shapes` unmoved, popReads 0, lostDefs 0, armExits 0, wildBranches 0, unencodableNames 0 — and **loop shapes identical**: `for` 6/6/6/6, `while` 111/106/99/98, `do/while` 78/77/76/77, `if` 1987/1938/1719/1759. With `unanimousCompare` disabled the emitted C is **byte-identical to base on all 1127 functions**, which is what proves the rename and the early return for a block that already owns its flags changed nothing.
    **Stated limitation**: the two recovered guards read `ah` and `al`, which `canonReg` maps to one register, and in these two functions the emitted C never assigns `ah` — a pre-existing sub-register aliasing defect shared with the `if (ah != al)` guard two lines above, not something this introduces.
- **A memory destination is spellable, and refusing it was a spelling limit that read as a dataflow fact.** `dec dword ptr [ebp + 0x10] / je` owns its Jcc's flags like any other `RESULT_OWNERS` form, and the value tested is the *destination read after the instruction ran* — which for memory is a deref, exactly as it is a register read for `dec ecx`. It emitted `__unrecovered_N` anyway, for two mechanical reasons in different files: `flagModel.ts` published only `destReg`, which is null for memory, so `canSpellCondition` answered `destReg !== null` and refused; and `branchFor` then built the condition with `irReg(destText, regSize(destText))`, which cannot express `dword ptr [ebp + 0x10]` — and would have been *worse than a refusal*, since `regSize` falls back to 4 for any unrecognised name. The bead on file had recorded the cause as dataflow ("the result is in memory; naming it would need a load that is not in the IR") and that was **wrong**: `liftBlock` lifts the form to a `store`, `promoteVars` names the slot, and t32 0x4020EE printed `arg_2--;` on the line directly above `if (!!__unrecovered_1 …)`. The value was already on the page. Four things about the fix (`peek-a-bin-ie0j`):
  - **`destForm: "reg" | "mem" | "none"` is what spellability is asked of, and `destReg` stays for `spoils`.** They answer different questions — "which register do I watch for an overwrite" has the answer *none* for memory, which is not the same claim as "nothing can name this". Conflating them is what refused every row. The `"none"` arm keeps the relaxation from being "anything that is not a register" and is pinned by a test, since no well-formed x86 result owner reaches it.
  - **The ordering is the whole soundness argument.** These are read-modify-write guards, so the `if` must read the location *after* the store — which holds because `structureCFG` emits a block's statements above the `if` it closes with, the same property the compare arm's `conditionSpoiled` depends on read the other way round. `pipeline.test.ts` asserts the line order directly rather than assuming it; no unit test of `flagModel` or `branchFor` can see it.
  - **Refusal is on ANY store, with no attempt to prove the addresses alias** — that is `spoils`' entire alias analysis. It also refuses across a `push`, which `writesMemory` deliberately exempts because a `push` only *reads* its operand, and that reading is right for a compare over memory and wrong for a memory result: `push` writes `[rsp - N]`. Zero corpus occurrences, so it costs nothing measurable; it is there so the claim is sound by construction rather than by that measurement, and it is negative-controlled.
  - **Admitting memory admits no new Jcc forms and does not touch the compare/result split.** `getCondition`'s `result` arm still answers only ZF/SF, so a `jae` after `dec [ecx+4]` stays unrecovered, and `branchFor`'s refusal 3 (`blockHasCompare`) is unchanged — `corpus/staleGuards.ts` reads any condition emitted at such a jcc as the superseded one. Measured at base `5c5dab9`: 14/5/5/14 guards recovered (t32/t64/w64/w32) out of a population of 15/5/5/15 memory-destination owners, the odd one out being an `and byte ptr [ebp - 3]` a later store spoils on each x86 binary. Unrecovered values 108/45/35/88 → 94/40/30/74, token occurrences 216/90/70/176 → 188/80/60/148. `compare.mjs` `CHANGED 0` and `only-base 0` on all four — every guard is *new*, none was rewritten — with anchor A unmoved at 499/563/490/430 all correct and the new rows landing on A2 (+3 each, all correct) and B (+3 on the x86 pair). `if`/`while`/`for`/`do-while` identical, gcc 1127/1127, offsetof ratio 1.00, 0 inverted, 0 statements dropped, 0 callees lost, arity/staleReads/staleGuards/popReads/lostDefs all flat. **`staleGuards` is a control here, not a detector**: both its mechanisms require a `cmp`/`test` in the block, so a result-owner guard is not in its population at all — the oracle for these rows is polarity's A2/B verdict plus hand-reading against `objdump`, and all four shapes were read (`dec [ebp+off]`, `dec [reg+off]`, `add [esi+4], -2`, and the `do { … } while (arg_0 != 0)` at t32 0x405974 that the reader was previously told nothing about).
  - **Two functions per x64 binary change with no guard in them**, and that is the cross-function struct refinement working: a signed `js` guard over `[rdx + 8]` makes `inferTypes` read the field as `int32_t` where the base had `uint32_t`, and `StructRegistry` shares the definition. Layouts unmoved at 297/297 and 300/300 fields — it is the CRT's `--stream->_cnt < 0`, which really is signed.
- **A `lock` prefix changes atomicity and the bus. It changes no value and no flag, so it must not change how anything is classified.** `flagEffect` returned `{clobber, "locked"}` for **any** `lock`-prefixed instruction before the base mnemonic was looked at, and `liftBlock` dispatched on the prefixed text — so `lock dec dword ptr [rbx] / jne`, the textbook atomic refcount release, printed `/* unlifted: lock dec dword ptr [rbx] */;` above `if (!!__unrecovered_1 /* jne */)` with the free-it branch guarded by nothing the reader can check. Both halves were the same mistake in two files: the prefix is not a fact about the flags, and it is not a fact about the values either. Dropping the blanket leaves each locked form in the class its own mnemonic earns — `lock dec`/`inc`/`add`/`or` are ordinary memory-destination result owners, spellable since `peek-a-bin-ie0j`; `lock cmpxchg` reaches `"unrecognised"`, `lock xadd` `"carry-in"`, `lock bts` `"partial-write"` — and `FlagClobberReason` loses `"locked"` because nothing produces it any more. Five things (`peek-a-bin-3qrl`):
  - **The lift has to come first, and the negative control is the whole argument.** With `flagModel.ts` fixed and `liftBlock` still dispatching on `"lock dec"`, the emitted C is `/* unlifted: lock dec dword ptr [rbx] */; if (*(int32_t*)(rbx) != 0)` — a guard naming a location the block never stores to, so it tests the refcount on the way *in*, one release early, in C that compiles. That is the defect class this repo keeps finding (the `eflags` proxy, `peek-a-bin-c33` stage 2b), and it is why the bead's ordering warning is right: lift the instruction, then the owner becomes spellable by the ordinary path. Reverting the two halves separately fails disjoint sets of `pipeline.test.ts` cases.
  - **Only `lock` is stripped, and only for the dispatch key.** `withoutLockPrefix` (`flagModel.ts`) is the one declaration, so `flagEffect` and `liftBlock` cannot disagree about what the base is. `rep` must survive it — Capstone spells the string primitives sometimes into the mnemonic and sometimes into `opStr`, `liftBlock` has a case for both, and stripping prefixes generally routes one spelling into plain `movsd`. The `raw` fallbacks keep the **verbatim** mnemonic (`rawMn`), or `__asm { cmpxchg … }` would drop the one word telling the reader the exchange was atomic.
  - **Everything the lifter has no handler for is still refused, and gets there by itself.** `cmpxchg` and `xadd` are not in `ARITH_OPS` and have no case, so `lock cmpxchg` and `lock xadd` land on `raw` exactly as before — no list of "safe locked forms" is needed or wanted, and inventing a reading for a compare-exchange is the harm this avoids.
  - **Atomicity is lost from the page, deliberately.** Nothing in this IR expresses it, `xchg` with a memory operand is implicitly locked on x86 and has been lifted as a plain swap since long before this, and a comment naming the instruction beside an unrecovered guard states strictly *less* than the decrement plus the test. Say so rather than implying the C is a faithful concurrency model.
  - **Measured at base `d514274`, both sides pinned.** The population is exactly 4 `lock dec [mem] / jne` per x64 binary and 0 on the PE32 pair (adjudicated against `objdump`: the `lock inc`/`lock add` sites all have a `test`/`cmp` between them and their Jcc, so a compare already owned those flags, and `lock or byte ptr [rsp], 0` is a fence followed by `jmp`). Unrecovered values **40 → 36 on t64 and 30 → 26 on w64**, t32/w32 unmoved at 94/74 and **byte-identical**; token occurrences 80 → 72 and 60 → 52; polarity audited 563 → 566 and 490 → 493, **all correct, 0 inverted**; 34 of 279 and 29 of 275 functions changed. `compare.mjs` `only-base 0` — no guard was lost — with `only-change 3` per binary and 2 `CHANGED` rows that are both benign: one guard's *text is byte-identical* and its anchor moved A2 → **A**, the gated one, and one is a `struct_26` → `struct_28` renumber. The one substantive cost is +23 lifted statements per x64 binary and the struct evidence they carry: `offsetof` 297 → 301 fields / 53 → 54 definitions on t64 and 300 → 303 / 52 → 53 on w64, **ratio 1.00 on both**, i.e. every new layout is verified by the compiled-and-run program. gcc 1127/1127, `if`/`while`/`for`/`do-while` identical on all four, statements dropped 0, callees lost 0, arity exact/under/over unmoved, staleGuards 0 named with `shapes` unmoved, staleReads 0/0, popReads 7/0/0/6, lostDefs 0, armExits 0 — flat. **The oracle for the four new guards is polarity's verdict plus hand-reading**: `t64!sub_140005B70` 0x140005bcb reads `lock dec DWORD PTR [rbx] / jne 0x140005be3` and now emits `*(int32_t*)(rbx) -= 1; if (*(int32_t*)(rbx) == 0) { … }`, with the skipped block being the `cmp rcx, 0x140014550 / je / call` a refcount release ends in.
- **…and a locked read-modify-write with NO value effect is a memory barrier, not a store.** `lock or byte ptr [rsp], 0` is the classic full fence, and MSVC emits it immediately after a non-temporal `movnti` store loop — where all six corpus occurrences sit (t64 0x140007F80 / 0x14000C5CA / 0x14000C76A and the same three in w64, `f0 80 0c 24 00`, each followed by `jmp`). 3qrl's base-mnemonic dispatch sent it to the `or` handler like any other locked RMW, so it lifted to `*(rsp) = *(rsp) | 0`, `foldExpr` folded `x | 0` to `x` and `promoteVars` named `[rsp+0]` a frame slot: the page got `var_0 = var_0;` and a `uint8_t var_0;` above it that nothing reads, and the fence vanished. That is strictly **less** information than the `/* unlifted: … */` of before — 3qrl accepted losing atomicity from the page and did not consider inventing a variable. `isValueNeutralLockedRmw` (`decompile/lifter.ts`) sends it back to `raw` (`peek-a-bin-qbk3`). Five things:
  - **The test is the nil value effect, and the `lock` prefix is required AS WELL, never instead.** An *unlocked* `or [mem], 0` is not a fence, it is a genuinely dead store, and whether to delete one is a different judgement. The restriction costs nothing measurable: there are **0** unlocked value-neutral RMWs over memory in the corpus.
  - **`and <mem>, <all ones>` is REFUSED, and that refusal is measured rather than cautious.** `and`'s identity element is width-dependent, and a text-level "all ones" reading misfires on real instructions *in this corpus* — `and <reg>, 0xff` and `and <reg>, 0xffff` (2 each per PE32 binary) and `and <reg>, 0xfff` (2 per x64 binary) are truncations, not no-ops. A misfire in the admitting direction silently deletes a real store, and the form has 0 occurrences to calibrate against.
  - **`xchg` with itself is not in this class at all, by the ISA rather than by measurement.** `lock` requires a memory operand and `xchg` requires a register one, so a locked `xchg` can never have equal operands. The bead listed it; the encoding does not exist. The shifts are excluded for the same kind of reason — `x << 0 == x`, but `lock` applies only to ADD/ADC/AND/BTC/BTR/BTS/CMPXCHG/DEC/INC/NEG/NOT/OR/SBB/SUB/XOR/XADD/XCHG, so `lock shl` raises #UD.
  - **`raw` rather than `comment`, and the text stays the verbatim disassembly.** A `comment` is not a statement and carries no address; the emitter never emits a truly empty `if` body, so a fence that is a region's whole content would take the region with it — which is precisely how `peek-a-bin-frt8`'s six `bt` guards had been surviving. Naming it a barrier in prose would also put editorial into a field `emit.ts` documents as always being a disassembly line.
  - **Measured at base `41113c9`, both sides pinned.** Self-assignments **3 → 0** per x64 binary and `var_0` declarations **2 → 0** — not 3, as the bead predicted: the two fences in `sub_14000C440` / `sub_14000A690` promote to the same slot and share one declaration. `var_0` mentions 8 → 0, −2 emitted lines, **2 of 279 and 2 of 275** functions changed, t32/w32 **byte-identical** (0 locked instructions of any kind in either). Every gate flat — gcc 1127/1127, `offsetof` 935/935 across 161 at ratio 1.00, polarity 1554/1554 with 0 inverted and `CHANGED 0`/`only-base 0`/`only-change 0` on all four, unrecovered values unmoved, loop shapes unmoved, 0 statements dropped, 0 callees lost, arity/staleReads/staleGuards/popReads/lostDefs/armExits/wildBranches/unencodableNames all 0. `compare.mjs`'s only moved rows are `lostDefs`' liveness counters, −6 per x64 binary, exactly the two `rsp` reads per fence leaving the examined set. **No gate could see this defect** — gcc compiles a self-assignment, polarity judges guards that exist, the statement-drop audit snapshots after `foldBlock` and counts drops rather than inventions, and the store→raw swap is 1:1 so even the lifted-statement count is unmoved. The instrument is the **self-assignment census resolved to the originating instruction** (`peek-a-bin-o7pj`'s), and note the PE32 pair's 31 self-assignments are that bead's population and must stay: 1 per binary is an honest `sub eax, 0` the machine really executes (t32 0x402FAD, w32 0x403201). **Do not fix this class by suppressing self-assignments** — that trace is what caught `peek-a-bin-3axd`.
- **`bt` is a compare over one bit, and it is neither a compare nor a result.** `bt r11d, 0xa / jb` sat in `PARTIAL_FLAG_WRITERS`, so `flagEffect` clobbered and the Jcc reading its CF was unrecovered — 34 sites across the four binaries, all in MSVC `_bittest`-style flag chains where the sibling bits *were* recovered: `t64!sub_140003B44` emitted `if ((r11d & 1) != 0) { if ((r11d & 8) != 0) { /* unlifted: bt r11d, 0xa */; if (!!__unrecovered_1 /* jb */)`, so bit 10's test was the one thing on the page the reader was told nothing about. The fix is a third owner kind, `bittest`, plus a `RegState` flag op of the same name (`peek-a-bin-frt8`). Six things, and the first is the correction to the bead:
  - **The bead's mechanism was half wrong, and the half that was wrong is the ordering warning.** It said the condition "would have to name a value the block never produces", so the lift had to come first. `bt` **writes nothing** — the bit base is unmodified — so the value the condition names is the operand as the instruction wrote it, and it needs no lift at all. Checked by executing it: with `flagModel.ts` fixed and `liftBlock`'s `bt` case removed, the guard is emitted correctly *beside* the `unlifted` comment. This is the exact opposite of `peek-a-bin-3qrl`'s `lock dec`, where the same-shaped warning is right because the value really is one the store produces — and the contrast is the point: "lift first" is a rule about *result* owners, not about flag owners generally.
  - **So it must not be modelled as a `FlagOwnerResult`.** `destText` there means "the value the instruction produced", and reading `bt`'s operand afterwards gives what it held before. `defines: "cf"` keeps it apart from `"zf-sf"` for the harder reason: **`bt` leaves ZF *unaffected***, not undefined (SDM: "The CF flag contains the value of the selected bit. The ZF flag is unaffected"), so a `je` after one really does branch on an older instruction's ZF and answering it from the bit would be a *wrong test*, not a missing one. `getCondition`'s `bittest` arm answers `jb`/`jc`/`jnae` and `jae`/`jnb`/`jnc` and nothing else. Note this is the mirror image of the `test` arm, which answers strictly MORE forms than a result because `test` pins OF and CF to 0 (`peek-a-bin-92yy`) — the two look parallel and are not.
  - **`parseBitTest` admits a register bit base with an immediate offset, and that is the whole recoverable population.** A **memory** bit base is refused because it would be *unsound*, not merely unmeasured: Intel documents the modulo reduction for a register base, and with a memory base the offset indexes a bit string, so `bt DWORD PTR [esp], eax` can select a bit outside the dword the operand names and `(*(uint32_t*)esp >> eax) & 1` is right only while `eax < 32`. Both PE32 binaries have exactly that shape and nothing else (t32 0x40B678/0x40BB21, w32 0x40A3A8/0x40A4E1, MSVC's `_bittest` on a stack temporary), which is why this recovers **nothing on PE32 and leaves both byte-identical**. A register bit offset is refused as unmeasured rather than unsound. `bts`/`btr`/`btc` stay clobbers permanently: their CF is the bit's value *before* the write, so nothing readable afterwards names it. **One predicate, two callers** — `flagEffect` to claim ownership and `liftBlock` to record the same reading — because a second copy could disagree about which forms are sound.
  - **The spelling is `(x >> n & 1) != 0` with `>>`, not `>>>`.** Both are correct — the `& 1` discards everything the sign extension could contribute — but `emitLogicalShiftRight` turns a `>>>` whose operand width it cannot determine into an `__unrecovered_N`, and after copy propagation has folded a call result into the bit base that would trade a recovered guard for an admitted one. The modulo in `parseBitTest` is load-bearing for the same emitter: an index at or past the operand width is a width contradiction.
  - **`corpus/sweep.ts`'s polarity table had to gain vocabulary, and that is the audit moving in the same commit as the change it judges.** `TAKEN` is a `cmp` model — it maps each jcc to the operator of the magnitude comparison its flags describe — and for a `bt` there is no `<`-shaped or `>=`-shaped spelling of "that bit is set". Without the extension the gate reports 8 MISMATCH rows on t64 and 5 on w64, every one of them provably correct output. `BIT_TAKEN` gives `jb → !=` and `jae → ==` for a jcc whose **immediately preceding instruction** is a `bt` — decided from the machine text, deliberately with no flag-transparency reasoning, so the widening is exactly as narrow as the fact that justifies it and a `bt` one instruction further away still reports MISMATCH. **It is a widening and not a weakening, and that is checkable**: `NEG` maps `!=` and `==` to each other, so flipping `getCondition`'s `bittest` arm makes `npm run corpus` exit 1 with `inverted=8` on t64 and `inverted=5` on w64 — the gate keeps full discriminating power at exactly the sites it admits. Contorting the emitted C into `0 < (x >> n & 1)` to satisfy the old table was considered and refused: it would be gaming the oracle.
  - **`if` emitted FALLS, and the whole −6/−4 is accounted for.** Two thirds of it is `cleanup.ts` folding a now-complete chain into one `&&` — `if (a) { if (b) { if (c) } }` becomes `if (a && b && c)`, semantically identical and no longer anchorable to a single jcc, which is `peek-a-bin-3axd`'s documented behaviour and is why 8 guards on t64 and 5 on w64 leave the audited set while 8 and 5 enter (`CHANGED 0`, coverage exactly flat at 566/566 and 493/493, all correct). The other third is a **real if small information loss**: 4 regions on t64 and 2 on w64 disappear entirely, and all six are `bt r12d/r13d, 0xc / jb` guarding exactly `mov r8d, r8d`. The emitter never emits a truly empty `if` body — measured, 0 in both runs — so those guards survived in the base *only* because the `unlifted: bt` comment was a statement propping the body open. The region has no content because `mov r8d, r8d` is an x64 zero-extension of the upper 32 bits that reached the page as nothing; fix that and both guards come back with something to guard. **Now fixed — `peek-a-bin-tez6`, see the gotcha below, and note that "`liftBlock` models it as nothing" was the wrong file**: the lift emitted an ordinary `assign` and `copyPropagation` deleted it. All 6 regions are back, `if` emitted 1934 → 1938 and 1717 → 1719.
  - **Measured at base `ebac41e`** (i.e. with `peek-a-bin-3qrl` already in), both sides pinned: unrecovered values **36 → 21 on t64 and 26 → 17 on w64**, token occurrences 72 → 42 and 52 → 34, t32/w32 **byte-identical** at 94/74; 7 of 279 and 5 of 275 functions changed; statements lifted 7764 → 7745 and 6892 → 6881 (the `raw` statements the `bt`s used to be). Every gate held — gcc 1127/1127, `offsetof` ratio 1.00 with fields and definitions unmoved, polarity 0 inverted and 0 mismatch, 0 statements dropped, 0 callees lost, 0 loops short, arity exact/under/over unmoved, staleGuards 0 named with `shapes` unmoved, staleReads 0/0, popReads 7/0/0/6, lostDefs 0, armExits 0, gotos/labels/dangling identical at 3070/2517/0 — and **loop shapes identical on all four**: `for` 3/5/5/3, `while` 114/107/100/101, `do/while` 78/77/76/77.
- **THE POLARITY AUDIT'S POPULATION WAS ONE TRAILING BRACE WIDE, AND THE LOSS WOULD HAVE BEEN SILENT.** `corpus/sweep.ts`'s guard scan matched a header with `/^(\s*)(?:\}\s*else\s+)?(if|while)\s*\((.*)\)\s*\{\s*$/`, so an `if` whose body moved onto the header's own line stopped being a guard **at all** — not judged, not skipped, not a row. `peek-a-bin-252` refused to one-line `if (!c) { break; }` for exactly that reason and the refusal was right; the fix is that the grammar no longer cares. `corpus/guardShape.ts` is now the one declaration of what a guard line is, read by the polarity scan, by `auditLoopExits`' header detection and — through `statementOnLine` — by the two scrapes that look for a terminator; `unparsed` counts every line that begins with a guard keyword and an open paren and was not understood, and **gates at 0**. Restamped at `baa7f61`: **572/550/512/515 `if`s (2149) whose whole body is a single terminator** on t32/t64/w64/w32 — 437/426/392/384 `goto`, 114/115/111/110 `break`, 19/9/9/19 `return`, 2/0/0/2 `continue` — of which only the **`return` bodies carry a line-map address**, because `stmtAddr` answers for `assign`/`store`/`call_stmt`/`return`/`raw`/`phi` and for nothing else. So the split is **56 anchorable and 2093 not**, and the bead's claim that a `goto` body carries an address is wrong. Seven things, and the last three are the evidence:
  - **The condition is depth-counted, not anchored, and widening the old regex in place is a defect.** `/\((.*)\)\s*(.*)$/` is greedy to the *last* `)` on the line, so `if (a == 0) x = f(b);` reads as the condition `a == 0) x = f(b` — a wrong condition is a wrong verdict, which is worse than the silence being fixed. `guardShape` matches the condition's own parentheses, which makes the tail an independent question. Pinned by a test named for it.
  - **`inline` was a bound on the grammar rather than a measurement, and `peek-a-bin-0qib` gave it a population.** At `baa7f61` `emit.ts` always opened a block, so the arm had **0 corpus occurrences** and was pinned by `build/guardShape.test.ts` or nowhere — the status `pop esp` and the `rip`/`rsp` refusals have — and the emitter was deliberately left alone so that byte-identical emitted C would prove the widening inert on today's output rather than measuring its own input. It is now **572/550/512/515 lines**; see the bullet below.
  - **The inline anchor rested on a contract with the emitter, and `peek-a-bin-0qib` is what checks it.** An emitter that one-lines must attach the **body's** address to the line, because that address is what identifies the arm; attaching the guard's own block instead anchors to the jcc one decision earlier, which is the `peek-a-bin-8r0` / `peek-a-bin-lbz` class of false INVERTED. `bodyAddrAt`'s `inlineBodyAddr` says so where it reads it, and `oneLinedGuard` in `emit.ts` is written so the guard's address is not in its scope.
  - **One-lining breaks THREE text scrapes, not one, and they fail in different directions.** The polarity scan goes silent. `auditLoopExits` counts a way out with `/^break\s*;/`, `/^return\b/` and `/^goto\s+loc_/` anchored at the line start, so it goes **RED on correct output** — measured, 31/32/33/32 loops falsely short. And `emitAudits.ts`'s `gotoCheck` matches `/^\s*goto (\w+);/gm`, so it reports **`dangling` 0 out of a population it no longer sees** — measured, gotos 3086 → **1447** with dangling still 0, a gate at 0 passing because it stopped looking, which is the worst of the three. All three now read one grammar; wiring `statementOnLine` in is output-neutral (gotos/labels/dangling **3086/2527/0 either side**).
  - **CONTROL 1 — the hazard, measured.** One-lining with the audit as it stood at `baa7f61`: guard ledger **919/822/727/821 → 901/814/719/803** rows, i.e. 18/8/8/18 anchored verdicts gone (anchor **A 2/1/1/2**, the gated tier, plus A2 16/7/7/16); anchor A **517/574/497/441 → 515/573/496/439**, A2 391/234/216/369 → 375/227/209/353; and `skipped` **1263/1305/1173/1119 → 709/763/669/622**, i.e. 554/542/504/497 rows that were being *counted as skipped* stopped being counted at all. 554+18, 542+8, 504+8 and 497+18 are exactly 572/550/512/515 — the accounting closes on every binary.
  - **CONTROL 2 — the widening restores the ledger exactly, not approximately.** One-lining with the new grammar: anchor A, A2, B and `skipped` **identical to base on all four**, and the guard rows joined on `(jcc, sense, expect, emitted, verdict, anchor)` **hash-identical to base** on all four (919/822/727/821 rows). `braced` falls by exactly 572/550/512/515 while `inline` rises by exactly the same, so the census sums to the base total; loop exits back to 0 short; gotos back to 3086.
  - **CONTROL 3 — full discriminating power at the sites it admits, the `BIT_TAKEN` standard.** Control 2 with every emitted comparison operator flipped in `extractCondition`: `inverted` equals `checked` exactly — **517/574/497/441 with 0 correct**, A2 0/391, 0/234, 0/216, 0/369, B 0/11 — and **all 18/8/8/18 rows control 1 had lost are individually INVERTED**, none missing and none MISMATCH. A widening that admitted rows and judged them laxly would show up here as a shortfall against `checked`.
- **THE SECOND HAND-ROLLED GUARD-HEADER PATTERN IS GONE, AND THE DRIFT GUARD DELIBERATELY COULD NOT SEE IT.** `corpus/selfAssigns.ts`'s `FOR_HEADER` was `/^\s*for \((.*)\) \{\s*$/` — the brittleness `peek-a-bin-vwr5` closed in `corpus/sweep.ts`, surviving in a file whose `wrong` and `unresolved` columns **gate at 0**, and invisible to `build/guardShape.test.ts` because that guard's tell is a regex *alternating* two guard keywords and this one named `for` alone. It also encoded single-space formatting and a trailing brace, so `for(`, `for  (` and `)  {` each defeated it — a formatting change `biome` could make, taking rows out of a gate silently, and a gate that stops looking reads as a clean tree. It now asks `forHeaderCond` and `splitForHeader` (`corpus/guardShape.ts`), and reads its assignment statement through `statementOnLine` (`peek-a-bin-hfsq`). Five things:
  - **The grammar answers what the LINE is; what a caller wants out of it stays the caller's.** `selfAssigns.ts` wants the init and the update as *statements* — the reason it reads a `for` header at all — and `sweep.ts` wants the middle clause to judge against a jcc. Neither is entitled to its own answer about the header, so `splitForHeader` moved into `guardShape.ts` and `sweep.ts`'s private `splitFor` is deleted; `forHeaderCond` exists so no caller re-spells `kind === "braced" && kw === "for"`.
  - **`forHeaders` and `forHeadersUnsplit` are the liveness and integrity halves, and the second is a GATE at 0.** A text-scraping audit fails by matching nothing, so a header shape the grammar stopped recognising must be visible: `forHeaders` is **6 per binary** (agreeing with the independently counted `for` loop shape) and `compare.mjs` flags a fall. `forHeadersUnsplit` is a header recognised and then refused by the splitter, i.e. clauses not read — `emit.ts` always writes three, so every row is provably a site that left the scan, which is `unresolved`'s character rather than a baseline's. 0 on all four, and 0 occurrences means the corpus cannot demonstrate it discriminates, so both are negative-controlled in `build/selfAssignAudit.test.ts` and by perturbing a summary through `compare.mjs`.
  - **Reading the assignment through `statementOnLine` recovers nothing today and is the point.** No emitted assignment is one-lined — `peek-a-bin-0qib` admits terminators only — but `ASSIGN_LINE` was applied to the raw line and matched the *first* ` = `, so `if (ecx == 0) eax = eax;` would read its destination as `if (ecx == 0) eax`, fail the equality test and drop the row. Pinned in `build/selfAssignAudit.test.ts` rather than measured, which is the same status the `inline` arm itself had before 0qib.
  - **WIDENING THE DRIFT GUARD TO SINGLE-KEYWORD PATTERNS WAS MEASURED AND REFUSED.** The tell would have to be a guard keyword beside an open paren, and the three patterns under `corpus/` that trips are all asking a different question: `sweep.ts`'s `OPENER` asks whether a line opens a block at all (it also names `{`, `__try` and a bare `} else`), its `siteOf` buckets an unrecovered value's site *descriptively* — that file says in terms the buckets are not load-bearing — and `clobbered.whiles`/`fors` are raw `while (` / `for (` match counts kept deliberately raw so artifact directories from before the do/while split still compare. None of the three gates anything and none reads a guard header as a whole line, which is the shape that goes silent; the class worth catching is a second reader feeding a gate, and there is now none. Recorded in the guard's own docstring so the next reader does not re-derive it.
  - **Measured at base `87a8499` + `peek-a-bin-0qib`, both sides pinned: NOT ONE MOVED ROW.** Emitted C **byte-identical on all four binaries**, `compare.mjs` verdict "no regression", and `report.txt` differs in exactly the four lines the new `; 6 for headers read` suffix adds. `openOperand` **1/0/0/1** and `identity` **30/0/0/30** unmoved with `wrong` and `unresolved` 0 — necessarily, since the population it touches is empty, but measured rather than assumed. `src` 3558/3558 over 95 files, `build` 118/118 over 10.
- **A GUARD WHOSE WHOLE BODY IS ONE TERMINATOR IS EMITTED ON ONE LINE, and the only reason that was ever unsafe is now gone.** `if (c) break;` for `if (c) { break; }` — **2149 sites, 572/550/512/515 on t32/t64/w64/w32**, re-measured at `87a8499` and identical figure for figure to the `baa7f61` stamp, split 437/426/392/384 `goto`, 114/115/111/110 `break`, 19/9/9/19 `return` and 2/0/0/2 `continue`, of which 20/10/10/16 are the last arm of an else-if chain. `peek-a-bin-252` refused it and the refusal was right: `corpus/sweep.ts`'s guard scan matched an `if` only when the line ended in `{`, so a one-lined guard was **not a guard at all** — not judged, not skipped, not a row. `peek-a-bin-vwr5` made `corpus/guardShape.ts` the one grammar for a guard line and gated the lines it cannot read at 0, which is the whole precondition. **The benefit is density and nothing else** (−4298 emitted lines, −6.5% to −7.0% per binary); the reason to record it at length is that it is the first exercise of an arm that had no population (`peek-a-bin-0qib`). Six things:
  - **THE ONE-LINED LINE MUST CARRY THE BODY'S ADDRESS, and `oneLinedGuard` (`emit.ts`) exists so that it cannot carry anything else.** It is handed the body's own `EmitResult` and the guard is not in its scope, so there is no guard address available to attach by mistake — and `IRIf` has no `addr` field either, so `stmtAddr` answers `undefined` for it. Attaching the guard's own block would anchor the arm to the jcc one decision earlier, which is the `peek-a-bin-8r0` / `peek-a-bin-lbz` class of false INVERTED, and `bodyAddrAt`'s `inlineBodyAddr` reads exactly this line's entry.
  - **The contract is now CHECKED on real rows rather than argued.** The inline arm carries 572/550/512/515 census lines; of those, **19/9/9/19 (56) carry an address** — only a `return` does, since `stmtAddr` answers for `assign`/`store`/`call_stmt`/`return`/`raw`/`phi` and `break`/`continue`/`goto` are none of them — and **18/8/8/18 (52) are judged, 2/1/1/2 of them in the GATED anchor-A tier** and 16/7/7/16 in A2. Those are exactly the rows vwr5's control 1 measured as *lost*, so the widening restores precisely what naive one-lining would have destroyed. Measured by disabling `inlineBodyAddr`: anchor A 522/578/501/446 → 520/577/500/444, A2 396/237/219/374 → 380/230/212/358, `skipped` up by exactly 18/8/8/18. Since the guard rows are md5-identical to base over `(jcc, sense, expect, emitted, verdict, anchor)`, a guard-block address would have had to produce the same 52 verdicts against a different jcc, which it cannot.
  - **The four admitted kinds are all TERMINATORS, and an assignment is deliberately not one.** A terminator ends the arm, so a one-lined guard states the whole of it; `corpus/selfAssigns.ts`'s `ASSIGN_LINE` reads `<dest> = <src>;` at statement position and a one-lined assignment would hand it the guard as the destination — a row leaving a scan whose `wrong` and `unresolved` columns gate at 0. `peek-a-bin-hfsq` closed that prospectively by asking it of `statementOnLine`; widening the emitter still needs its own measurement, and this change did not do it.
  - **THE DIFF IS PROVABLY NOTHING BUT THE JOIN.** Re-expanding every one-lined guard in the change's emitted C back to three lines reproduces the base **byte for byte on all 1072 functions of all four binaries**, at exactly 572/550/512/515 sites. That is the statement worth taking from this, because `emitted C identical` reads only 130/260, 163/279, 160/275 and 131/258 — a purely cosmetic change touching half the corpus looks, to that row, like a large one.
  - **Every other number held, and the report differs in FOUR LINES.** `corpus/artifacts/*/report.txt` is byte-identical between the two runs but for the one `guard-shaped lines` line per binary. Guard rows md5-identical over `(jcc, sense, expect, emitted, verdict, anchor)` at 929/829/734/831; anchor A/A2/B and `skipped` identical; `skipReasons` totals identical, with 593/624/571/527 `if:body-has-no-address` becoming 40/83/68/31 plus 553/541/503/496 `if:inline-body-has-no-address`; gcc 260/279/275/258 clean; `offsetof` ratio 1.00 with fields and definitions unmoved; `if` 1987/1938/1719/1759 and `for` 6/6/6/6, `while` 111/106/99/98, `do/while` 78/77/76/77; gotos/labels/dangling 3086/2527/0; invented prelude declarations 1460/2456/2278/1417; `emptyCaseBodies` unmoved with **every sub-row identical** (80 labels / 37 a lone goto / 43 with a body on t32, 62/37/25 on w32) — the reclassification the bead warned of does not happen, because `armExit`'s one-lined `if (cond) goto L;` matches neither `break;` nor `/^goto \w+;$/`; and 0 on every gate. The two rows that move are the census split and `emitted lines scanned` (selfAssigns' liveness half), 16367/16930/15275/14923 → 15223/15830/14251/13893, i.e. **exactly two lines per site**.
  - **`compare.mjs` flagged four regressions that were not one, and the fix is the rule its own comment already stated.** The predicate was `braced` falling, so a guard moving to the `inline` spelling read as a lost guard line; the comment beside it said "a fall in `braced` with no matching rise in `inline`". It now flags **`braced + inline`**, printed as `guard lines seen (top-tested)`, which is **exactly flat at 2104/2050/1824/1863** across this change — a stronger statement than two rows moving by equal amounts. It is a widening and not a weakening: replaying vwr5's control 1 (`inline` forced to 0) takes the sum down by 572/550/512/515 and is still flagged on all four, and a shape the grammar refuses outright is `unparsed`, which gates at 0 in the run itself.
- **A `for`'s init does not have to be the statement immediately before the loop, and the guard that said so was costing 3 loops per 32-bit binary.** `structureFrom` has already emitted the init as part of an earlier block and a `for` header repeats it, so one of the two copies must go and the only sound direction is to delete the emitted one — which used to require `result[result.length - 1] === asFor.init`, i.e. object identity against the *last* statement. Everything else was demoted back to the `while` it was built from. `initHoistable` answers the general case instead, and the question is precisely stated: hoisting moves the init **later**, past everything between it and the loop, so it is not enough that nothing in between touches the induction variable — the value the init computes has to be the same after the move. Four refusals, each measured rather than hypothesised (`peek-a-bin-9q2`, census at `c560b4c`):
  - **A whitelist of what may be in between — `assign`, `store`, `comment` — not a blacklist.** A `label` in between is a jump target, and a `goto` reaching it *skips* the init today while after the move it would *run* it: a different program, and one no gate here models. A `raw` is an instruction the lifter refused, so its effects are unknown by definition. Every intervening statement in the corpus population is an `assign` or a `store`, so nothing measured is refused by this.
  - **A side-effecting init** (`hasSideEffects`) — `x = f()` moved later calls `f` later — and **any mention in between of the induction variable or of anything the init's right-hand side reads**, scanned with `walkStmts` so a nested body counts. A mention is not necessarily a write and the distinction is deliberately not drawn: refusing on a read costs a `for` and claims nothing false.
  - **A memory write in between when the init's right-hand side reads memory**, with no attempt to prove the two do not alias — the policy `spoils` applies, for the same reason. `writesMemoryDeep` asks it of nested bodies too and counts an `assign` whose *dest* is a deref, since `IRAssign.dest` is an `IRExpr`.
  - **`initAt >= 0` is not redundant with the equality test, and getting that wrong invents statements.** `detectForLoop` searches every non-back-edge predecessor for the last assignment to the induction variable, so the statement it names can live in a region this walk never emitted — that is the *entire* x64 population, which is why this recovers nothing there. For an empty `result` both `indexOf` and `length - 1` are `-1`, so an equality-only test takes the hoist path with no init present and copies one into the header that the walk never emitted. The `result[result.length - 1] === init` form this replaces was incidentally safe against that (`result[-1]` is `undefined`); measured, the bug added **+3 `for` on each x64 binary** where the census predicted 0, and that predicted-versus-measured gap is the only thing that caught it.

  **At HEAD (`4ce0b2d`), the loop shape is `for` 6/6/6/6, `while` 111/106/99/98, `do/while` 78/77/76/77 and `if` 1985/1938/1719/1757** (t32/t64/w64/w32), from one run over the integrated tree; the `if` figures carry `peek-a-bin-tez6`'s +4/+2 as well, and `for` 6/5/5/6 with `while` 111/107/100/98 is the figure for `da0815d`, one commit earlier. Every other figure quoting a loop shape in this file is a historical measurement of its own change and is stamped as such.

  Measured at `c560b4c`, both sides pinned: **`for` 3/5/5/3 → 6/5/5/6** and top-tested `while` 114/107/100/101 → 111/107/100/98, with **`do/while` unmoved at 78/77/76/77** and both x64 binaries **byte-identical** (0 functions changed, 816/816 guards `CHANGED 0`). 2 functions of emitted text changed per 32-bit binary, −6 lines each — a hoist *removes* a line, so a duplicated init would show here as a rise. Every gate flat: gcc 288/288 and 285/285, `offsetof` 331/331 and 418/418, 0 inverted, 0 throws, 0 statements dropped, 0 callees lost, unrecovered values unmoved at 91/21/17/72, arity unmoved, staleReads/staleGuards/popReads/lostDefs/armExits/unencodableNames all flat. `compare.mjs` flags **one row per 32-bit binary and it is the documented benign anchor shift**: `polarity guards audited` 499 → 498 and 430 → 429, because the strict anchor-A tier loses the single `if (al != 0)` guard at t32 0x405662 / w32 0x4059c3 whose **text is byte-identical** and whose anchor moved A → A2 when the hoist removed the statement carrying that block's start address — the A2 tier rises by exactly 1, **A+A2 is flat at 879 and 789**, and every verdict is `OK`. The only other `CHANGED` rows are the intended ones: a guard whose `kind` went `while` → `for` with identical condition, operator, verdict and anchor.
- **`detectForLoop` commits to the FIRST increment-shaped statement it sees and used never to reconsider — and a loop body routinely increments more than one thing.** The corpus shape is MSVC's newline count, `for (p = start; p < end; p++) if (*p == '\n') n++;`: `n++` is a self-increment too, its block is numbered *below* the latch, and it has no initialiser anywhere in the function because `n` comes in from an enclosing scope. So the search picked `n`, failed the init test and returned null, and the loop was emitted as a `while` — **26 loops corpus-wide, 8/8/5/5 on t32/t64/w64/w32** at `bd73798`. Every increment-shaped candidate is now tried in block-id order and the first with an init wins. Two things about that (`peek-a-bin-9q2`):
  - **It is additive by construction, not by measurement.** If the first candidate has an init, behaviour is byte-for-byte what it was; the later candidates are only ever reached where the old code returned null. A candidate is *offered*, never imposed — `structureCFG` still refuses it unless it is the final statement of the structured body, which is why 22 of the 26 newly recognised loops are still emitted as `while` with output byte-identical to the base.
  - **A predecessor inside the loop body is not a source of inits, and `p < header.id` was the wrong test.** Block-id order stood in for "before the loop", and the latch is routinely numbered *below* its header: in **19/18/15/16** of the loops reaching this site one of the "pre-loop" predecessors is a body block, so what the search found was an in-loop write — 4 loops corpus-wide had the update itself as their "init". It has been harmless only because `structureFrom` cannot find such a statement among the ones it has already emitted and demotes the `for` back to a `while` (`f_init_absent`). The loop body is the fact, so `!bodyBlocks.includes(p)` is what gets asked; the header is named explicitly too, since `collectLoopBodyBlockIds` omits it and a self-loop would otherwise offer its own statements. **The suite pinned the proxy as the rule** — `it("ignores predecessors whose id is above the header (assumed back-edges)")`, whose comment conceded it "only costs a for/while downgrade" and justified itself with "real initialisers precede the header in address order", which the 19/18/15/16 refutes. Replaced by two tests asserting the real rule in both directions.

  Measured at base `bd73798`, both sides pinned: **`for` 6/5/5/6 → 6/6/6/6** and top-tested `while` 111/107/100/98 → 111/**106/99**/98, **`do/while` unmoved at 78/77/76/77**, `if` unmoved at 1987/1938/1719/1759, and **t32 and w32 byte-identical** (288/288 and 285/285 functions). Exactly one function per x64 binary changed — `t64!sub_140003200` / `w64!sub_14000356C`, the same CRT routine — and the diff is 4 lines: `rax = …field_0x10; while (rax < …field_0x0) { … rax++; }` became `for (rax = …field_0x10; rax < …field_0x0; rax++) { … }`, read against the emitted C of both runs. Every gate flat: gcc 1127/1127, `offsetof` ratio 1.00 with fields and definitions unmoved, 0 inverted, 0 mismatch, 0 throws, 0 statements dropped, 0 callees lost, 0 loops short, arity exact/under/over unmoved, staleReads 0/0, staleGuards 0 named, popReads 4/0/0/4, lostDefs 0, armExits 0, unencodableNames 0, unrecovered values **unmoved** at 91/21/17/72. `compare.mjs` flags **one row per x64 binary and it is the documented benign anchor shift**: `polarity guards audited` 566 → 565 and 493 → 492, `CHANGED 0` / `only-change 0`, the lost guard being `if ((*(uint8_t*)(rax + rcx + 8) & 0x80) != 0)` at t64 0x1400032a5 / w64 0x140003611 — verdict `OK`, anchor `A`, **text unchanged and still on the page** — whose `bodyAddr` was the init statement that has now moved into the `for` header, so nothing anchors that block any more.

  **THE REST OF THE FUNNEL IS REFUSED ON EVIDENCE, and the census is worth more than the fix.** Full funnel at `bd73798`, per binary t32/t64/w64/w32, from a probe inside `detectForLoop` and at the `structureLoop` call site (both reverted):

  | | t32 | t64 | w64 | w32 |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | top-tested loops reaching the for-detection site | 48 | 34 | 30 | 39 |
  | reject: `detectForLoop` returned null | 28 | 17 | 14 | 19 |
  | … increment exists but is not its block's LAST statement | 15 | 4 | 4 | 9 |
  | … no increment anywhere; last statement an ordinary assign | 2 | 1 | 1 | 2 |
  | … increment found, no init in a pre-loop predecessor | 11 | 12 | 9 | 8 |
  | reject: update not the last statement of the structured body | 10 | 8 | 7 | 10 |
  | ACCEPTED as a `for` | 10 | 9 | 9 | 10 |

  The two rejection populations the bead named as the remaining work are **both dead**, and the instrument that kills them is one probe: for every loop reaching the site, find the *last* increment-shaped top-level statement of the structured body — the most favourable choice, since an earlier one's tail contains it — and ask whether the update could be hoisted past what follows. Under `initHoistable`'s own whitelist (`assign`/`store`/`comment`, no mention of the induction variable, no call) the answer is **0 loops on all four binaries**. Widening the whitelist to admit a *benign nested `if`* changes nothing: **0 again**, because every one of the 65 trailing `if`s contains a control transfer — `continue` in 35, `goto` in 28, a `label` in 2 — and a `continue` reaches a `for`'s update while the machine's back edge does not, which is `hasFreeContinue`'s own reason. Asked of the "update not last" population specifically, all 26 whose update is even present in the structured body are blocked by `continue` (16), `goto` (6) or `label` (4), and the other 9 have the update nowhere in the body at all. **So the update-position guard is not strict, it is exact**: there is no loop in this corpus it costs. Do not re-attempt it, and do not read "the update sits one statement from the end in 6/5/5/6 of them" — the earlier census's figure, which is correct — as evidence that a relaxation is available; that one statement is the loop's own exit.

  What is left, and its size: **40 loops whose increment has no init in a pre-loop predecessor** (11/12/9/8) survive the fix above only as far as the update-position guard, which rejects 36 of them for the reasons just given. The 4 that pass are the ones this change recovers (2 emitted as `for`; the other 2 are `t64`/`w64` 0x14000333c / 0x1400036a8, where `initHoistable` correctly refuses because `rcx_13 = edi + rax` between the init and the loop reads the induction variable). **The residual init-search population is therefore worth at most 4 loops and probably 0**, and 12 corpus-wide accepted `for`s are still demoted because the init lives in a region the walk never emitted — out of reach by construction, since there is no copy to delete.
- **`mov <r32>, <same r32>` on x64 is a ZERO-EXTENSION, and the pass that lost it was `copyPropagation`, not the lift.** A 32-bit write clears bits 63:32 of the parent register, so `mov r8d, r8d` is MSVC asking for exactly that; on PE32 `mov edi, edi` is the hot-patch pad and a true no-op. `liftBlock` emitted an ordinary `assign r8d = r8d`, `isCopyStmt` admitted it — both sides `reg`, versioned dest, neither RSP, which `r8_2 = r8_1` satisfies — and `copyPropagation` rewrote every reader to the **pre**-truncation version and dropped the statement. Correctly, by its own rule: nothing in `r8d = r8d` says the high half was cleared. **So the truncation has to be in the expression**, and the spelling is `x & 0xFFFFFFFF` over the 64-bit parent — the one `foldExpr` already refuses to strip when the left operand is wider than the mask (`narrowEnoughForMask32`, `peek-a-bin-6hw`, which exists for this exact semantics), so it survives folding as well as copy propagation. Five things (`peek-a-bin-tez6`):
  - **The bead's mechanism was wrong and it named the wrong file.** It said `liftBlock` "treats a same-width self-move as a non-definition and emits nothing"; the `mov` handler has **no self-move test at all**. The only one in `lifter.ts` is in `firstCalleeSavedWrites`, answering a different question — which address first writes a callee-saved register — and it must keep reading `mov X, X` as a non-definition: generalising that test withdrew t32 0x4068C6's zeroing and turned four real `Sleep(esi)` calls per x86 binary into `Sleep()` (`peek-a-bin-6lmh`). The fix is scoped to the lift and changes nothing about what counts as a first write.
  - **Three bounds, each load-bearing.** `is64` only, or the 173 + 170 PE32 hot-patch pads all acquire a mask; **32-bit width only**, because `mov al, al` and `mov ax, ax` leave their parent's upper bits alone and `mov rax, rax` writes what it read, so only this width zero-extends; and `canonReg !== "rsp"`, for the reason the `pop` path excludes it — RSP is the one register no stage models. The last has 0 occurrences and is a bound on the rule rather than a saving.
  - **The population is 8, not the 6 the bead named, and the 2 it missed are the strongest evidence.** Only 32-bit self-moves exist on x64 (0 at any other width): t64 4 × `mov r8d, r8d` + 1 × `mov eax, eax` at 0x140002BD6, w64 2 + 1 at 0x140003072. Six are the `peek-a-bin-frt8` residue, where the self-move is the *entire* arm a `bt r12d/r13d, 0xc / jb` guards. The other two are `call / … / mov eax, eax / sub rbp, rax` — a 64-bit subtraction of the zero-extended result — where the base emitted `rbp -= eax;`, the **untruncated** value under a 32-bit name, in a function whose preceding `if (eax == -1)` invites reading it as signed. That is a wrong value in compiling C, not merely a region missing.
  - **The restored pair folds into one `&&`, so the guards do NOT re-enter the polarity audit** — the bead predicted they would. `cleanup.ts` turns `if (a) { if (b) { body } }` into `if (a && b)`, which is not anchorable to a single compare, so each site yields **one** `if` and `guards w/o single compare` rises by exactly +4 and +2 while `polarity guards audited` stays *exactly* flat at 566/566 and 493/493. `compare.mjs` flags that rise; it is the new guards themselves, not guards lost, and `CHANGED 0 / only-base 0 / only-change 0` on all four binaries is the proof no pre-existing guard was rewritten or dropped.
  - **Measured at base `6d5ae92`, both sides pinned.** `if` emitted **1934 → 1938 (t64)** and **1717 → 1719 (w64)**; statements lifted 7745 → 7750 and 6881 → 6884, i.e. exactly the 5 and 3 sites; 5 of 279 and 3 of 275 functions changed; **t32 and w32 byte-identical** (288/288 and 285/285 — a PE32 self-move is a real no-op and the two are the control). Every gate flat: gcc 1127/1127, `offsetof` ratio 1.00 with fields and definitions unmoved, 0 inverted, 0 mismatch, 0 throws, 0 statements dropped, 0 callees lost, 0 loops short, arity exact/under/over unmoved, staleGuards 0 named with `shapes` unmoved at 9/6, staleReads 0 wrong / 0 spoiled with sites unmoved at 227/225, popReads 7/0/0/6, lostDefs 0, armExits 0, unencodableNames 0 — and **unrecovered values UNMOVED** at 91/21/17/72, so nothing was traded for an admission. Line-map coverage *rises* (t64 insns 6132 → 6137, blocks 3461 → 3470). **Loop shapes identical on all four**: `for` 3/5/5/3, `while` 114/107/100/101, `do/while` 78/77/76/77. All 8 sites read by hand against `objdump` — six emit `if ((0x8000 & r12d) == 0 && (r12d >> 0xC & 1) == 0) { r8 &= 0xFFFFFFFF; }` and two `rbp -= eax;` → `rax &= 0xFFFFFFFF; rbp -= rax;`. Pinned in `pipeline.test.ts` ("a 32-bit self-move"); no pre-existing test asserted the old behaviour, since every `mov edi, edi` fixture in the suite is PE32 or `firstCalleeSavedWrites`.
- **A branch condition goes through the lifter's real `parseOperand`.** `extractCondition` (`structure.ts`) used to re-parse the `cmp`/`test` operands with a private `parseSimpleOperand` that hardcoded `size: 4` and never called `ripRelative.ts` — the **tenth** hand-rolled copy of the parsing this repo has already centralised nine times, and the one that mattered most, because it decides what every guard says. `cmp byte ptr [rcx], dl` read as a 32-bit load, and `cmp byte ptr [rip + 0x13358], 0` kept the literal `rip + 0x…` while a `mov` two lines away resolved. Guard derefs went from 550/420/385 uniformly `int32_t` to their real widths and literal `rip` in guards to zero, and three separate classes fell out of the width alone: `*(int32_t*)(rbp - 0x64)` became the frame slot `var_64`, `r13 != -1` became `r13 != INVALID_HANDLE_VALUE`, and 51 more structs were synthesized. `structureCFG` takes `is64` for this; `pipeline.ts` passes it (`peek-a-bin-w6f`, `peek-a-bin-h0us`).
- **SSA version 0 means a register's *entry* value. The definition counter starts at 1.** `renameExpr` and the phi-operand fill both map a read with an empty version stack — the function-entry or parameter value — to version 0, so if `newVersion()` also handed out 0 then `rax_0` the incoming value and `rax_0` the first definition were the same `(name, version)` pair. `ssaopt`'s `sameReg`/`regKey` key on exactly that pair, so copy propagation, constant propagation, GVN and DCE all treated the two as one value: a store of an incoming register emitted the value written to it *afterwards*, and a `return` on a path that never assigns the accumulator returned the other path's value. Real binaries: 0 lost callees, 0 throws, −66 to −71 emitted lines, polarity unchanged (`peek-a-bin-swi`).
- **Version 0's repair is taken at the function's entry, and nowhere else.** Version 0 is the register's *entry* value, so no statement defines it and `splitStaleReads`' usual repair — a copy at the defining statement — has no site. It used to take the copy at the top of the *reading* block, and only when an earlier statement in that block was the overwriter. Both halves fail when a **strictly dominating** block has already written the register: the read gets no repair and binds to a name holding something else (78 reads on t64, 28 on t32), or it gets one taken past the damage, so `rcx_0` preserves the wrong value under a name that looks recovered (19 spoiled copies on t64, 13 on t32). The one point where the register provably holds version 0 is the function's entry, so the copy goes there and every stale read of that version routes to it — one copy per register per function. The objection on record, a `wcslen` loop reading RAX_0 while the body advances RAX, needs a block the *unwinder* enters: no predecessor, hence no `idom` entry, hence `dominates` from the entry block declines it structurally. Corpus gate: `corpus/staleReads.ts`, 0 on all four binaries (`peek-a-bin-dqpk`).
- **`liftBlock` emits plain register reads. It does not substitute `RegState`'s symbolic value at each read.** It used to, while still emitting the assignment that produced the value, so one machine `call` became three calls in the IR (`rax := call f(r9)`, `r9 = f(r9)`, `eflags = f(r9) & f(r9)`) and `sub rax,rcx / sar rax,1 / dec rax` emitted RCX subtracted three times. Measured fix: lines calling the same function twice 121/188/120 → 0 on t64/t32/w64, `eflags` lines 138/326/135 → 5/4/5 (and 0 since `peek-a-bin-c33` stage 2b stopped emitting the pseudo-register at all). Propagation belongs to SSA, `ssaopt` and `foldBlock`, which have the version information that makes it sound; `RegState`'s symbolic values are still needed for `getCondition`/flag tracking (`peek-a-bin-urs`, `peek-a-bin-zsb`).
- **No read of RSP may be moved to another program point.** `push`, `pop` and the return address a `call` pushes are not lifted, so RSP changes with nothing in the IR recording it — there is no faithful definition chain to reason over. Inlining `mov ebp, esp` across an unmodelled `push` printed `*(int32_t*)(esp + 8)` where the instruction said `[ebp + 8]`: a base register the instruction never named, one push off the value it did name. **Both** `ssaopt.ts`'s copy propagation and `fold.ts`'s single-use inlining guard this, and both are needed — the second re-introduced the defect the moment an unrelated fix made the frame-pointer copy single-use (`peek-a-bin-rt4`).
- **…but `push <imm>` / `pop <reg>` IS lifted, because it is a `mov` wearing a stack instruction's clothes.** Not lifting a `pop` means it is not a *definition* in SSA, so every later read of that register binds to the value it held **before** the pop — and MSVC spells `mov reg, imm` as this two-byte idiom pervasively. The damage is wrong values in compiling C that no gate here can see: `add edi, esi` emitted as `edi = edi` where ESI is 8, `cmp eax, esi / je` as `if (eax == 0)` where the machine tests 8, and worst, t32!sub_401E71's `push 0x16 / pop esi / mov [eax], esi` as `*_errno() = 0` on the one path a guard directly above had just proved the old ESI was zero — an inverted success/failure return. 97 wrong reads over 28 functions on t32 and 80 over 26 on w32; **only 2 of the 97 land on a self-assignment**, which is why `peek-a-bin-o7pj` never revealed the scale (`peek-a-bin-3axd`). Four things about the fix:
  - **The pairing rule has exactly one declaration, in `disasm/stackIdiom.ts`.** It was private to `functionDetect.ts`, where the *same* `push 7 / pop ecx` sizes a jump table (`peek-a-bin-mk42`) — so the two could have disagreed about the identical question. The module exists as its own leaf because `functionDetect.ts` imports `capstoneWindow`, and `decompile/lifter.ts` must not gain that edge: `pipeline.test.ts` is the end-to-end suite precisely because `decompileFunction` takes `Instruction[]` and needs neither Capstone nor a worker. Two drift guards in `__tests__/stackIdiom.test.ts` pin it — `stackIdiom.ts` imports nothing, and nothing under `decompile/` names `capstone` or `functionDetect` in a specifier.
  - **`pop esp`/`pop rsp` is refused explicitly**, even though `push 8 / pop esp` really does set it. ESP is the one register no stage here models, so a definition of it in the IR would be read by the frame analysis as a value it can move.
  - **Everything the rule refuses stays unlifted** — every save/restore pair, and (until `peek-a-bin-6ilz`, below) every `pop` whose `push` is in another block. That residue is `peek-a-bin-4ynk`, and its counts here are superseded: the real figures are `corpus/popReads.ts`'s, in the Verification section.
  - **Capstone prints a sign-extended `push imm8` as `push -2`, not `push 0xfffffffe`**, so `loneImmediate`'s decimal branch had to accept a leading minus or the idiom was refused on a spelling technicality. Safe for the jump-table caller by construction: a negative bound gives `maxCases = bound + 1 <= 0`, which `readAbsoluteTable` already refuses, and `jumpTables` is measured unmoved at 13/0/0/11.

  Measured at `890f2d7`: 33/288 t32 and 31/285 w32 functions changed, **x64 byte-identical** (an x86-only encoding), unrecovered token occurrences **unmoved** at 212/90/70/174 — this recovers a value rather than admitting a gap — self-assignments 34 → 31, loop shapes identical, and every gate green. `compare.mjs` flags 3 rows and all three are one benign thing: total guards *judged* rose (t32 831 → 837, w32 764 → 769) while a recovered constant let `cleanup.ts` fold two nested `if`s into one `&&`, which is semantically identical C that is no longer anchorable to a single compare.
- **…and the same idiom split across a branch is a PHI OF IMMEDIATES, which is why it cannot live in `liftBlock` at all.** `stackIdiom.ts`'s pairing is handed one block, so a `pop` whose `push` sits in a predecessor got no definition and every later read named the value from before the pop — `3axd` one block further out. The tempting rule, "every predecessor pushes the same immediate, so assign it at the `pop`", **describes not one site in this corpus**: all five are MSVC selecting a *different* constant per arm of an `if`/`else if` chain and popping it once, so the value is a phi and the definition has to land in **each predecessor** with `buildSSA` building the phi. `liftBlock` returns one block's statements, so this is `pipeline.ts` **step 2b** — `crossBlockPopImmediates` + `liftCrossBlockPops` in `lifter.ts`, appending through **`pushBeforeTerminator`**, which is mandatory rather than tidy: a predecessor already ends in an `IRBranch` and a plain `push` would land the definition after the guard that reads it. Five things (`peek-a-bin-6ilz`):
  - **Defining the register at the push is EARLY, and four refusals are what make it sound.** *Every* predecessor must pair or none do — defining it on some incoming edges leaves the phi's other operand naming the stale value, which reads as recovered while being wrong on one path, and a block the unwinder enters has no predecessor so the loop refuses it structurally. A pushing predecessor's only successor must be the `pop`'s block, since the definition reaches every successor of it. Its tail must not be a conditional jump, because `pushBeforeTerminator` places the definition *before* the `IRBranch`. And the `pop` must be its block's **first** instruction, which is what makes the interval provably empty and is why **no second register-liveness grammar was written** — `firstCalleeSavedWrites` stays the only machine-write model here. `pop esp`/`pop rsp` is refused for `liftBlock`'s reason. Depth is still `stackIdiom.ts`'s question, asked of the predecessor's tail as `pushedImmediate(pred.insns, pred.insns.length)`.
  - **The block-leader restriction costs exactly 0, measured rather than argued.** Of the non-leader pops whose prefix touches neither the stack nor the register — 53 on t32, 50 on w32, 0 on x64 — **0 would have paired**. The conditional-tail refusal likewise fires 0 times (it is implied by the successor test for any CFG that lists both edges) and is kept because it is the shape where the successor test alone could let an early read through.
  - **The statement carries the POP's address, not the push's**, for two reasons: `liftBlock`'s block-local form does the same, so one pair cannot be attributed to two instructions depending on where the push landed; and `corpus/popReads.ts` asks "did the lifter define this pop" by looking for an assignment at the pop's address, so that audit's `popsLifted` liveness check covers this rule with no second notion of "handled".
  - **Every replica of the lift loop had to gain the call**, and that is the one way this could regress silently: `popReads.ts`, `staleReads.ts` and `lostDefs.ts` each replicate `pipeline.ts` stages 1–3, and a replica missing step 2b keeps reporting rows the fix removed — a green tree reading as a red one. `stackIdiom.test.ts` fails if any file calling `liftBlock(` does not also call `liftCrossBlockPops(`.
  - **Measured at base `6d5ae92`, both sides pinned.** `corpus/popReads.ts` **7 → 4 wrong on t32 and 6 → 4 on w32** over 5 → 2 and 4 → 2 pops, `popsLifted` 67 → 73 and 63 → 68 (6 and 5 pops paired, of which only 5 had a read to fix); **x64 byte-identical** — an x86-only encoding, 0 sites — and only **6 of 288 and 5 of 285** functions changed. `unrecovered values` **unmoved on all four** (91/21/17/72) with token occurrences identical, because this recovers a value rather than admitting a gap; `if`/`while`/`for` identical (1987/1934/1717/1759, 192/184/176/178, 3/5/5/3); `compare.mjs` `CHANGED 0`, `only-base 0`, `only-change 0` on all four, verdict "no regression"; gcc 1127/1127, `offsetof` ratio 1.00 with fields and definitions unmoved, polarity 0 inverted, 0 statements dropped of 7227/7745/6881/6679, 0 callees lost, arity exact/under/over unmoved, `staleReads` 0/0, `staleGuards` 0 named, `lostDefs` 0, `armExits` 0, `unencodableNames` 0. Statements lifted +13 and +8, emitted line-map coverage +4 each.
  - **All 11 sites read by hand against `objdump -d -M intel`, and three are wrong-value defects rather than cosmetics.** t32 0x402530 (`w32` 0x402890) is `call _errno / push 0x16 / pop esi / mov [eax], esi` against `push 0x22 / jmp`, and the base emitted `*(int32_t*)(eax) = ecx_0` — the destination *pointer* written into `errno` on both paths, where the machine writes EINVAL or ERANGE; it now reads `esi = 0x16` / `esi = 0x22`. t32 0x401a79 (w32 0x401b76) is the command-line quote/space delimiter passed as `sub_401EB8(eax, ecx)` where the base's `ecx` was **never assigned anywhere in the function**. t32 0x40a462 (w32 0x40904d) is a BOM length (3 for UTF-8, 2 for UTF-16) that is a **loop bound**, and the base read a `-1` assigned in an unrelated earlier branch. The two the bead named (t32 0x4077f3, w32 0x407003) are MSVC's CR/LF pair and now store 0xD or 0xA. **The one cost is cosmetic**: in `t32!sub_4072B2` LICM hoists the two constant definitions into the loop preheader and leaves two dead `eax = eax_8N` copies there — provably dead, because the loop's first statement is `eax = arg_2`.
- **…and a MATCHED `push <reg>` / `pop <reg>` is lifted too, as a stack slot named after the push.** Not lifting a `pop` means it is not a definition in SSA, so every later read binds to the value the register held *before* it — and for a save/restore that is a value the emitted C has since overwritten. `t32!sub_40D99A` is MSVC's SSE memset: `push edx` at 0x40DA7C saves the remaining byte count, the block then reuses EDX as a scratch and a loop decrements it to zero, `pop edx` at 0x40DA97 restores the count and 0x40D9F2's `mov eax, edx` reads it — and the C read the scratch, which is 0 at loop exit. Worse, the same function's `push ecx` / … / `pop eax` / `ret` is memset **returning its destination pointer**, and the emitted `return eax` returned the loop counter. Two functions per x86 binary, and the whole residue of `corpus/popReads.ts` (`peek-a-bin-6f3v`, `peek-a-bin-4ynk`). `matchedStackSlots` (`lifter.ts`) is the pairing and `liftBlock` spells it as one assignment at each program point: `stk_<pushaddr> = <reg>` where the push is, `<reg> = stk_<pushaddr>` where the pop is. Eight things:
  - **The slot is identified by the PAIRING, never by an address**, which is what keeps CLAUDE.md's standing rule intact — no read of RSP is moved, and no RSP-relative expression is built at all. What is computed is *which push a pop takes its value from*, by depth, over the whole CFG; a save and its restore are routinely in different blocks with a loop nest between them, so this cannot be answered inside `liftBlock` and is a function-wide fact threaded in as an argument, the `firstCalleeSavedWrites` pattern.
  - **The slot is a pseudo-REGISTER, not an `IRVar`, and that is why it costs nothing where it recovers nothing.** `stk_x = ebx` is a copy, so `copyPropagation`'s `isCopyStmt` deletes it and rewrites the pop's readers to the pushed register's own SSA version; `ebx = ebx_0` is a copy too and DCE takes the pair. A save/restore the C already read correctly is therefore **byte-identical**. An `IRVar` survives both passes — DCE only deletes a versioned `reg` destination — and would have put a dead `slot_N = ebp` in front of every prologue in the image.
  - **Where the slot DOES reach the page, it is because SSA never saw the region.** Both t32 sites sit in code that follows a `ret` and that nothing in the function branches to — a merged-in `memset`/`memcpy` — so `renameVariables`, which walks the dominator tree from the entry, never reaches it: no versions, no copy propagation, no DCE. The slot is then the only thing that can name the saved value at all, and `stk_40da7c = edx - ebx` / `edx = stk_40da7c` is what the reader gets.
  - **The lattice needs a TOP element, and seeding an unreached block with the EMPTY stack instead is what a first attempt did — losing exactly the `ret` half this bead is about.** `sub_40D99A` carries a one-instruction alignment NOP at 0x40DA40 that nothing branches to and that falls into the middle of the memset body; a concrete depth of 0 there met against the real two-slot shape, took the whole region to BOTTOM and left `pop eax` unpaired. Every block therefore starts at TOP, the first `push` is what makes a shape concrete, and an unreachable predecessor that touches nothing is unconstraining. One that *does* touch the stack still degrades its successors.
  - **Nothing is seeded with a depth, and the base depth never matters.** A slot id is a push's own address, so a pairing survives the meet only where every path into the pop pushed that instruction's value and has not popped it — the claim is entirely relative to the push. A `pop` with nothing known on top is refused, which is the same answer as "the base is opaque". Stated limitation, and the mirror of the TOP rule: a block only an EXTERNAL transfer can reach contributes TOP, so if such a transfer really happens the pairing below it is not the machine's. That is the assumption every stage here makes about a detected function's boundaries.
  - **It refuses across a `call`, and that is not conservatism to be tuned away.** Whether the callee popped the arguments (stdcall — every Win32 import) or the caller will (cdecl, an `add esp, N` below) is not a fact about the instruction; guessing either way mis-pairs every `pop` after it. `add`/`sub`/`lea esp` by a whole number of slots IS modelled, and `lea esp, [esp]` — MSVC's multi-byte NOP — has to be, because it sits inside the loop the memset pairing must cross. A store *through* ESP refuses (it writes the very slots being tracked); a read through ESP does not. So do a push or pop narrower than a slot, `pop esp`, `leave`, `enter`, the flag/GPR block forms, and a join whose predecessors disagree about the depth or about which push is at a position.
  - **An immediate or memory push keeps its DEPTH and claims no VALUE.** The immediate forms are `stackIdiom.ts`'s and must stay there — the two rules are disjoint by construction, this one pairing only a register push — and a memory push would move a load across every intervening store, which is the one aliasing question this model does not answer. `regState` is deliberately **not** told about a slot pop either: `RegState` is what `collectArgs64` reads through `wroteAnyAlias`, so recording a `pop rcx` there would turn a stack restore into an invented argument at the next call, and the arity OVER-count is a gate at 0.
  - **Measured at base `bd73798`, both sides pinned.** `popReads` **4 → 0 wrong** and **2 → 0 `ret`-wrong** on both x86 binaries, `popsLifted` 73 → 151 and 68 → 146, and **5 pops paired on each x64 binary with the emitted C byte-identical** — which is the pseudo-register argument holding. 6 of 288 and 8 of 285 functions changed. `unrecovered values` **unmoved on all four** (91/21/17/72) with token occurrences identical; **loop shapes identical** (`for` 6/5/5/6, `while` 111/107/100/98, `do/while` 78/77/76/77); gcc 288/279/275/285 clean, polarity 0 inverted and 0 mismatch, 0 throws, 0 statements dropped, 0 callees lost, arity exact/under/over unmoved, `staleReads` 0/0, `staleGuards` 0 named with `shapes` unmoved, `lostDefs` 0, `armExits` 0, `wildBranches` 0, `unencodableNames` 0. Three costs, all read by hand: `offsetof` w32 **418 → 421 fields / 58 → 59 definitions at ratio 1.00** (a new 3-byte shape from the case bodies that now read the restored pointers, every layout verified by the compiled-and-run program); `if` emitted **1987 → 1985** and 1759 → 1757 with **one guard per binary leaving the audited set**, which is `cleanup.ts` folding a now-complete chain into one `&&` — text unchanged, verdict `OK`, the documented `peek-a-bin-3axd` behaviour; and **one dead statement lost per x86 binary**, `t32!sub_40E1D8`'s `edi = … & 0x80` above an unrecovered `jne`, whose value the machine really does discard at the `pop` but which was a hint about what the guard tested (`peek-a-bin-thsj` would recover the guard itself, since `pop` writes no flags).
- **A trivial phi's operand is the register's IDENTITY, not a spelling, and `simplifyPhis` is the one place that can put it on the page.** `insertPhis` mints every phi as `irReg(canonReg(...))` and the operand fill copies that name and width verbatim, so a phi operand is `rsi` at eight bytes even in 32-bit code (`peek-a-bin-1k4`, `peek-a-bin-pzws`, `peek-a-bin-0s6e`). `destroySSA` corrects that for a *lowered* phi copy, through `registerSpeller`; `simplifyPhis` substitutes the operand into every reader of the phi's destination, where it becomes an ordinary read with no lowering step left to correct it. It now spells the operand the way the value's own mentions spell it — `registerSpeller`'s `perVersion` rule, one pass earlier, with the width following the name; a version with no mention at all keeps the operand as it was. Two things worth knowing:
  - **It was latent and `peek-a-bin-6f3v` exposed it.** Restoring EDI/ESI at `t32!sub_40B780` 0x40B7BB made an RSI phi trivial in one function per 32-bit binary, and `unencodableNames` went 0 → **34 mentions / 3 distinct over 2 of 573 PE32 functions** — 9 reads of `rsi_1`, 9 of `rdi_1` and 2 of a bare `rdi`, in images that have no such register. The gate is the only oracle here that can see a wrong register *name*: `gcc` declares `rsi` and `esi` as two unrelated `long`s and `corpus/staleReads.ts` compares the name a read uses, deliberately.
  - **Output-neutral on its own, measured as its own stage.** With the slot pairing disabled, the emitted C is **byte-identical on all 1127 functions of all four binaries** (`compare.mjs` `CHANGED 0`, verdict "no regression"). Negative-controlled the other way by a unit test in `ssa.test.ts` that builds the trivial phi by hand — which of a trivial phi's operands is `nonSelf[0]` depends on predecessor order, and in the corpus one operand carries the canonical name while the other has been rewritten to a real spelling by copy propagation, so pinning the bad one as the survivor is what makes the assertion mean something in both orders.
- **`foldBlock` counts uses inside ONE block, so a definition that escapes the block is not single-use.** Inlining moves the right-hand side into its one reader and **deletes the assignment**, which is correct only while that reader is the last thing that wants the value. It is handed a single block's statement list, so a definition read once locally and again two blocks later looked single-use, and every read in the successors was left naming a register the emitted C never assigns — `t32!sub_40D99A`'s `mov ecx, [ebp+8]` had one in-block reader and eleven reads below it, so the whole function operated on an `ecx` nothing produced, with a `ecx = ecx;` from an unrelated `lea ecx,[ecx]` NOP as the only mention of it on the left of an `=`. `blockLiveOut` (`fold.ts`) answers the missing question — ordinary backward may-liveness over the CFG, computed once on the unfolded program in `pipeline.ts` step 4 — and `foldBlock` refuses any candidate in its block's live-out set. Four things are load-bearing:
  - **The refusal is `killedInBlock`-guarded, and that is not an optimisation.** A later definition of the same register in the same block ends this value's live range, so the live-out set describes the *other* definition; refusing there would stop folds that are sound and churn the emitted C for no recovered value. The scan already breaks at such a definition — the flag is only recording that it did.
  - **`raw` reads nothing.** An unlifted instruction reaches the page as a comment, so the register names inside it are not reads in the emitted C, and counting them would hold values alive for a statement that cannot use them. A `branch` very much is a read, because `pipeline.ts` extracts the conditions *after* this stage and they reach the page.
